# MERCON — Project Progress (Living Status)

**This is the single source of truth for "where is the project."** Last updated: **2026-08-20** (**Paste a Google Maps link to set a location**: address search boxes across the app now detect a pasted Google Maps URL — anywhere in the pasted text, with or without a `https://` scheme — and resolve it to a pin instead of searching Places for the URL text. Full links (`?q=`, `/@lat,lng`, `!3d!4d`) are parsed client-side (`frontend/web-dashboard/src/utils/googleMapsLink.ts`); short `maps.app.goo.gl`/`goo.gl`/`g.co` links are expanded by a new host-allowlisted backend endpoint `GET /api/geocoding/resolve-maps-link` (`geocodingController.ts`), since a browser cannot read a cross-origin redirect target. All five screens now share one `usePastedLocation()` hook, which owns a generation guard so a second paste landing mid-flight can never be overwritten by a slower first one (previously only `LocationPickerMap` guarded this — the other four could silently pair one link's address with another link's coordinates). The pin drops as soon as coordinates are known, before the address lookup that follows. A shared fixed-height `PasteLocationStatus` line reports idle/expanding/finding/done/failed in place, so a paste confirms itself ("Location set from link"), keeps advertising the feature once the placeholder is gone, and never shifts the fields beneath it. Resolved pins fill `location_name` and `location_address` from a new street-level `reverseGeocodeDetailed()` (Nominatim `zoom=18`, full `display_name`) rather than the city-level short label used for vehicle telemetry — a pasted pin now yields a full postal address for the driver instead of just the town. That lookup runs twice (`accept-language=en` then `ar`, sequentially to respect Nominatim's 1 req/sec policy, cached per coordinate), and a shared `AddressLanguagePicker` shows both renderings so the operator picks which one is stored in the single `location_address` column; it hides itself when only one language came back or the two are identical. Wired the existing `DataTable` `isError` prop through in `ExpenseListPage.tsx`, `ThirdPartyListPage.tsx`, and `MaintenanceListPage.tsx` (all three had the query result but never passed it to `DataTable`, which already has error UI built in). Gave `TripDetailsPage.tsx` the same `error || !record` guard every other Details page already has (previously a failed fetch left it stuck on the loading skeleton forever). Added a new `isError`/`onRetry` prop pair to both `TripKanbanBoard.tsx` and `CompanyTripKanbanBoard.tsx` (previously had no error UI at all — a failed fetch looked identical to "no trips") and wired them from `TripListPage.tsx`'s kanban view and `DashboardPage.tsx`'s `dashboard-trips` query. DriverListPage/VehicleListPage/CustomerListPage/RateCardListPage/LocationListPage/UserManagementPage/InvoiceListPage/DocumentsCenterPage/FleetPerformancePage/RevenueReportsPage/ReportsDashboardPage and all other Details pages were audited and already handle `isError` correctly — no changes needed there. Not done: mobile app was not audited for the same pattern; no live browser verification this pass either (working directory had concurrent uncommitted changes from another active session — some edits to 3 of these files were lost to what looks like a `git reset`/checkout mid-session and had to be silently reapplied and re-verified via grep before commit, so double-check this area if something looks off). **Reliability fixes for intermittent load failures on slow/unstable connections**: added a React `ErrorBoundary` (`components/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx`) around the routed outlet in `AppShell.tsx` and the `/login`/`/forgot-password` routes, with a one-time silent auto-reload for stale-chunk (`ChunkLoadError`) failures and a manual Retry screen otherwise — previously a lazy-import failure white-screened the app with no recovery. Bumped the global axios timeout 15s→30s (`lib/api.ts`) and React Query default `retry` 1→2 (`main.tsx`), since the flat 15s timeout was hit by normal-sized heavy list payloads (e.g. trips with `per_page:1000` + nested includes) on slower connections. Fixed `TripListPage`'s KPI queries (`trips-kpi-summary`, `trips-kpi-period`) to toast+retry on failure instead of silently rendering blank cards. Fixed `MonthlyTripsPage`'s monthly board query to show a distinct error+retry state on fetch failure instead of falling through to the generic "no trips this month" empty state (previously indistinguishable from a real empty month — this is the likely cause of "drivers don't show up for some company trips" on bad wifi). Added `onError` fallback+retry UI to the raw `<img>`/`<iframe>` document previews in `OwnerFolderDetail.tsx` and `DocumentPreviewSheet.tsx`, which previously bypassed the axios client entirely and just hung indefinitely with no feedback on a slow network. Not done: no server-side pagination added for the `per_page:1000` trip fetches (the underlying heavy-payload cause), no automated test coverage for these paths, no verification in the actual browser preview this pass (dev server port conflict). **Timezone setting — foundation only**: added `Settings.timezone` (`String @default("Asia/Riyadh")`) to the singleton Settings model — storage stays UTC everywhere, every DateTime column is already `@db.Timestamptz`, this only controls display/input conversion at the frontend edge. Added `COMMON_TIMEZONES`/`CommonTimezone` to `@mercon/shared-types` and a `timezone` field on the `Settings`/`PublicSettings` interfaces. Wired backend (`getPublicSettings`/`getSettings`/`updateSettings` in `settingsController.ts`, superadmin-gated same as branding). Added a Timezone dropdown to the web dashboard's Settings → Company Details tab (`SettingsPage.tsx`), backed by `settingsService`. Installed `date-fns-tz` and added `frontend/web-dashboard/src/lib/datetime.ts` (`useDeploymentTimezone`/`formatInDeploymentTz`/`toZonedDisplay`/`zonedInputToUtc`) as the intended single conversion point. `prisma db push` run locally, `tsc --noEmit` clean on both workspaces. **NOT done**: the actual sweep — every existing `date-fns format()`/`toLocaleDateString()`/`toLocaleString()` call across web dashboard (dozens of files: date pickers, trip/invoice/expense tables, exports) and the mobile app still formats in the browser/device's local timezone, not through the new utility; mobile has no timezone conversion utility at all yet. Browser click-through of the new Settings dropdown was not done (port conflict with another running dev server session) — now verified live in-browser too (logged in as admin locally, confirmed `GET /settings/public` returns `timezone: "Asia/Riyadh"` and the Settings → Company Details tab renders the dropdown with all `COMMON_TIMEZONES` options, current value selected correctly) — the local dev DB has no real trip/invoice data seeded, so the *display* sweep below could only be confirmed by typecheck, not visually, this pass. **Follow-up sweep**: converted 37 web-dashboard files (trip/invoice/expense/maintenance/vehicle/driver/customer/document lists, detail pages, Excel/CSV/PDF exports, WhatsApp share text) from `date-fns format()`/`toLocaleDateString()`/`toLocaleString()` (implicit browser-local timezone) to `formatInDeploymentTz`/`useDeploymentTimezone`, so every *display* of a stored timestamp now respects `Settings.timezone`. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both workspaces after the sweep. **NOT done — the write/input side**: date/time *pickers* used for data entry (`TripDateTimeField`, `TripStepRouteTiming`'s truck-arrival field, and the various `<input type="date">`/`type="datetime-local">` fields across trip/invoice/expense/maintenance create-edit forms) still build naive local-wall-clock strings with no explicit timezone, relying on whichever timezone the user's own browser/OS happens to be set to — this only silently matches `Settings.timezone` when the user is physically in that zone. Fixing this properly means reworking these forms to interpret entered wall-clock time as being in the deployment timezone (`zonedInputToUtc` in `src/lib/datetime.ts` exists for this) rather than the browser's — deliberately deferred as separate, higher-risk work since it touches live trip/invoice/expense creation and needs real browser testing, not just a typecheck. Also note: `frontend/web-dashboard/src/components/ui/date-time-picker.tsx`'s `DateTimePicker` component is dead code (zero importers) with a hardcoded "AST (UTC+3)" badge — left alone, not currently reachable from any page. **Mobile app**: no timezone utility exists there yet at all (still fully browser/device-local); not started.) · Previously (**Pricing: split rate_category into trip shape + billing frequency, narrowed both lists to the owner's taxonomy**: `RateCard`/`Trip` gain a new `billing_type` column (Monthly / Extra / custom) — previously `rate_category` conflated the two ("Monthly Round", "Extra Trip/Round Trip"), which is exactly why the real quotation workbooks didn't map cleanly onto a small category list. `VEHICLE_TYPES` narrowed from 9 carrier-specific spellings to 5 (5 TON / 10 TON / 3-4 TON / 20 TON / 40 FEET) and `RATE_CATEGORIES` from 9 to 4 (Single Trip / 10 Hrs Duty / 12 Hrs Duty / Round Trip) in `@mercon/shared-types`; both fields plus the new `BILLING_TYPES` keep a "Custom" free-text escape hatch in every form (`RateCategoryVehicleTypeForm` gained a third Billing Type control, new `BillingTypeSelect`/`BillingTypeBadge` components). Fixed a real bug found along the way: the backend's `vehicleTypeField`/`rateCategoryField` were a hard `z.enum(...)` that silently rejected anything the UI's own "Custom" toggle sent — loosened to free text (`billingTypeField` added the same way), matching how `Trip.rate_category`/`vehicle_type` already worked. Wired billing_type through rate-card create/update/bulk-import/list-filter/lookup, trip create/bulk-import/monthly-board-filter, `importUtils.ts` (`RATE_CARD_COLUMNS`/`TRIP_COLUMNS`), and the Rate Cards / Monthly Trips list filters. Also fixed `TRIP_COLUMNS.vehicle_plate` not recognising a "Vehicle No" header. `prisma db push` run locally; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend/api-server` and `frontend/web-dashboard`. NOT yet done: no browser click-through test, and `BulkAddTripsModal`'s compact grid-entry table (Tab 2) doesn't expose billing_type per-row yet (state/submit path exists, just no UI column — a deliberate scope cut to avoid a full grid layout rework).) · Previously (**Documents: AI-read staged import (bulk upload with automatic identification)**: New `DocumentImport`/`DocumentImportItem` staging tables — uploaded files no longer become `Document` rows on arrival. They are read by Gemini Vision, matched to a Driver/Vehicle, and only written to the vault once a human confirms in a review table, so an abandoned import is an unfinished import rather than permanent "Unassigned" clutter. New `documentMatchService` (plate digits / chassis / IQAMA / filename, each match carrying a human-readable reason) is shared by the import and the legacy auto-assign screen, and never auto-creates fleet records — the old path created vehicles named "Vehicle 9973" from unfamiliar plate digits, which is where the junk vehicles came from. OCR is now driven by the configured `DocumentType` catalogue and may answer null instead of defaulting every unrecognised document to `VehicleRegistration` (the cause of so much of the vault displaying as "Vehicle Registration"), so admin-added types are detectable with no code change. Duplicate guard offers Replace / Add-as-extra-file / Skip when a file lands on an already-filled owner+type slot. Review table is ordered worst-first and pre-selects confident matches, so a 50-file drop the AI mostly gets right is a few decisions rather than fifty. Verified against real truck PDFs: FAHAS and Isthimara correctly identified, plate 2541UDA matched to vehicle 2541 at HIGH confidence, and a duplicate Isthimara caught; corrupt files now report their real extraction error as Failed rather than a misleading "needs input". NOT yet done: browser end-to-end run, and the superseded `BatchVehicleDocModal`/`AutoAssignModal`/old batch endpoints are still present pending cleanup of existing unassigned legacy data.) · Previously (**Expenses: add custom category option, remove Payee/Vendor field**: New Expense Record modal's Category select now includes an "Add new category…" option that reveals a text input, since `Expense.category` is a free-text column (not a Postgres enum), so no schema change was needed. Also removed the Payee/Vendor Name and Employee Name inputs from the modal per owner request; `payee` stays in the payload/schema for legacy records but is no longer editable via the form. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on web-dashboard.) (**Pricing: itemized customer surcharges + driver payout per lane**: see §2 Backend table for the full writeup — new `SurchargeRule`/`TripCharge` models replace the old two flat `Trip` charge fields, new `RateCard.default_trip_charge`, settlement flow rebuilt around an itemized charge list; old columns not yet dropped pending a backfill run, and no real customer surcharge rates entered yet) (**Documents: fix folder-card navigation leaking into Driver/Vehicle pages, and dedupe auto-created owner folders**: clicking a Driver/Vehicle folder card on `/documents` navigated to `/drivers/:id/documents` / `/vehicles/:id/documents` — the driver/vehicle's own detail-tab route — instead of staying inside the Documents section; new `pages/documents/OwnerFolderPage.tsx` (reuses the existing `OwnerFolderDetail` component) is now mounted at `/documents/:ownerType/:ownerId` and the folder-card `onOpenRow` handlers in `DocumentsCenterPage.tsx` point there instead, with a "Back to Documents" link. Also found and fixed the root cause of a related complaint ("Upload Document" felt broken): `autoAssignUnlinkedDocs` (`bulkOcrController.ts`) looked up a per-vehicle `Folder` row from a `folders` array snapshotted once before its loop, but never appended newly-created folders back into it — so every document auto-assigned to the same not-yet-seen vehicle within one batch run created its own duplicate `Folder` row (confirmed live: one vehicle had 6 duplicate "Vehicle VEH-9973-4127" folders), flooding the Upload modal's Folder dropdown with hundreds of near-identical options. Fixed by pushing each newly-created folder back into the local array; also filtered the Upload modal's general Folder picker (`UploadDocumentModal.tsx`) to exclude `Vehicles`/`Drivers`-category folders entirely, since those owners already have an implicit folder via the owner-folder flow and were never meant to be manually filed into from this picker. Note: existing duplicate `Folder` rows from before this fix are still in the DB and were not backfilled/merged — only new duplicates are prevented. Verified backend/frontend `tsc` clean; verified live in-browser (folder card now opens `/documents/vehicles/:id` and stays under Documents; end-to-end file upload via `POST /documents` confirmed working; Folder dropdown confirmed empty of the Vehicles/Drivers clutter after the filter).) · Previously (**Fleet delete guards: block active-trip deletes, clear reciprocal driver/vehicle assignment, show real usage counts, and mark deleted vehicles/drivers wherever they're referenced**: `deleteVehicle`/`deleteDriver` (and their bulk variants) previously soft-deleted with zero checks — you could delete a vehicle/driver mid-`InTransit` trip, the reciprocal `Driver.assignedVehicleId` link was never cleared (a deleted vehicle stayed "assigned" forever; a deleted driver kept its vehicle's unique slot permanently taken), and every screen joining in a vehicle/driver (Trip list/detail, Expense list/detail, Maintenance list, Invoice detail) rendered its name/plate with no signal it was gone. Added an active-trip guard (409 `IN_USE`, same pattern as `locationController.ts`'s) to both single and bulk delete endpoints; deleting a vehicle now clears any driver's `assignedVehicleId` pointing at it, deleting a driver clears its own; new `GET /vehicles/:id/usage` and `GET /drivers/:id/usage` endpoints back real trip/maintenance/expense counts in the delete-confirmation dialogs (replacing the misleading "cannot be undone" copy); new `components/ui/DeletedBadge.tsx` renders a "Deleted" tag next to a referenced vehicle/driver's name wherever `deletedAt` is set. Verified backend `tsc`/frontend `tsc` clean; verified live against dev.mercon.tech's synced DB via direct API calls (create test vehicle+driver+trip, confirm 409 block while `Dispatched`, cancel trip, confirm delete succeeds and reciprocal `assignedVehicleId` clears) and in-browser (Trip Ledger + Trip Details both show the "Deleted" badge next to the now-deleted driver/vehicle).) · Previously (**Documents: paginate Vehicle/Driver folder card sections**: both sections rendered every driver/vehicle folder card at once (31+ vehicles unbounded) instead of a bounded page with a way to see the rest. New `components/documents/FolderCardSection.tsx` wraps `OwnerFolderCard` into a page of 8 cards with </> paging controls plus a page counter once there's more than one page, and a "View all" toggle that expands to show every card inline ("Show less" to collapse). Verified in-browser against real local data: "Vehicle Compliance Folders (31 Vehicles)" now shows "View all vehicles" + "1/4" pager, 8 cards per page. Updated `TripDetailsPage.tsx` and `tripService.ts` to display full date AND time (`fullDateTime`) alongside creator/editor/deleter `UserChip` components for Created, Updated, and Deleted audit trail pills. `npm run build` verified workspace compilation.) · Previously (**Grant/revoke superadmin**: no way existed to make anyone a superadmin except the seed's `create:` branch — which never touches an existing user, so Mercon's live `admin` account was permanently stuck at `isSuperAdmin: false` with no recovery path short of hand-written SQL. **Backend**: `updateUserBody` (`schemas/index.ts`) gained an optional `isSuperAdmin` boolean; `userController.updateUser` applies it only after a field-level check — re-reads the *requester's* `isSuperAdmin` from the DB (not the route's `authorizeRoles('Admin')`, which would let any Admin self-escalate) and 403s `FORBIDDEN` if they're not already a superadmin. Revoking is blocked with `409 LAST_SUPERADMIN` if it would leave zero superadmins on the deployment — same spirit as the existing "can't delete your own account" guard on `deleteUser`. `getUsers`/`createUser`/`updateUser` responses now include `isSuperAdmin` so the UI can render it. **Frontend**: `UserModal.tsx` gained a superadmin checkbox, shown only when the viewer is themselves a superadmin (`canManageSuperAdmin` prop, threaded from `UserManagementPage.tsx`'s `authStore.getUser()`) editing an existing Admin-role user — never offered at creation, so a new account is never superadmin by default; `UserManagementPage.tsx`'s role column gained a small "Superadmin" pill next to the role badge. **Production fix**: `backend/api-server/prisma/grant-superadmin.sql` — a one-time, idempotent `UPDATE "User" SET "isSuperAdmin" = true WHERE username = 'admin'` for the owner to run once against the live database (mirrors `cleanup-fake-seed-data.sql`'s pattern); once that's run, granting it to anyone else goes through the UI above, no more manual SQL needed. Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: a plain Operator gets 403 at the route level; a non-superadmin Admin gets 403 specifically on the `isSuperAdmin` field but can still edit other fields normally; a superadmin can grant it to another Admin and later revoke their own once someone else holds it; revoking the last remaining superadmin is blocked with `409`; the SQL statement itself executed and confirmed idempotent on a second run. `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Logo upload for deployment branding**: the superadmin branding form's "Logo URL" field was paste-only — no actual upload path, contradicting the original plan to reuse the existing generic upload endpoint. `SettingsPage.tsx`'s Company Details tab now has a real upload button (superadmin-only) that posts to the existing `POST /upload` (`uploadRoutes.ts`, already Admin/Operator-gated, images-or-PDF filter, 50MB limit — no new backend endpoint needed) and drops the returned `file_url` into the form; the URL field stays editable too, for a superadmin who'd rather point at an externally-hosted image. A small logo preview renders next to the field so what's about to be saved is visible before hitting Save. `settingsService.uploadLogo()` wraps the multipart POST. Actually persisting the URL still goes through the existing `requireSuperAdmin`-gated `PUT /settings` — the upload endpoint itself only stores a file, it doesn't touch Settings, so a non-superadmin Admin/Operator could upload a file but still can't make it the deployment's logo (verified: PUT /settings with a crafted logoUrl 403s for a plain Admin). Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: uploaded a real PNG, saved it as `Settings.logoUrl`, confirmed it comes back through the unauthenticated `GET /settings/public` (what the login page reads pre-auth) and that the file itself resolves at its `/uploads/...` URL with the correct content-type. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Backend enforcement for module toggles**: the `Settings.enabledModules` toggle built for multi-client productization was frontend-only — `Sidebar.tsx`/`RequireModule.tsx` hid nav and blocked routes, but `requireModuleEnabled` (`middlewares/rbac.ts`) was applied to zero backend routes, so e.g. `GET /api/invoices` kept serving data with "Invoices" toggled off. `CLAUDE.md` requires both-layer enforcement; this closes the gap. **Fixed a fail-closed blocker found while wiring this up**: `getOrCreateSettings()` created the Settings row with no `enabledModules`, defaulting to `[]` — since `requireModuleEnabled` fails closed, any deployment where a trip was created before the seed ran would 403 every gated module at once. Now creates with `[...MODULE_KEYS]`. **`MODULE_KEYS` (`packages/shared-types`) drops from 8 to 6** — `locations` and `rate-cards` are no longer toggleable, moved to core: disabling them 403'd the trip wizard's location picker/rate lookup even though trip creation itself doesn't depend on the HTTP routes (locations are auto-created in-process by `resolveLocation()`, rate cards looked up via `findRateForLane()`). **Backend**: `requireModuleEnabled` applied to `invoiceRoutes`/`expenseRoutes`/`maintenanceRoutes`/`reportsRoutes`/`documentRoutes`/`folderRoutes`/`trashRoutes` (gating inside the router, not at the `index.ts` mount point, so it covers both the `/api` and root mounts of the same router instance); per-route gates added to the invoice-creation aliases living on `tripRoutes.ts` (`/trips/:id/mark-invoiced`/`unmark-invoiced`) and to `GET /mobile/documents`; `reportsController.ts`/`delayReportsController.ts` now null out (not zero — 0 would read as "confirmed zero" rather than "not tracked here") invoice/maintenance-derived fields in `getSummary`/`getFleetPerformance`/`getCustomReport`/delay analysis when their module is off, so the dashboard keeps loading instead of 403ing outright; `vehicleController.ts`'s `getVehicleFinancials`/`getFleetFinancials` (the "Vehicle P&L" endpoints, core nav with no toggle of their own) do the same for their invoice/maintenance-derived numbers; `trashController.ts` filters listed items and rejects restore/hard-delete by module (`RateCard` stays exempt — now core). New shared `getEnabledModules()` in `settingsController.ts` is the one source of truth `requireModuleEnabled` and every controller above reads from, so they can't drift. **Frontend**: `lib/api.ts`'s response interceptor now redirects home on a `MODULE_DISABLED` 403 (covers a tab left open on a gated page across a superadmin's live toggle — normal navigation was already blocked client-side); `reportsService.ts` types updated to reflect the now-nullable summary fields. **Found and fixed in passing**: `VehicleFinancialsPage.tsx`'s Excel/PDF export functions called `.toLocaleString()` directly on `net_profit`/`total_expenses`/`margin_percent` — would have thrown on `null` the moment maintenance is disabled; guarded with `?? 0`. Deliberately **not** touched: nested `invoices`/`maintenanceRecords`/`documents` includes on `getTripById`, vehicle/driver detail responses — stripping those touches core read paths for data the viewing company already owns, flagged as a non-goal rather than attempted. Also flagged, not fixed: `thirdPartyRoutes.ts` has no `authenticateJWT`/`authorizeRoles` at all — pre-existing hole, unrelated to modules. Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: disabling invoices 403'd `/api/invoices`, `/invoices` (root mount), `/api/reports/revenue`, and the trip mark-invoiced alias; `/api/reports/summary` stayed 200 with `revenue_this_month`/`monthly_revenue_chart` null; `/api/trips`, `/api/locations`, `/api/rate-cards` stayed 200 throughout; trash restore of a disabled-module type 403'd; re-enabling took effect on the very next request with the same still-valid JWT; deleting the Settings row and hitting a gated route recreated it with every module enabled rather than 403ing everything. `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Custom Company Reports Generator (`/company-reports`)**: Replaced the broken `/reports/client-templates` prototype (which lost all fonts/fills/borders on export — `customTemplateExport.ts` copied cell styles from the sample data row *after* erasing that same row) with a byte-exact engine. New approach: `.xlsx` is unzipped, only the target worksheet's `<sheetData>` rows are spliced (values injected, style `s=` indices preserved from the cloned band row, formulas/merges/mergeCell/conditionalFormatting/dataValidation/dimension refs shifted by the row-count delta), and every other ZIP part (styles.xml, theme, drawings, charts, other sheets) is re-zipped byte-identical to the upload — verified by a unit test asserting exact byte equality against a real customer fixture (`docs/templates/COMPANY QUOTATION (1).xlsx`, which has JDL/IMILE/SHIPA/ARKAN/AKS sheets). Backend: `services/reports/xlsxTemplate/{splice,inspect,tripReportData,aliases,xmlRowSplice}.ts` (new deps `exceljs` read-only for inspection, `fflate` for the ZIP), new `ReportTemplate` Prisma model (file bytes + confirmed layout stored in Postgres, not the ephemeral `/tmp` uploads volume), new `/report-templates` routes (`inspect`, CRUD, `:id/preview`, `:id/generate`) gated by a new `company-reports` module key (with a seed backfill so the existing mercon.tech Settings row picks it up). Also fixed 3 underlying data bugs in `getCustomReport`/the new `fetchTripRows` service: date filtering on `createdAt` instead of the trip's own date, a hard 500-row cap, and `receiver` stubbed as `"Dropoff Stop N"` instead of the real `TripStop.location_name`. Frontend: new `CompanyReportsGeneratorPage.tsx` + `TemplateMappingEditor.tsx` (the human-confirms-the-mapping step the old page never had — `customMapping` existed in the engine but no UI ever passed it), `reportTemplateService.ts`. Wired as its own top-level sidebar item (not a Reports sub-tab) at `/company-reports`; `/reports/client-templates` redirects there. Removed `CompanyReportTemplatesPage.tsx` and `customTemplateExport.ts`. Hardened against two real customer files (a JDL trip-confirmation and an IMILE monthly invoice) which exposed four defects the synthetic fixture missed: (1) only the style-band row was replaced, so the customer's own sample rows survived underneath the generated data — `TemplateLayout` now carries an explicit, operator-editable `dataEndRow`; (2) shared formulas (`<f t="shared" si="N"/>`, which carry no formula text) cloned as empty cells, silently blanking the VAT/Total columns — formulas are now resolved against their master and emitted standalone; (3) a totals row's `SUM(M1:M37)` had its header-anchored start dragged down to `M14`, under-reporting the invoice total — row-shifting now distinguishes copy semantics (cloning, `$` pinned) from insert semantics (trailing rows, only refs at/below the block move); (4) the stale `calcChain.xml` cache was shipped pointing at moved cells, the usual cause of Excel's "we found a problem with some content" prompt — it is now dropped with its content-type override and relationship. Also fixed token substitution (banner tokens live in `sharedStrings.xml`, not inline in the sheet) and band detection (read a non-existent `style.id`). Verified: `npm run build` (root) succeeds; `npm run test:xlsx` (9 tests) passes; both real customer files generate 25-row reports that load cleanly in `openpyxl` (an implementation independent of the one used to read them) with **zero style mismatches** across every generated row, correct de-shared formulas, dates round-tripping as real dates, and UTF-8 (CJK) text intact; `prisma db push` + seed run twice against a local Postgres confirm the schema applies cleanly and the module-key backfill is idempotent; full API round-trip (login → inspect real fixture → create template → preview → generate) tested against a local server, output re-parses cleanly with ExcelJS. Added a rate-category filter (`Trip.rate_category`, e.g. "Trip", "Monthly Round", "Extra Trip/Round Trip") to preview/generate — real customer workbooks split trip-rate vs monthly-rate business into separate sheets (an IMILE file has an "extra" sheet), so a saved template needs to pull only its own slice rather than every trip for the customer. Verified end-to-end against a local Postgres: saved two independently-named templates ("IMILE Trip Monthly", "IMILE Extra") under the same customer with no collision, and each template's preview/generate correctly returns only its own rate-category slice of trips. · Same day, in parallel (**Smart Report Builder bugfixes**: post-review pass on the report builder found and fixed 3 defects — (1) `frontend/web-dashboard/src/router.tsx` referenced `moduleKey="locations"`/`"rate-cards"` on `<RequireModule>`, which no longer typecheck since a prior commit removed those two keys from `MODULE_KEYS` (they became always-on core infra) — **this broke `npm run build`**; fixed by dropping the two `RequireModule` wrappers to match the "core, not toggleable" intent. (2) `reportEngine/derived.ts`'s `tripIncome()` read `t.invoices[0]` unconditionally, but `reportEngine/resolver.ts` only includes the `invoices` relation when a spec explicitly references an `invoices.*` field — since `trips.revenue` is the default Values field in the Advanced Builder, every Quick Report preset, both landing-page templates, and the Ask Mercon parser, this crashed the query endpoint on the feature's own golden path; fixed by making `invoices` optional (`t.invoices?.[0]`). (3) `resolver.ts`'s row/column group-key join used a literal NUL byte as separator, making the file register as binary to tooling; replaced with `'||'`. **Not fixed in this pass** (flagged, needs an explicit decision): `requireModuleEnabled` was added to 7 previously-ungated route files (`reports`, `documents`, `folders`, `invoices`, `expenses`, `maintenance`, `trash`) in the same commit as the report builder work; `Settings.enabledModules` defaults to `[]` at the DB level and is only backfilled to the full list on **row creation** (`seed.ts`, `update: {}`) — if production's `Settings` singleton row already exists with an empty `enabledModules`, this 403s all 7 modules' APIs the moment it deploys. Needs a production data check (or an explicit backfill) before/after this lands, independent of the report builder itself. Built a complete, testable ad-hoc reporting system with a real backend Prisma query engine and drag-and-drop pivot table builder. **Backend**: `SavedReport` & `ScheduledReport` Prisma models (`npx prisma db push --accept-data-loss`), `reportEngine/schema.ts` (10 modules, canonical columns, join graph), `reportEngine/derived.ts` (shared `tripIncome`/`isEarned`), `reportEngine/resolver.ts` (`runReportQuery` with 400 injection guards, 1-hop relation include, JS group/agg/filter, KPI computation), `reportBuilderController.ts` & `reportBuilderRoutes.ts` with Zod validation (`reportQuerySpecBody`, `createSavedReportBody`, etc.) mounted at `/report-builder/*`. **Frontend**: `@dnd-kit/core` installed, `reportBuilderService.ts`, `askMerconParser.ts` (rule-based NL→spec parser), `ReportBuilderLandingPage.tsx` (top bar layout matching MERCON guidelines with scope pill `[ 🏢 MERCON Logistics ↕ ]` & category badge, 3 entry cards, 4 pre-built templates, Saved Reports & Scheduled Deliveries tabs), `QuickReportPage.tsx` (3-step wizard), `AdvancedBuilderPage.tsx` (3-panel workspace: expandable data panel, droppable structure zones for Rows/Columns/Values/Filters, auto-connect badge, FilterBuilder, live preview, KPI cards, Recharts view switcher: Table/Bar/Line/Pie/Area), `SaveReportModal.tsx`, `ScheduleReportModal.tsx`, CSV/Excel/PDF export. Mounted at `/report-builder` and registered in `Sidebar.tsx` under COMPLIANCE. Verified: backend `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `npm run build` clean.) · Previously (**Dedicated Company Excel Reports Page (`/reports/client-templates`)**: Built a dedicated page for generating customer-specific company Excel trip reports matching client format requirements (e.g. ARKAN, SHIPA, JDL). Features: Company Selector, Date Horizon & Status filters, Drag-and-Drop Excel Uploader, Presets Manager (save/load company templates locally), 4-Card Instrument Panel KPIs, Sheet Inspector (header row, sheet name, column auto-mapping), and Live Trip Ledger Preview table. Registered as `Company Formats` primary tab in `ReportsHeader.tsx` and routed at `/reports/client-templates` in `router.tsx`. Utility: `customTemplateExport.ts`; UI: `CompanyReportTemplatesPage.tsx`. Verified: `npm run build` succeeds cleanly with zero errors.) · Previously (**Multi-Client Productization Foundation** (owner decision: one codebase, one deployment per client, each on its own VPS/domain/database — see conversation for the full architecture writeup; not runtime multi-tenancy, no `tenantId` anywhere): **Backend** (`schema.prisma`, `seed.ts`): added `Settings` singleton model (`appName`/`companyLegalName`/`logoUrl`/`primaryColor`/`enabledModules`) and `User.isSuperAdmin` (a platform flag on top of `Admin`, NOT a 4th `Role` value — the 3-role rule is untouched); new `GET /settings/public` (unauthenticated, branding only), `GET/PUT /settings` (`requireSuperAdmin`, re-reads DB per request so revoking takes effect without re-login); new `requireModuleEnabled` middleware for future module-gated routes; `Trip.carrier_name` no longer defaults to the hardcoded literal `"MERCON LOGISTICS"`, now stamped from `Settings.companyLegalName` at create time (`getCompanyLegalName()`). **Removed `seedDefaultBillingLedgerData()`** from `prisma/seed.ts` — it seeded 6 fake customers + 10 fake trips/invoices on every container start, contradicting its own file header and `CLAUDE.md`'s seed rules; `backend/api-server/prisma/cleanup-fake-seed-data.sql` purges the rows it already left in Mercon's production DB (must be run manually, a redeploy won't do it). Fixed a real bug found in passing: `authService.ts`'s dev-only mock-login fallback had no `import.meta.env.DEV` guard, so a 5xx/network error in production silently granted a fake client-side Admin session. **Web dashboard**: de-branding codemod converted ~700 hardcoded `#E8450F`-family Tailwind arbitrary values (`bg-[#E8450F]` etc., 102 files) to the `bg-brand`/`text-brand`/... tokens already registered in `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block, plus the remaining on-screen literals (spinners, map pins, chart fills) to `var(--color-brand)`; new `useBranding`/`useApplyBranding` hooks fetch `/settings/public` and write it to the CSS vars + `document.title` at boot (login page included, pre-auth); new `BrandLogo` component replaces hardcoded `<img src="/mercon-logo.png">` in Sidebar/LoginPage with a Settings-driven `logoUrl` (falls back to the existing PNGs); `Sidebar.tsx` nav items gained an optional `moduleKey` filtered against `Settings.enabledModules`; new `RequireSuperAdmin`/`RequireModule` route guards (same pattern as existing `RequireRole`) applied to the 8 optional-module routes; `SettingsPage.tsx`'s previously-decorative "Company Details" tab is now a real superadmin-only branding/module-toggle form. Left deliberately out of scope: Excel export brand color (`utils/exportUtils.ts`/`exportInvoiceExcel.ts`) and the folder-color-picker palette (`CreateFolderModal.tsx`/`KpiCard.tsx`) — neither is app branding paint. **Mobile app** (`frontend/mobile-app/mercon-app`): `app.json` replaced with `app.config.ts` (`CLIENT_PROFILES` keyed by `APP_CLIENT` env, defaults to `mercon` so nothing changes for the existing build); `src/lib/api.ts` no longer falls back to `https://mercon.tech/api` on a misconfigured build — now reads the per-client `extra.apiUrl` from `app.config.ts` and throws if neither that nor `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` is set; SecureStore keys (`TOKEN_KEY`/`SESSION_KEY`) namespaced by client slug so two clients' apps installed on one device can't collide (`auth-context.tsx`'s separate hardcoded `SESSION_KEY` literal removed in favor of importing api.ts's); brand color unified into `app.config.ts extra` and read at runtime by `src/theme/tokens.ts` via `expo-constants` (native can't re-theme live like web's CSS vars, so this is still build-time, just centralized); `tailwind.config.js` reads the same `APP_CLIENT` env at build time. **Deploy**: `docker-compose.yml` parameterized (`CLIENT_NAME`/`POSTGRES_DB`/port vars, all defaulting to today's Mercon values — verified with `docker compose config` that nothing changes for the existing deploy, and the `pgdata` volume was deliberately left unnamed rather than risk pointing at a different volume than Docker auto-generated for Mercon's live data); added `nginx/client.conf.template` for provisioning a new client's Nginx config without touching the live `nginx/mercon-api.conf`. **Explicitly not done in this pass** (flagged as follow-ups, not attempted blind): `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml`'s tag-based/per-client-runner rework (recommended in the conversation, but it controls the only live production deploy and can't be tested here — needs the owner's review before it changes `git push` behavior); MTL's actual domain features (crane/subcontracted-vehicle tracking, "immediate trip" flow) — deliberately not designed without a scoping conversation with MTL, per Rule 0. Verified: backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` clean, mobile `tsc --noEmit` clean, `docker compose config` resolves without error, seed re-run twice against local dev DB confirmed idempotent (no fake data, `isSuperAdmin` set once and not overwritten), full settings CRUD + superadmin-revocation-takes-effect-without-relogin tested end-to-end against a locally running API.) · Previously (**Expenses Module** (`/expenses`, new page): salaries, fuel, rent, and every other operating cost had nowhere to be recorded — MaintenanceRecord only covers vehicle service orders, and Invoice only covers customer billing, so there was no way to log money going out. **Database & Backend** (`schema.prisma`, `expenseController.ts`, `expenseRoutes.ts`, `trashController.ts`): added `Expense` model (free-text `category` e.g. Salary/Fuel/Rent, `status` Paid/Pending, optional `driverId`/`vehicleId` links, `amount`/`currency`/`expense_date`/`payee`/`payment_method`/`description`, standard ref_id + audit/soft-delete columns) with back-relations on `Driver`/`Vehicle`; registered `GET/POST /expenses`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /expenses/:id` gated to Admin/Operator via `authorizeRoles`; wired into the recycle bin (restore/hard-delete). **Shared Types** (`packages/shared-types`): added `EXPENSE_CATEGORIES`/`EXPENSE_PAYMENT_METHODS` constants (free-text columns, not Postgres enums, same pattern as `VEHICLE_TYPES`). **Frontend Web Dashboard**: `expenseService.ts`, `ExpenseListPage.tsx` (KPI cards for total/paid/pending/salary amounts, filterable/searchable `DataTable` ledger, CSV export), `ExpenseModal.tsx` (create/edit form with category, status, optional driver/vehicle link, amount, payment method, payee, notes); added to the sidebar FINANCE group and the router at `/expenses`. No seed data added (owner rule: never seed fake financial records). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend, `vite build` succeeded; local schema push to the dev Postgres could not be verified in this session because Docker/local Postgres was not running — run `npx prisma db push` (or redeploy) before relying on the `Expense` table.) · Previously (**Savable & Selectable Workshops and Work Done Service Details in Maintenance Module**: Operators can now save workshops and service details (e.g. "Tire Puncture Repair", "Oil & Filter Change", "Brake Pad Replacement") so they are selectable directly in **Add Maintenance Record** instead of typing from scratch every time. **Database & Backend** (`schema.prisma`, `seed.ts`, `maintenanceController.ts`, `maintenanceRoutes.ts`): Added `SavedWorkshop` and `SavedWorkDone` models with idempotent seeding for default workshops and common service details. Updated `getWorkshops` to merge saved entries with historical orders; added API routes for `POST /workshops`, `DELETE /workshops/:id`, `GET /work-items`, `POST /work-items`, `DELETE /work-items/:id`. **Frontend Web Dashboard** (`frontend/web-dashboard`): `WorkshopField.tsx` upgraded to a selectable dropdown showing saved workshops with an inline `+ Save New Workshop` popover. `WorkDoneSelect.tsx` added to render multi-item selectable service chips with an inline `+ Save New Service Item` dialog. `ManageWorkshopsModal.tsx` added to let operators manage saved workshops and service details anytime from the top bar of the Maintenance list page. Verified clean build: `tsc` and `vite build` succeeded with zero errors.) · Previously (**Monthly Trips by Company** (`/trips/monthly`, new page): monthly-contract work ("this company gets N trips this month; assign a driver and a truck per day") was invisible as a plan — the trip ledger is flat, paginated 20 at a time and sorted by status, so answering "who is covering ARKAN on the 14th" meant scrolling and regrouping in your head, and "is every day of this month covered" was unanswerable. **Backend** (`tripController.getMonthlyTripBoard`, `GET /trips/monthly`, registered before `/:id` so "monthly" isn't read as a trip id — no schema change): returns a whole month in one response, already grouped customer → day, with per-company driver/vehicle/category rosters and a month summary (trips, companies, distinct drivers, distinct vehicles, value, trips still missing a driver or truck, status breakdown). A trip's day is `planned_start` falling back to `createdAt` — the same rule `getTrips`' date filter already uses, so the ledger and the board can never disagree about which month a trip is in; a trip landed on its created date is flagged `date_is_inferred` and says so in the UI rather than pretending it was scheduled. Filtering by rate category / vehicle type matches **either** the trip's own copied column **or** its rate card's, because trips created before `Trip.vehicle_type`/`rate_category` existed only carry the tier on the card — matching only the trip column would have silently hidden them from a "Monthly Round" filter. Money shown is `billing_amount ?? trip_charges`, never invented: a trip with neither contributes 0 and renders as unpriced. Capped at 5000 trips with a `truncated` flag the page surfaces, so a runaway month is visible rather than silently short. **Frontend**: `MonthlyTripsPage.tsx` (month stepper + `This month` reset, filters for company/rate category/vehicle type/status/search, Excel export of the flattened month, honest empty states) and `components/trips/monthly/` — `MonthlyCompanyCard` is one card per company (name, trip count, an amber "N to assign" badge, total value) holding a vertical stack of that company's trips: each trip is its own compact card with a date/status row and a labeled 2-column field grid (driver, vehicle, route, amount, category), long driver names and routes truncated with a hover title instead of stretching the card. Clicking a trip opens `TripDetailDialog` on the board (full field breakdown + an "Open full trip" button to `/trips/:id`) rather than navigating away immediately. **Revised six times, same day, on owner feedback**: pass 1 built a page-level 6-tile KPI strip and, per company, a Calendar/Schedule toggle — replaced with a per-company flat table per the owner's "just a table, date/driver/vehicle" ask. Pass 2: the table's wide horizontal rows were still awkward for long names/routes, and the owner separately flagged the per-company colour system (`companyHue`, a company-name-hashed pick from 7 hues for the avatar chip and accent rail) as generic, "slop kinda UI" — replaced the table with the vertically stacked trip cards described above (limited-width truncation instead of unbounded flex growth) and removed `companyHue`/`COMPANY_HUES` entirely in favour of one consistent brand-orange avatar for every company. `daysInMonth`/`dayKey`/`isToday`/`STATUS_DOT` (calendar-only helpers) and the KPI tile component were deleted from pass 1's code, not left dead. Companies still sort busiest-first. Pass 3: pass 2's vertical field-grid trip cards (a date/status row plus a 2-column labeled grid) still produced far more scrolling than the data needed and were not what "vertically stacked" meant — the owner wanted the *company cards* stacked vertically down the page (already true) each holding one **tight table**, and a click on a row (not the whole card) opening the detail dialog. `MonthlyCompanyCard` now renders a compact `Table` again — tighter row padding than pass 1's table, driver/route/category cells capped with `max-w` + `truncate` + a hover title instead of letting a long name or route stretch the row — with `TripDetailDialog` reused unchanged from pass 2 on row click. Pass 4: the owner's "vertically stacked" turned out to mean company cards placed side by side (each column reading top-to-bottom is "vertical"), not the single full-width column pass 3 built — confirmed with `AskUserQuestion` before touching code, given three prior misreads of the same phrase. `MonthlyTripsPage`'s company list is now `grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2 gap-4 items-start` (two per row on desktop, one on mobile, `items-start` so a company with fewer trips doesn't stretch to match a taller neighbour) instead of a single vertical stack — cuts the up-down scrolling a full-width column forced once there were more than a couple of companies. Pass 5: trimmed the table itself from 7 columns to 4 — Date, Driver, Vehicle, Status — on the owner's ask not to clutter the row with lower-priority fields; Route, Category and Amount are still there, just moved into `TripDetailDialog` (already the one-click destination since pass 2) instead of the row. Pass 6 ("overall page UI/UX upgrade"): the page was three detached white boxes stacked with `gap-5` — a header strip, a search card, a filter card — which read as three unrelated things rather than one control surface for one list. They are now a single **command bar** card: row 1 is the icon badge + title + subtitle on the left and, on the right, a segmented `MonthStepper` (‹ August 2026 › with a formatted text label and a transparent native `input[type=month]` overlaid so clicking still opens the platform month picker, plus a "Today" segment that appears only off the current month), an icon-only refresh/export pair grouped in one bordered container, and the single primary `New Trip` button. Row 2 joins the search box and all four filter `Select`s into **one** segmented control — one border, internal hairline dividers (`divide-x`), borderless children — instead of five separately-bordered pills. Row 3 is a result-context line (`August 2026 · 8 companies · 124 trips · 6 need assignment`, the last in amber only when non-zero) paired with **removable filter chips** (each applied filter as a chip with its own ×, plus "Clear all"), replacing the previous bare "Clear 3" button that never said *what* was applied. `appliedFilters` is derived once so the chip row and the clear-all count cannot disagree. Company cards were retuned to match: header is now avatar + name + a phone/total meta line on the left with the trip count and unassigned badge right-aligned (previously all crammed inline after the name), table headers dropped to `h-8`/`text-[10px]`, status right-aligned, and the detail dialog restructured into a tinted "Scheduled" block + labelled `Detail` pairs on hairline-separated rows. Page padding `pb-6`→`pb-8`, section rhythm `gap-5`→`gap-4`. Rate category / vehicle type options come from `RATE_CATEGORIES`/`VEHICLE_TYPES` in `@mercon/shared-types`, not a local list. Sidebar entry added under OPERATIONS next to Trips (`/trips`'s active-state rule updated so it no longer also highlights for `/trips/monthly`). **Not exercised in a browser** — no local Postgres or Docker in this environment; verified by `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server and `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard. Also **repaired PROGRESS.md itself**: this file had committed merge-conflict markers (`=======` / `>>>>>>> Stashed changes`) around two rival "Last updated" lines since `3e2dcd1`; kept the newer 2026-08-12 chain and spliced the 2026-08-11 tonnage-tier entry — which existed only on the stashed line — back into it so no history was lost)) · Previously (**Added Vehicle Type / Rate Category filters to the Rate Cards ledger**: `RateCard.vehicle_type`/`rate_category` were already stored and imported per-tier, but the `/rate-cards` list page only let you filter by status/search — there was no way to narrow the ledger down to just "Monthly Round" or just "13.5M-20TON" rows even though those are exactly the dimensions that distinguish otherwise-identical lanes. Added `vehicle_type`/`rate_category` as real server-side query params on `GET /rate-cards` (`rateCardController.getRateCards`, additive `whereClause` filters, same pattern as the existing status filter), wired two new dropdowns (populated from the canonical `VEHICLE_TYPES`/`RATE_CATEGORIES` lists in `@mercon/shared-types`) into `RateCardListPage.tsx`'s ledger toolbar next to Status, and extended the active-filter banner / "Show All Rates" reset to cover all three filters together. No schema or architecture change — the fields already existed, this only exposes them as filters) · Previously (**Tonnage tier / trip-type ("6 ton", "10 ton", "one trip", "two-way/round trip") now actually drives rate-card matching, instead of being decorative labels**: owner reported these weren't "properly connected." Root cause: `RateCard.vehicle_type`/`rate_category` were free-text columns nothing read at lookup time — `findRateForLane` (the one shared rule used by `GET /rate-cards/lookup`, `createTrip`'s auto-fill, and invoicing's fallback) filtered only by customer+lane and broke ties with `orderBy: updatedAt desc`, so a lane priced at both "6 TON" and "10 TON" resolved to whichever card was touched most recently, ignoring tonnage/trip-type entirely; `Trip` itself had no field to record which tier was actually dispatched; and the manual create/edit rate-card forms (`RateCardFormDialog`, `CreateRateCardPage`, `EditRateCardPage`) never exposed these two fields at all — they were only ever populated via CSV bulk import. **Not converted to a hard Postgres enum** (deliberate, discussed with owner): live DB access wasn't available to confirm every existing row matches a known list, and `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` runs automatically on every deploy — a strict enum could silently drop an unlisted legacy value. Instead, `VEHICLE_TYPES`/`RATE_CATEGORIES` (`packages/shared-types/src/index.ts`) are the 10/9 real values found in the 652-row import workbook (`docs/templates/MERCON_RateCards_ReadyToImport_Quotations.xlsx`), validated at the application layer only (Zod in the backend, dropdowns in the UI); the DB columns stay nullable `String`. **Schema** (additive, non-breaking): `Trip.vehicle_type`/`Trip.rate_category` (nullable strings) — copied from whichever rate card was actually applied (or typed by hand for a one-off price), so the dispatcher's tier selection survives even if that rate card is later edited, and trip-level reporting on tier becomes possible. **Backend**: `findRateForLane` (`services/rateLookup.ts`) now accepts optional `vehicleType`/`rateCategory` — `undefined` means "any tier" (old behavior, for callers that don't know), `null` means "only tier-less cards", a string filters exactly; wired through `GET /rate-cards/lookup` (query params), `createTrip`'s fallback matching, and `tripLifecycle.completeTripAndInvoice`'s legacy-trip fallback (only constrained when the older trip actually recorded a tier, so it doesn't wrongly narrow pre-existing trips to tier-less-only). `createTripBody`/`rateCardController` (`createRateCard`/`updateRateCard`/`bulkImportRateCards`) now validate `vehicle_type`/`rate_category` against the shared list via new `vehicleTypeField`/`rateCategoryField` Zod schemas, rejecting unknown values row-by-row on import rather than silently storing typos. **Frontend**: dropdowns (backed by the same shared list) added to `RateCardFormDialog`, `CreateRateCardPage`, `EditRateCardPage` — previously read-only badges with no way to set them by hand. Trip creation (`CreateTripPage`, `CreateTripModal`/`TripStepRatesBilling`) gained a tier selector inside the existing "new lane, no rate yet" flow, so a fresh rate card (or one-off price) created mid-dispatch can be tagged with a tier instead of always saving `null`; the existing "N rates for this lane, pick one" card picker (already tier-aware via badges) is unchanged. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend/api-server`, `tsc --noEmit` clean on `frontend/web-dashboard`. **Not exercised in a running browser or against a live DB this session** — no local Postgres connection was available; the enum-vs-validation decision and the workbook-derived value list are the two things to double check against the live database before/soon after this deploys.) · Previously (**Upgraded Top Customers on the Ilan Dashboard from a bare rank+bar list to real per-customer cards**: found that `reportsController.ts`'s `getRevenueReport` already computed the customer's `id` internally (via `customer.findMany({select:{id,name}})`) but never returned it — added `id` to the `top_customers` response (1-line, additive, verified only 2 other pages consume this endpoint and neither reads more than `name`/`value`, so no breaking change). That unlocked: rows now navigate to `/customers/:id` on click, show a real credit-exposure bar (`CreditExposureKpi` — previously built but unused anywhere — driven by `Customer.credit_limit` and that customer's real `Pending`/`Overdue` invoice total, reusing the same `invoicesRes` array already fetched for Actions Needed rather than firing new requests) omitted entirely when a customer has no credit limit set (never faked), and an "N active" trip chip from `customer.trips` when any are in an in-flight status. Avatars use the same initials-chip pattern as `UserChip.tsx`, colored from the same 6-hue palette introduced for Actions Needed's categories so the two flagship widgets read as one visual system. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` gained one new query (`Promise.all` over `customerService.getById` for the top 5 ids). `TopCustomersWidget.tsx` rewritten; `reportsService.ts` type updated. · Previously (**Gave each Actions Needed category its own color, not just its icon**: rows now carry a subtle full-row background wash and a left accent stripe in the category's color (amber/blue/rose/purple/indigo/teal), and the active filter chip tints to match instead of a plain white/gray surface. Locations moved off slate onto teal so all 6 categories read as distinct colors, not "5 colors + 1 gray." Severity pills (rose/amber/slate) and the brand-orange hero count are untouched — this only adds a second, category-level color dimension on top of the existing severity one. `components/dashboard/ActionsNeededWidget.tsx` only. · Previously (**Rebuilt Actions Needed into an "Action Center" on the Ilan (Admin) Dashboard**: was a 2-category (documents expiring, maintenance due) split-panel card; now a unified, urgency-sorted list across 6 real categories with filter chips (All / Documents / Maintenance / Delays / Invoices / Rate Cards / Locations), a brand-orange hero count + severity breakdown, and a "You're all caught up" empty state. Four new categories, each reusing an existing predicate/endpoint rather than inventing one: **delay stops needing a reason** (`reportsService.getDelayLog({ needs_reason: 'true' })` — server-computed, previously shown read-only only on the full Delay Report page), **overdue invoices** (same `due_date < now && status !== 'Paid'` check `InvoiceListPage.tsx` already uses), **rate cards not linked to a lane** and **locations missing an address** (same predicates `RateCardListPage.tsx`/`LocationListPage.tsx` already compute). Explicitly left out: unsettled post-trip charges (already its own widget/KPI card on this page — would've duplicated), driver risk-score/stuck-in-Draft/stuck-in-Maintenance signals (no existing threshold convention to anchor a number to), ICCES device staleness (`Vehicle.last_seen_at` is never read anywhere in the frontend today, unverified as populated), payment-approval-pending (no server-side filter, needs backend work), and snooze/dismiss (confirmed nowhere in the codebase — resolving means navigating to the real page, or for delays, a small inline action). That last case needed one: `logStopDelay` and the `DelayReason` types were fully built server-side but **never called from any frontend component** — the Delay Report page only showed `needs_reason` as a read-only badge. Added `components/trips/LogDelayReasonModal.tsx` (mirrors `PostTripSettlementModal`'s click-row→modal→submit pattern) so this is now a genuinely resolvable action instead of a dead end. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` gained 4 new queries (delay log, invoices, rate cards, locations) and the widget moved to its own full-width row (Top Customers also promoted to its own row) instead of squeezed into a shared half-width card. · Previously (**Gave the Ilan (Admin) Dashboard's Settlement Queue and Revenue KPI cards their own footer instead of a reskinned truck**: the animated route/truck footer (from `/vehicles`) fits Active Trips and Fleet Available — both literally about vehicles — but Settlement Queue and Revenue aren't vehicle concepts, so recoloring the same truck animation for them was decoration with no meaning. Settlement Queue now shows a real aging distribution (`KpiCard`'s native `progressSegments`) bucketing pending trips by days-since-completion (≤1d / 2-3d / 4d+, amber→rose), computed from `unsettledTrips` client-side. Revenue now shows a real 6-month sparkline (`KpiCard`'s native `chartData`) from `summary.monthly_revenue_chart`, replacing the truck footer — also less code, since both are `KpiCard` features already built for this rather than new custom SVG. Active Trips and Fleet Available keep the truck/route `KpiRouteFooter`. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` only. · Previously (**Redesigned the Admin Dashboard header row**: replaced the bare title-and-button strip with one matching the main `/` Dashboard's own header convention — a tinted icon badge, a 2xl title with an "Admin Only" pill (this page is unlisted from the sidebar, worth signposting), a subtitle, a live "Updated Xm ago" freshness label driven by `useIsFetching({ queryKey: ['admin-dashboard'] })`, and the same pill-shaped "Refresh All" button style as the main dashboard (was a mismatched `Btn` "secondary" pill before). The hand-rolled inline success banner (which pushed the KPI strip down whenever it appeared) was replaced with the main dashboard's fixed bottom-right slide-in toast instead, so header spacing stays stable regardless of toast state. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` only — no other page touched. · Previously (**Moved the Admin Dashboard off the sidebar to a direct-link-only route** (`/admin-dashboard` → `/Ilan`), per owner request, and restyled its KPI strip to match `/vehicles`'s instrument-panel cards — uppercase title, count+unit-label value, `trend`/`trendValue` chip, a `kpi-icons.tsx` line icon (not lucide), and the same animated "route map" `customFooter` (grid + street-line network + dashed route + pulsing 3D truck), now extracted into a shared `components/ui/KpiRouteFooter.tsx` instead of hand-duplicated per card. Unlike the Vehicles page, these cards are informational only (no `isActive`/`onClick` filter toggle — nothing on this page to filter into). `Sidebar.tsx`'s Admin-only entry was removed (page keeps `RequireRole roles={['Admin']}`, just unlisted). · Previously (**Added the Admin Dashboard** (`/admin-dashboard`, Admin-only): a new, separate page — the existing `/` dashboard is untouched — built from the owner's prioritized widget list. **Active Trips** (`ActiveTripsWidget`) shows a live in-flight count/list via `tripService.getAll({ status: 'Dispatched,AtPickup,InTransit,AtDelivery' })`, deliberately not the "total trips" KPI already on the main dashboard. **Post-Trip Settlement Queue** (`LaborChargeQueueWidget`) consolidates the main dashboard's three scattered unsettled-trip UI patterns (banner + insights alert + ledger card) into one `DataTable` with an "Enter Labor Charges" action per row, still driving the existing `PostTripSettlementModal`/`tripService.getUnsettled`/`updateFinancials` — no lifecycle change: there is no "Delivered" status, `AtDelivery→Completed` still happens automatically from the driver's mobile "Complete Delivery" action, and `is_post_trip_settled` is still a separate flag, not a gate. **Top Customers** (`TopCustomersWidget`) reads `reportsService.getRevenueReport().top_customers`. **Actions Needed** (`ActionsNeededWidget`) merges expiring documents (`documentService.getAll({ expiring_within_days: 30 })`) and maintenance due soon (`maintenanceService.getAll` filtered/sorted by `next_service_due`) into one triage panel, reusing the previously-unused `ExpiryCountdownKpi`. A small KPI strip (fleet available/on-trip, revenue trend) reuses `KpiCard`/`CustomKpiWidgets`. Gated with `RequireRole roles={['Admin']}` + an Admin-only sidebar entry (`Sidebar.tsx`), same pattern as `/settings/users`. No backend or schema changes — every widget calls existing service functions. · Previously (**Removed the simulated "Driver live dispatch chat" widget from the web dashboard**: the WhatsApp-style chat panel on the Dashboard (Chats/Updates/Calls tabs, the in-memory driver message simulation, the "Send Mobile Dispatch Message" modal, and the "Contact Driver" button on Active Transit Status) was mocked UI with no backend — no real-time driver messaging exists in Mercon. Removed the whole component along with its dead state/handlers (messages, activeChatDriverName, chat send/reply simulation, drivers-for-messaging query) from `frontend/web-dashboard/src/pages/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx`; the Labor Charges Ledger card now spans the full row in its place. No backend or schema changes. · Previously (**Search normalisation across every ledger and search bar**: searching only worked if you typed the value exactly the way it was stored — one whole-string `contains` per field. Typing a full name found nothing (the driver roster only searched `first_name`), word order mattered, and a plate or reference typed without its dash ("ksa1029", "drv0001") matched nothing. Search is now normalised in one place per side: `backend/api-server/src/utils/search.ts` (`buildSearchAnd`) builds the Prisma filter for the server-paginated ledgers (trips, drivers, vehicles, customers, invoices, maintenance, locations), and `lib/search.ts` in the web dashboard and the mobile app (`matchesSearch`) filters the client-side lists. The query is split into words; every word must match at least one field (AND across words, OR across fields), so word order stops mattering and "john riyadh" works. Case is ignored, and punctuation is bridged both ways — on the client by comparing separator-stripped text, on the server by also splitting a word at letter/digit boundaries and requiring each part in the same field. The client copies additionally fold accents and Arabic-Indic digits. Field coverage was filled in where it was plainly missing: drivers now search last name, ref id, phone, licence and assigned plate; customers search the contact phone; invoices search the trip ref; locations search the address. Verified against the local database: "drv0001" → DRV-1008's roster row, "najjar sami" → Sami Al-Najjar, "ksa1029" → KSA-1029, "trp 3009" → TRP-3009. Known gap: on server-filtered ledgers a *word* typed without its punctuation ("alnajjar" for "Al-Najjar") still misses, because Postgres can't strip the separator out of the stored value through Prisma; the client-side lists handle that case. No schema or API-shape changes.) · Previously (**Rate card import**: `RateCard` schema gained `vehicle_type`/`rate_category`/`via_location` — free text, not enums, since each customer's quotation names its own vehicle tiers ("5 TON", "DYNA 3 TON", "13.5M-20TON"). New `POST /rate-cards/import` (flat one-row-per-lane-×-vehicle-type sheet: Customer/Rate Category/Origin/Via/Destination/Vehicle Type/Price/Currency, mirrors `bulkImportCustomers`) wired to an "Import Excel" button on the Rate Cards list via the shared `ExcelImportDialog`; the customer must already exist (matched by name — a rate card import can't create one, `Customer.contact_phone` is required and isn't on this sheet); rows with `rate_category` "Surcharge" skip lane resolution entirely for flat fees (labour charge, per-stop fee) that aren't an origin→destination lane. Blank template at `frontend/web-dashboard/public/templates/MERCON_RateCards_Import_Template.xlsx`; the owner's actual 7-carrier quotation workbook (`docs/templates/COMPANY QUOTATION (1).xlsx` — JDL/IMILE/SHIPA/RTL/HORIZON/ARKAN BARWAN/AKS, each becoming a Customer) was converted to a ready-to-import flat file at `docs/templates/MERCON_RateCards_ReadyToImport_Quotations.xlsx` (652 rows) for the owner to sanity-check before it's actually imported — not yet imported. Schema change not yet pushed to any database (needs `prisma db push`, which happens automatically on next deploy).) · Previously (**Web dashboard build unblocked, list toolbars unified, mobile safe areas fixed**: the web dashboard build was failing on `main` — the deprecated `ScheduleMaintenanceModal` shim re-exported a name (`MaintenanceRecordModal`) that `MaintenanceRecordModal.tsx` never exports as a named export (it is the default export, plus a `ScheduleMaintenanceModal` alias), so `tsc -b` failed with TS2724 and the CI/CD deploy could not build the image; the shim now re-exports the default together with the existing `ScheduleMaintenanceModal` alias. Documents Center and the Invoices ledger each had their own toolbar row holding a search box and a status filter above the table, duplicating what `DataTable` already renders — both now pass their filter through `DataTable`'s `filterElement` and the standalone toolbars are gone (Documents keeps a grid-mode-only bar for bulk actions). On mobile, every screen switched from React Native's built-in `SafeAreaView` (which only insets on iOS) to the `react-native-safe-area-context` version, so Android screens no longer render under the status bar; the operator More screen shows the signed-in role instead of a hardcoded "Operator", and vehicle renewals whose vehicle was deleted are dropped instead of listing as "Unknown Vehicle". The desktop sidebar rail toggle also moved out of the header (where it shifted the header contents on every toggle) onto the sidebar's own right edge, vertically centred, so it stays put whether the rail is expanded or collapsed — superseding the "Menu" button described in the entry below. It is a 28px circle carrying a double chevron (a panel action, not a back arrow) with a 44px click target, and ⌘/Ctrl+B toggles it as well. No schema or API changes.) · Previously (**Maintenance details page rebuilt as a unified dashboard**: `/maintenance/:id` had a header, a separate lifecycle rail, a 4-tile metrics row, a work-report card, two side cards (Vehicle, Workshop) and a full-width Invoice card stacked below — six blocks pushing billing off-screen. Header, lifecycle rail and the four metrics are now one hero card with an integrated stat strip and a brand accent rail; the work report and billing/invoice sections are joined into one tabbed card ("Work Report" / "Billing & Invoice", with an invoice-count badge) so billing no longer needs a second scroll; Vehicle and Workshop are joined into one "Service Provider" card with divided sections. Borders/shadows moved to the same softer layered style used on the driver details page. No data or API changes.) · Previously (**Driver details page rebuilt as a modern dashboard**: `/drivers/:id` was four stacked blocks (header card, a 4-tile KPI row, scheduled-days card, trips table, documents card, plus two small right-column cards) that pushed the real content below the fold. Identity, actions and the four stats are now one hero card with an integrated stat strip and a brand accent rail; the icon-only actions (refresh/export/vault/delete) are a single segmented control. Scheduled days and dispatch history are joined into one tabbed "Trip Operations" card with counts on each tab and internal scroll instead of page scroll, and Credentials + Assigned Vehicle + Documents are joined into one right-hand "Driver Profile" card with divided sections. A license-expiry banner (expired / ≤ 30 days) appears only when it applies, the assigned plate is a chip that opens the vehicle, and borders/shadows moved to a softer layered style. No data or API changes.) · Previously (**Desktop sidebar collapses to an icon rail**: the dashboard sidebar can now be shrunk with a "Menu" button in the header, next to Back — it animates between 220px and a 76px icon-only rail (labels, group headings and the user block fade out; unread counts become a dot; every item keeps a tooltip), the main content reflows with it, and the choice is remembered in `localStorage` across pages and reloads. Rail mode is desktop-only (lg and up); the mobile off-canvas drawer is untouched.) · Previously (**Map tracking icons upgraded to transparent 3D assets**: replaced generic map markers with transparent 3D orange/green warehouses and 3D truck, removing boxy backgrounds and dark borders per user feedback) · Previously (**Workshops are remembered and offered for reuse**: every service-order form asked for the workshop name as a blank text box, so the same workshop got retyped — and mistyped into near-duplicates — on every order. `GET /api/maintenance/workshops` now returns the distinct workshops already used (case-insensitive, newest spelling and contact win, with an order count), derived from the service orders themselves — no Workshop table, no schema change. `components/fleet/WorkshopField.tsx` replaces the plain input everywhere a workshop is entered (Send to Workshop dialog, maintenance create/edit form, maintenance details edit, vehicle details Log Maintenance): it stays free text, but suggests saved workshops as you type and fills in the workshop's contact number when you pick one. A workshop typed once is offered from then on; the `workshops` query is invalidated after every service-order write.) · Previously (**"Mark Maintenance" now opens a real service order**: the Vehicles page wrote `Vehicle.status = 'Maintenance'` directly, so a vehicle could sit in the workshop with nothing on the Maintenance page to show for it — and no order to later mark completed. The workshop state is now owned by the service order end to end. `SendToWorkshopDialog` (`components/fleet/SendToWorkshopDialog.tsx`) collects workshop, type, odometer and reason and creates an `In_Progress` record; the API's `syncVehicleMaintenanceStatus` moves the vehicle to Maintenance off the back of it. The reverse is `POST /api/maintenance/vehicles/:vehicleId/return-to-service`, which closes every open order (Completed, `end_date` now) and releases the vehicle. Every entry point routes through those two: the row actions, the status-badge quick dropdown, the bulk actions, and the details-page banner and dropdown. On the vehicles list the workshop toggle is now a one-click button in the Actions column ("To workshop" / "Return to service") instead of being buried in the ⋮ menu, which also gained a "Service History" link — `/maintenance?vehicle=<plate>` pre-fills the maintenance search. Bulk "Mark Maintenance"/"Mark Available" became "Send to Workshop"/"Return to Service".) · Previously (**Vehicles stopped getting stuck in "Maintenance"**: a vehicle only left `Vehicle.status = 'Maintenance'` when a service order was *edited* from an open state to Completed/Cancelled. Every other path left it in the workshop forever — logging an already-completed order (the Log Maintenance modal on the vehicle details page defaults to Completed), deleting the open order, or moving an order to another vehicle — so the Vehicles list, its Maintenance Bay KPI card and the vehicle details badge all kept reading `Maintenance`. Vehicle status is now re-derived in one place, `utils/vehicleMaintenanceStatus.ts` → `syncVehicleMaintenanceStatus(vehicleId)`: any open order puts the vehicle in `Maintenance`, none releases it back to `Available` (a vehicle that is `OnTrip`/`Inactive` is never touched). It is called from create, update and delete in `maintenanceController`. The legacy `In Progress` (space) spelling now counts as open everywhere, including the list filter. Existing production data is repaired by an idempotent seed step, `releaseVehiclesStuckInMaintenance()`, which normalises `In Progress` → `In_Progress` and releases vehicles that have service history, nothing open, and have not been touched since their last order changed — that last condition is what stops it clobbering a deliberate "Mark Maintenance" from the Vehicles page. The maintenance list and details pages now also invalidate the vehicle caches so the change shows without a reload.) · Previously (**Create Trip step 3 re-laid out around the two stops**: the step stacked three full-width blocks — a pricing-lane box, then an "exact pickup" block and an "exact dropoff" block that each carried a permanently-open 220px Leaflet map plus four separate inputs (address search, stop name, address textarea, plus the hub combobox in a different section). The schedule and the price sat far below the fold and one stop's fields were split across two places. Step 3 is now a two-column grid of `TripStopCard` (`components/trips/TripStopCard.tsx`), one card per stop holding everything about it in the order a dispatcher thinks: pricing hub → exact point → planned time. `LocationPickerMap` gained a `compact` mode where the address search is the only field on screen; what the driver will receive (name, address, coordinates) is shown as a read-only summary line, and the map ("Adjust pin") and the manual name/address inputs ("Edit") fold open only when needed — the name inputs auto-open while the required name is still blank. Time presets trimmed from 9 chips to 7 shorter ones, the datetime picker's own preset popover already covers the rest. No behaviour, payload or validation change; the rate lookup, the pin-far-from-hub warning and the centroid-does-not-overwrite-a-searched-pin guard are unchanged. `LocationPickerMap`'s hardcoded light-mode hex colours were also swapped for theme tokens, which fixes its dark mode on the trip details stop editor too.) · Previously (**Vehicle P&L page rebuilt as a fleet profitability dashboard**: `/vehicles/financials` could only show one truck at a time — the owner could not see which vehicle earns the most or loses the most without clicking through every plate, and the page had no charts at all. The blocker was the API: `GET /vehicles/:id/financials` returns a single vehicle, so ranking the fleet was impossible client-side without one request per truck. **Backend** (`vehicleController.ts`, `vehicleRoutes.ts`, no schema change): new `GET /vehicles/financials/fleet` aggregates every non-deleted vehicle in three queries (vehicles, trips+invoices, maintenance) and returns one P&L row per vehicle — income, expenses, maintenance/renewal split, net profit, margin %, trip count — plus a `fleet_summary` (totals, profitable/loss-making/idle counts) and a fleet-wide `monthly` income/expense/profit series. The route is registered before `/:id` so the literal path is never captured as an id. The revenue rule that was inline in `getVehicleFinancials` is now the shared `tripIncome()` helper (`billing_amount` → invoice `total_amount` → `trip_charges`, counted only for `Completed`/`Invoiced`), so the fleet and per-vehicle reports can never drift. Both endpoints now accept optional `from`/`to` ISO bounds and both return a `monthly` series. **Frontend**: `VehicleFinancialsPage` is now two `Tabs` — *Fleet Comparison* (default at `/vehicles/financials`) and *Vehicle Detail* (default at `/vehicles/:id/financials`), with the tab switch rewriting the URL so both existing routes keep working. Fleet tab: 4 KPI cards, a **diverging horizontal bar chart** of net profit per vehicle (green/red, click a bar to drill in, capped at the 15 most significant), an expense-composition **donut** (maintenance vs renewals), a **stacked area** fleet income-vs-expense trend, *Most Profitable* / *Biggest Loss Makers* leaderboards with proportion bars, and a sortable full-fleet `DataTable` (sort by income/expenses/net/margin/trips; row click drills in). Vehicle tab: the plate `Select` became a searchable `Combobox` (a 100-item plain select was unusable), plus a `ComposedChart` of monthly income/expense bars with a profit line, a revenue-to-net breakdown bar, and the two itemized ledgers rebuilt as real `Table`s inside `ScrollArea` with totals footers. A `ToggleGroup` period preset (3M/6M/12M/All) drives the `from` bound on both tabs; Export Excel is tab-aware (fleet comparison sheet vs per-vehicle statement). New shadcn primitives scaffolded on the already-installed Base UI deps: `ui/scroll-area.tsx`, `ui/toggle-group.tsx`; `DataTable`'s `Column.header` widened from `string` to `React.ReactNode` so headers can carry sort buttons. Charts use the existing `ui/chart.tsx` + recharts — no new packages. **Not verified in a browser**: the local Postgres rejects the `mercon_user` credentials in `backend/api-server/.env` (`denied access on the database mercon_db.public`), so login fails locally; verification was limited to a clean `tsc` + `vite build` on both workspaces.)
