(**Maintenance module: sequential service-order numbers, date rules, uploadable workshop invoices, and a rebuilt details page**: owner reported four problems on `/maintenance` and `/maintenance/:id` — a new schedule accepted start/end dates years in the past, creating one returned a bare `Internal server error`, service orders had no human-readable number (the UI printed `#` + the first 8 chars of a UUID), and the details page was a tab bar (Work report / Invoice / Vehicle) with an oversized lifecycle stepper. **Schema** (`MaintenanceRecord.ref_id String? @unique`, hits production via `db push` on deploy): service orders are now numbered `MNT-001`, `MNT-002`, … through the existing `generateRefId` helper, which already gap-fills — so deleting `MNT-005` frees that number for the next order, exactly as asked. Because `ref_id` is unique across soft-deleted rows too, `deleteMaintenanceRecord` **clears** the ref_id on delete (otherwise the freed number would collide forever); `trashController`'s `MaintenanceRecord` restore branch therefore mints a fresh number when restoring an order whose ref_id was released. `prisma/seed.ts` gained an idempotent `backfillMaintenanceRefIds()` that numbers pre-existing records oldest-first and no-ops on every start after the first. **The 500 was never diagnosable from the response** — every `catch` in `maintenanceController.ts` swallowed the error and returned a generic `SERVER_ERROR`, so all five handlers now `logger.error({ err }, …)` and the real cause is visible in container logs. Three concrete 500 sources were fixed in the same pass: (1) `updateMaintenanceRecord` guarded its date conversion with `if (data.end_date)`, so an **empty string** — which is exactly what the edit dialog sends when a date is cleared — skipped the guard and was handed to Prisma as a `''` string for a `DateTime` column; a shared `toDate()` now maps `''`/`null`/`Invalid Date` to `null` for the nullable columns and drops the key entirely for the non-nullable `start_date`/`service_date`. (2) A `vehicle_id` that parsed as a UUID but pointed at a deleted/non-existent vehicle hit a Prisma FK violation; create now looks the vehicle up first and returns a 400 saying so. (3) `odometer_reading`/`cost` are now `Number.isFinite`-checked before reaching a `Float` column. **Date rules**, enforced in both places per CLAUDE.md: a new `validateDateWindow()` rejects a back-dated start or end on **create only** (an existing record legitimately holds old dates — editing an unrelated field must not fail because of them) and rejects `end_date < start_date` / `next_service_due < start_date` on both create and update; the web forms mirror it with `min` attributes (`min={TODAY_ISO}` on a new order's start date, `min={start_date}` on the end date) plus pre-submit checks, and changing the start date snaps a now-invalid end date forward rather than leaving it silently wrong. **`MaintenanceDetailsPage.tsx` rebuilt**: the three-tab switcher is gone — everything is on one page as sections. The 28px-circle stepper card was replaced by a 4px lifecycle rail with three milestone dots and labels under it, and the four 2xl KPI cards shrank to compact metric tiles. The work report is now laid out as an actual workshop document: a letterhead band (workshop name, type, plate, order no., service date), then "Scope of work performed" as accent-ruled body text, "Technician remarks", and a "Service record" field grid. Vehicle and Workshop are clean sidebar cards (the vehicle's plate gets a dedicated block); Invoice is a full-width section splitting billing details from **uploaded workshop invoices** — the new part: the invoice is issued by the workshop, so it's now uploadable via the existing `POST /documents` (`entity_type: 'MaintenanceRecord'`, `doc_type: 'Invoice'`), listed with open/remove actions and a confirm dialog. Added a Print action for the report. The list page gained an `Order #` column (and the grid cards a ref line), search now matches on `ref_id`, and CSV export carries `Order_No` instead of the raw UUID. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard. **Not exercised in a running browser** — Docker isn't running locally, so there is no local Postgres/API to log into; the create-path 500 in particular is fixed by reasoning plus the new logging, not by reproducing it.) Previously: **Fixed KPI counts changing when filtering on Vehicles, Drivers, and Invoices list pages**: clicking a KPI card or changing the status filter passed `status` to `getAll()`, which filtered the query response to only vehicles/drivers/invoices matching that status; because KPI values were computed from that filtered response, selecting any status caused all other KPI numbers to drop to 0 or collapse to the filtered count. Fixed by adding an un-filtered `kpiSummary` query (`['vehicles', 'kpi-summary']`, `['drivers', 'kpi-summary']`, `['invoices', 'kpi-summary']`) on `VehicleListPage.tsx`, `DriverListPage.tsx`, and `InvoiceListPage.tsx` — mirroring the pattern already used on `TripListPage.tsx` — so KPI instrument panel totals stay fixed and accurate across all cards while the list/grid/map filters independently. Verified: `tsc -b` and `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Fleet Excel bulk import shipped (Milestone 3.5)**: the templates, the field-mapping guide and the *export* side had existed for weeks; the import half did not — no `POST /drivers/import`, no `POST /vehicles/import`, no upload UI. Rows are parsed **in the browser** with ExcelJS (already a dependency for export) and posted as JSON, the same contract `/trips/bulk-import` already uses, so no file-upload handling or spreadsheet library was added server-side and the operator gets a real preview before anything is written. **Backend**: `bulkImportDrivers`/`bulkImportVehicles` + `bulkImportDriversBody`/`bulkImportVehiclesBody` (1000-row cap). Upserts on `phone_primary` for drivers (the only unique column — `license_number` is *not* unique in the schema) and `plate_number` for vehicles, so re-uploading a corrected file fixes records instead of duplicating them. **Status is deliberately never written**: a re-import must not flip a driver or truck that is out on a job back to Available. Both run **two passes** — the drivers sheet references vehicles by plate and the vehicles sheet references drivers by phone/name, so whichever is imported first has dangling references; pass 1 creates the records, pass 2 resolves the links. An unresolvable link is a per-row **warning** (record imported, assignment skipped) rather than an error, because 200 drivers should not fail over one mistyped plate. Driver phone matching is digits-only with suffix comparison (`+966 50 123 4567` / `+966501234567` / `0501234567` all match) and strips a trailing parenthetical, since the shipped template's own sample writes `+966 50 123 4567 (Ahmed)` — the file would otherwise have failed against itself. Ambiguous name matches are refused with an explanatory warning rather than guessing. **Frontend**: `importUtils.ts` (header matching that ignores case/`*`/bracketed units, accepts several spellings per field, locates the header row *by content* since the templates put banner rows above the table, and flattens Date/formula/rich-text cells — dates emitted from local parts, not `toISOString()`, which shifts UTC and can move a date back a day) plus a shared `ExcelImportDialog` (drag-drop, preview of row count / missing required columns / ignored headers / first 3 rows, then a created-updated-failed result panel splitting hard failures from warnings). Wired into the Drivers and Vehicles list pages. **Two real bugs found and fixed while verifying against the actual template files**: (1) the vehicles template **could not be opened by ExcelJS at all** — its sheet name `🚛 Vehicles & Trailers Directory` is 32 UTF-16 units (an emoji counts as two) against Excel's 31-char cap, so readers truncate it and then throw `worksheet name already exists`; shortened in `scripts/generate_excel_templates.py` and regenerated. (2) The generator wrote to `frontend/public/templates`, but this is a Vite app where only `frontend/web-dashboard/public` is served — the templates had been generated to a folder served to nobody, so every in-app template download link 404'd; paths are now repo-root-relative instead of hardcoded `d:\Mercon\...`. Verified end-to-end by parsing both shipped templates with the real matching rules: all driver fields map, all vehicle fields except the optional `trailer_type` (which has no column) map, header row located at row 7 in both, and the 50 blank spacer rows are correctly skipped leaving only the 3 sample rows. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Stop addresses are now visible and correctable on the web**: an operator could not see the address a driver had been given (`TripDetailsPage` rendered `location_name` only), and once a trip existed a wrong address was wrong forever — the only stop-level endpoint was `PATCH /trips/:id/stops/:stopId/delay`, and `EditTripPage` edited status/driver/vehicle and nothing else. New `PATCH /trips/:id/stops/:stopId` (`updateTripStop` + `updateTripStopBody`, every field optional since the usual case is fixing one address) updates the stop's label, full address, lane endpoint and pin. **Editable while the trip is in flight** — a wrong address is exactly what needs fixing mid-trip — and refused with 409 once the trip is Completed/Invoiced/Cancelled, because a finished trip is a record of what happened and its invoice is already priced off that lane. Timestamps are never touched: moving a pin does not un-arrive a driver, and the delay report reads arrival/departure, which stay as recorded. **Web**: `TripDetailsPage` now prints the stop's address under its name (falling back to the lane endpoint's address) and says *"No address — the driver only gets a map pin"* in amber when neither exists, rather than rendering a blank line; the lane endpoint's name is appended to the "Pickup Location"/"Drop-off Location" heading. New `StopAddressEditor` component drives a third section on `EditTripPage`, reusing `LocationCombobox` + the (already address-aware) `LocationPickerMap`; it saves per-stop on its own button rather than joining the manifest submit, since it hits a different endpoint and the common case is fixing one address without touching driver/vehicle/status. Closed trips render the stop read-only with an explicit reason. `TripStop` on the web service gained `location_address`/`locationId`/`location`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Locations management page** (`/locations`, new): the `/locations` CRUD API shipped without any UI, so places could only ever be born inline from a dropdown — nobody could see the full list, fix a misspelling, fill in a missing address, or retire an entry typed by accident, and those mistakes are invisible at the point of creation. `getLocations` now returns `_count` for `originRateCards`/`destinationRateCards`/`tripStops` (all filtered to non-deleted), which is what makes the page answer its two real questions: which places are actually used, and which are incomplete or abandoned. New `LocationListPage` + `LocationFormDialog` (`components/locations/`): four derived KPIs (total / on a rate card / missing address / unused), a banner when any place has no address (a stop defaulting to one hands the driver coordinates and nothing to read), search, and All/Unused/Missing-address filters. Renaming is the fix for a typo — the API already cascades the new name into every rate card quoting it, and the dialog warns before you do it; it is **not** a merge, so if both spellings already exist the rename is refused as a duplicate and the rate cards must be re-pointed by hand (noted as a gap, not built). Delete surfaces the API's own 409 verbatim ("used by N rate cards") rather than a generic failure, and warns separately when trip stops reference the place — those trips keep their own address and coordinates, only the picker entry goes. Sidebar entry added under FINANCE next to Rate Cards, since a location exists to be one end of a priced lane. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard. **Not visually verified in a browser** — the local dev API refused connections, so login couldn't complete.) Previously: **Driver app now receives real from/to addresses; duplicate planned-start removed; Google Maps scoped**: owner reported that a dispatched trip reaching the driver's phone showed "no proper address", asked whether Google Maps is connected (**it is not — nothing in the codebase calls Google**), and flagged the same date being asked for twice on Create Trip. Root cause of the address gap was **not** the backend: `mobileTripController` has always returned full stop rows including `location_name`, but the mobile `TripStop` interface (`lib/trips.ts`) declared only `location_lat`/`location_lng`, so the app literally could not see the field and every driver screen rendered coordinates or nothing. **Schema** (additive, nullable): `TripStop.location_address` (full postal address of the exact yard — kept separate from `location_name`, which is the short label reports group by, because truncating an address into a label is lossy and irreversible) and `Location.address` (the verified address behind a lane endpoint, inherited by stops that default to that place). **Backend**: `mobileTripController` now has one shared `tripInclude` — its four endpoints each built their own before, some with `stops: true`, which returns stop columns but not the related `Location`, so *which endpoint the app happened to call* decided whether the driver saw a route or a pair of coordinates; stops now always carry `location {id,name,address}`. `createTripBody` accepts `location_address` (500-char cap, vs 120 for the name). **Driver app**: `TripStop` gained `location_name`/`location_address`/`location` plus `stopLabel()`/`stopAddress()` helpers (most-specific-first fallback: the stop's own address → its lane endpoint's address → null). Home screen's route timeline showed only "PICKUP"/"DELIVERY" and a time — it now leads with the place name and address; `LiveNavigationScreen`'s bottom card gained a "PICKING UP AT"/"DELIVERING TO" block (it was a blue line and a distance that never named the destination); `PickupVerificationScreen` gained a location card so the driver can confirm they're at the right yard before photographing cargo; `DeliveryVerificationScreen` shows "Delivered to" + address in the review step; the trips list gained a route line, since a customer name alone doesn't distinguish two runs for the same customer on the same day. **Web**: `LocationPickerMap` kept the full Nominatim `display_name` instead of discarding it after extracting the short label (this discard was the original reason no address ever reached a driver), and gained an editable address textarea so a hand-dropped pin can still be given one; Create Trip sends `location_address` per stop and pre-fills it from the chosen `Location`. **Duplicate date removed**: step 1's "Planned Trip Start (Optional)" block deleted entirely and `Trip.planned_start` now derives from the step 3 pickup arrival time — one moment, entered once, where previously the two were separate fields that could silently disagree. **Google Maps decision**: addresses only (Places API (New) + Geocoding) — map *display* stays on OpenStreetMap tiles because Google bills per map load and the app renders maps on tracking, trip details and several KPI cards. Not yet wired: needs an owner-provisioned key (account creation + billing is theirs to do); `docs/technical/GOOGLE_MAPS_SETUP.md` documents exactly which two APIs to enable, the referrer/API key restrictions, the budget alert, that Vite inlines the var at build time so it must also be a compose build arg, and the requirement that absence of the key falls back to Nominatim rather than breaking. **Known remaining risk, deliberately out of scope**: driver turn-by-turn still routes via `router.project-osrm.org`, a public demo server with no SLA. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server and mobile app, `tsc -b` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Rate cards rebuilt around priced lanes, and wired to trip creation and invoicing**: owner reported the rate card module and its connection to Create Trip were wrong end to end. Root cause: `RateCard.route_origin`/`route_destination` were free text, and `CreateTripPage` matched them with a substring test against the *stop's* name — "Khamis Sorting Center" never contains "Riyadh", so matching fell through to "any active card for this customer" and finally to `rateCards[0]`, meaning the price shown was frequently not the price for that route. `tripLifecycle.completeTripAndInvoice()` then re-guessed the rate with a *different* rule (and a hardcoded `1000.0` fallback), so the invoice could quote a price the dispatcher was never shown. **Schema** (`schema.prisma`, hits production via `db push` on deploy): new **`Location`** model (`name`, `slug` unique = lowercased/whitespace-collapsed name so "Madina"/"Madinah"/"riyadh " can't become separate lanes, optional default `lat`/`lng`, soft-delete); `RateCard` gained `originLocationId`/`destinationLocationId` FKs (`route_origin`/`route_destination` kept as a denormalised copy of the names so old rows, exports and invoices still render) plus an index on `[customerId, originLocationId, destinationLocationId]`; `Trip` gained `rateCardId`; `TripStop` gained `locationId` (the lane endpoint "Riyadh", distinct from `location_name`/lat/lng which are the exact yard the driver navigates to). **Pricing rule, now single-sourced** in `services/rateLookup.ts` (`findRateForLane`, deliberately a service not a controller — importing a controller from `tripLifecycle` would pull `index.ts` and the express app in at module load): a card with `customerId: null` is the **standard** price for a lane and applies to every customer; a card with a customer **overrides** it for that customer only; order is customer's card → standard card → nothing. `createTrip` and `completeTripAndInvoice` both call it, and the flat `1000.0` invoice fallback was removed (an invented price is indistinguishable from an agreed one; an unpriced trip now invoices 0, which is visibly wrong and gets corrected). **Backend**: new `locationController`/`locationRoutes` (`/locations`, Admin+Operator) with `resolveLocation()` that creates-or-revives by slug and is shared with the rate card controller; renaming a location cascades into every rate card's denormalised names; deleting one is refused (409) while a live rate card still prices it. `rateCardController` rewritten — customer is now optional, lanes resolve from ids *or* names (so the trip wizard can name a new place inline), duplicate lanes per scope are refused with a message naming the clashing card, plus new `GET /rate-cards/lookup` and `POST /rate-cards/:id/assign` (copy a lane's price onto N customers; customers who already price that lane come back in `skipped`, never silently overwritten). `createTripBody` accepts `rate_card_id` and `stops[].location_id`. **Frontend**: new `locationService`, `LocationCombobox` (searchable, creates a place inline from the typed text), `RateCardFormDialog` (one add/edit form shared by the rate cards list, the details page and the customer page, so the standard-vs-customer choice is asked identically everywhere) and `AssignRateCardDialog`. Create/Edit Rate Card pages rebuilt on lane pickers with an explicit "applies to" choice (the create form previously *required* a customer, so the standard-lane case the schema already supported was unreachable from the UI). **Create Trip step 3** now picks an Origin and Destination `Location` above each map (picking one moves the map pin to that place's default coordinates and pre-fills the stop name, both still editable), looks the rate up server-side as customer+lane change, labels which tier matched ("Customer rate"/"Standard rate"), and when the lane is unpriced offers to save the typed price as the standard rate / for this customer only / not at all — creating the card before the trip so `rateCardId` is recorded, and dispatching the trip anyway if that save fails. Customer details page now lists the customer's own rates *and* the standard lanes they inherit (suppressing any standard lane they've overridden), with add/edit/override inline. **Invented content removed** (same rule as the maintenance page): `RateCardDetailsPage`'s "~480 km", "Est. Transit: 6h 15m", "24.0 Metric Tons", "Heavy Flatbed / Reefer / Lowbed", detention/overweight/night-premium fee schedule and "VERIFIED" badge — none of those fields exist in the schema and they printed identically on every card — replaced by a real "other rates on this lane" comparison; the customer page's rate block was rendering `rc.origin_city`/`rc.destination_city`/`rc.asset_type`, none of which exist on a rate card, so it had been showing "undefined ➔ undefined / Equipment: Flatbed"; the list page's fabricated KPIs (`'Riyadh → Jeddah'` default lane, `tripCount={42}`, `+4.2%`, `percentage: 90`, `|| 85`) and the create form's fake name/city presets ("SABIC Dammam Route", "Saudi Aramco Jubail Express"). Rate cards whose endpoints predate lanes are flagged "Not linked" in the list, on the details page and in the editor, because they can never match a trip until re-pointed. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Maintenance details page (`/maintenance/:id`) rebuilt on real data only**: owner flagged the page for duplicated branding chrome and invented content. Backend: `createDriverBody`/`updateDriverBody` (`schemas/index.ts`) gained `assigned_vehicle_id` (uuid, nullable, optional); `driverController.ts`'s `getDrivers`/`getDriverById` now `include: { assignedVehicle: true }`, `createDriver` accepts it, and `updateDriver` (previously a blind `...req.body` spread) now explicitly maps the snake_case API field to the camelCase Prisma column since the two no longer match — both create/update paths surface a friendly "That vehicle is already assigned to another driver" message on the `P2002` unique-constraint hit. Frontend: `driverService.ts`'s `Driver`/`CreateDriverPayload` types gained `assignedVehicleId`/`assignedVehicle`/`assigned_vehicle_id`; `AddDriverPage.tsx` and `EditDriverPage.tsx` each gained an "Assigned vehicle" `Combobox` field (same pattern as the trip wizard的 vehicle picker); `CreateDriverModal.tsx` (the inline "Add driver" dialog used mid-trip-dispatch) gained the same field so a driver created on the fly can also get a default vehicle immediately. `CreateTripPage.tsx` gained a `useEffect` that sets `vehicleId` from the selected driver's `assignedVehicleId` when it's still in the `Available` vehicle list — pre-fills but doesn't lock the field, so the dispatcher can still override per trip. Decorative multi-color icon chips (blue/violet/emerald for Customer/Driver/Vehicle) were left on Tailwind's palette since `Header.tsx`'s nav pills already establish that as an accepted pattern for categorical icon coloring, not covered by the gray/border rules. Rebuilt `TripDetailsPage.tsx` around that: a condensed status strip (trip id/status, customer/driver/vehicle/ETA in one row, a mini "created → departed → in transit" activity line, progress % + bar, and Distance/Elapsed/Stops/Avg-speed as inline text stats — replacing the old separate hero card + KPI strip + 4-step horizontal timeline), the live map promoted to the dominant element beside a compact 4-checkpoint "Activity" panel (replacing the separate Route-details card), and an amber inline banner with inline driver/vehicle `Combobox` assignment that only appears when a Draft trip is actually blocked (replacing the always-visible bulky driver/vehicle assignment cards). Replaced with `exceljs` (real `.xlsx`, added as a new dependency) and `jspdf`+`jspdf-autotable` (real generated `.pdf`, downloaded directly, no print dialog). Two shared generators added: `exportExcelTable`/`exportPDFTable` (explicit title+headers+rows, used by pages that build a custom column set) and `exportExcel`/`exportPDF` (derives headers from a plain object array, mirroring `downloadCSV`'s existing header/value logic). Both classify each cell (currency/number/date/status-good/status-bad/text) from the header text and value shape to apply brand-colored (`#E8450F`) header bands, zebra striping, bold double-bordered totals rows, right-aligned `SAR #,##0.00` currency formatting, green/red status pill fills, frozen header + autofilter (Excel), and a branded footer with page numbers (PDF). Rewired every existing Excel/PDF export call site: `VehicleListPage.tsx`, `DriverListPage.tsx`, `CustomerDetailsPage.tsx` (customer trip ledger), `CustomReportPage.tsx` (custom trip ledger) for Excel; `TripListPage.tsx` (both the filtered-export dialog and the row-selection bulk action) for PDF — all now produce `.xlsx`/`.pdf` filenames instead of `.xls`. CSV exports were left as-is (already a real, correctly-formatted CSV). The separate Export CSV/PDF buttons were replaced with one Export dialog (status group, driver, vehicle, date range), and `GET /trips` (`tripController.ts`) plus `tripService.ts` gained a `vehicle_id` filter to support it. Not verified in-browser (dashboard requires auth; another session had the dev server on this port). Earlier the same day: **site-wide `--font-mono` set to JetBrains Mono**: `frontend/web-dashboard` added `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` (self-hosted, matching the existing `@fontsource-variable/geist`/`inter` pattern) and declared `--font-mono` in `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block, which previously had no override — Tailwind's `font-mono` utility (used across 41 files for plate numbers, ref IDs, timestamps, currency) was falling back to the default OS-monospace stack. `--font-sans` (Geist-based) and the separate "Geist Mono Numbers" digit-only `@font-face` hack were left untouched — out of scope for this change. Verified: `font-mono` utility now resolves to `'JetBrains Mono Variable'` via computed styles, the woff2 loads with a 200 from `node_modules` locally (self-hosted, no CDN), and both `tsc --noEmit` and a full `vite build` pass. Not checked past the login screen in-browser (dashboard requires auth). Earlier the same day: **Active Freight Trips Carousel hover effects toned down** — the lift/glow/gradient-overlay hover animations added in the carousel rebuild below (translate-y lift, colored shadow glow, scale-in accent rail, gradient map overlay, rotating gauge icon, sliding CTA arrow, animated ping dot, gradient corridor/progress fills) were replaced with a plain border-color + `shadow-sm` transition per owner feedback ("that's AI slop"), and the card background moved from `#FAFAFA` to white with a visible border instead of a faint ring, matching the app's existing restrained style. Earlier the same day: **Active Freight Trips Carousel rebuilt on shadcn**: `components/trips/TripCardSwiper.tsx` (dashboard) moved off its hand-rolled `overflow-x-auto` + scroll-listener carousel onto the shadcn **`Carousel`** primitive (`components/ui/carousel.tsx`, added — embla-carousel-react, already a dependency), gaining drag, arrow-key nav, snap-accurate index tracking and clickable dot indicators wired through a new `useCarousel()`-consuming `CarouselToolbar` in the card header (`CardAction` slot). Three more shadcn primitives added to support it: **`avatar.tsx`** and **`hover-card.tsx`** (both on `@base-ui/react`, matching the repo's `base-nova` style — hover-card wraps Base UI's `PreviewCard`) and **`skeleton.tsx`**. Each trip card was redesigned: driver tile is now an `Avatar` + `HoverCard` (phone/plate/cargo on hover), vehicle tile a `Tooltip`, the route corridor uses `Separator` with pin/flag icons, the micro-map gained a gradient hover overlay showing km-remaining + ETA, and progress now shows distance done/left. Hover/focus/active animations are shadcn-idiomatic throughout (lift + ring + shadow on the card, a scale-in accent rail, icon transforms, animated dot width) and cards are keyboard-focusable (`Enter`/`Space` to open). Also fixed a latent bug: the progress bar的 `[&>div]` selector was coloring the **track** rather than the indicator, so every bar rendered visually full — it now targets `[data-slot=progress-indicator]` with a gradient fill. Verified by `tsc --noEmit` and a full `vite build`; not verified in-browser (the dashboard is behind login). Earlier: **Trip creation: "assign driver/vehicle later" added**: `POST /trips` no longer requires `driver_id`/`vehicle_id` — a trip created without one or both now lands in `Draft` status instead of `Dispatched` (driver/vehicle claiming and the assignment notification are skipped for whichever is missing). `/trips/:id/dispatch` (`dispatchTrip`) reworked to accept either field independently rather than requiring both, so a Draft trip can have its driver and vehicle assigned in separate calls; it only flips the trip to `Dispatched` once both end up set. `updateTripStatus` gained a guard rejecting a direct Draft→Dispatched status change when the trip still has no driver or vehicle (that path must go through `dispatchTrip`, which does the atomic Available→OnTrip claim) — web's `TripDetailsPage.tsx` mirrors this by hiding the "Mark Dispatched" quick action until both are assigned. Web changes: `CreateTripPage.tsx` (`/trips/new`) step 2 gained "Assign driver later"/"Assign vehicle later" checkboxes that disable the respective picker and relax the step/submit validation; `TripDetailsPage.tsx`'s driver/vehicle cards now show an inline assign-now picker (calling the reworked dispatch endpoint) when a Draft trip is missing one; `Combobox` (`components/ui/combobox.tsx`) gained a `disabled` prop to support the checkbox-disables-picker interaction. Not yet run against a live server for E2E verification — a concurrent session's in-progress `hazmat_flag` removal (`schema.prisma`) had the local API mid-edit and returning 500s on login at the time; both `backend/api-server` and `frontend/web-dashboard` typecheck clean (`tsc --noEmit` / `tsc -b`). **Hazmat removed entirely from the project**: `hazmat_flag` dropped from `Trip` in `schema.prisma` (production data loss on next deploy — owner confirmed), the zod schema, `tripController.createTrip`, both web (`tripService.ts`, `CreateTripPage.tsx`, `TripListPage.tsx` badge + KPI breakdown, `exportUtils.ts` CSV column) and mobile (`operator.ts` types, `TripDetailsScreen.tsx` cargo row) call sites, and the unrelated static "Hazmat Clearance" placeholder badge on `RateCardDetailsPage.tsx`. The IN-TRANSIT KPI's on-schedule/delayed breakdown had been (mis)computed from `hazmat_flag` as a stand-in for "delayed" — there is no real delay signal at the trip level (only per-stop `delay_reason`), so it now reads on-schedule = active-in-transit minus stopped, delayed = 0, until a real delayed-trip metric is built. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **removed the small uppercase "Module" badges next to page titles** — e.g. "Tariff Module" on Rate Cards, "Operations Module" on Trips, "Fleet Module" on Drivers/Vehicles/Maintenance, "Finance Module" on Invoices/Payment Status, "Commercial Module" on Customers, "Platform Module" on User Management, "System Administration" on Settings, "Dashboard Overview" on the dashboard greeting, "Fleet Roster" on Vehicles, "Operations Alert System" on Notifications — across 10 pages, per owner request; the `<h1>` title and the "Scope: ..." subtitle line beneath it were left untouched. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **fixed User Management's missing header/action buttons**: `DashboardLayout` never actually renders its `pageTitle`/`pageSub`/`actions` props — they're accepted but unused in the component body, so any page still passing them (only `UserManagementPage` did) silently got no page header and no action buttons at all, regardless of role. Reworked the page to render its own header row + Admin-only "Add Driver"/"Invite User" buttons inline, matching the pattern every other list page already uses (`DriverListPage`, `CustomerListPage`, etc: icon + title + badge + subtitle row, followed by `Button`s, all inside `children` rather than layout props). Also tightened permissions per owner request: Operators now see Admin/Operator rows only (no Drivers) and are view-only (no create/edit/delete/Add Driver); only Admin gets those actions and the Drivers rows. Backend `userRoutes.ts` split so `GET /users` allows Admin+Operator but `POST/PUT/DELETE /users` require Admin. Verified both roles by injecting a synthetic session client-side (no real credentials used) and reading the rendered page. Earlier the same day: added **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: a new hosted-runner (`ubuntu-latest`) CI workflow that runs on every PR into `main` and every push to a non-`main` branch — `npm ci`, `npm run lint -w @mercon/web-dashboard` (oxlint), then `npm run build` (build:types → api-server `tsc` → web-dashboard `tsc -b && vite build`), so a broken build/typecheck is caught before merge instead of failing on the self-hosted deploy runner. This is deliberately a separate workflow from `ci-cd.yml`, not a split of it: `ci-cd.yml` stays a single job because its steps are an ordered, stateful deploy sequence (containers must be healthy before nginx reloads) with no parallelism to gain on a single self-hosted runner — splitting *that* would only add fragile cross-workflow ordering. `ci.yml` is unrelated in trigger (PR/branch, not `push: main`) and runner (hosted, no secrets/prod access needed) so it's a genuinely separate concern, not a job split. Earlier the same day: backend **fixed a brief production outage**: the `ilan` seed commit below had hardcoded `phone: '+966500000003'`, which collided with an existing user's phone (unique constraint) already in the hosted DB; the container's startup chain is `prisma db push && seed.ts && node dist/index.js`, so the thrown seed error meant `node dist/index.js` never ran and `mercon.tech/api/*` 502'd entirely until the fix (drop the hardcoded phone — it's optional on `User`) deployed. Also added a permanent diagnostic step to `ci-cd.yml` that prints `mercon-api` container logs after every deploy so a startup crash is visible in the Actions run instead of a bare 502. Verified: `/api/health` 200 and `POST /api/auth/login` for `ilan`/`ilan1234` returns a valid token with role Admin. Earlier the same day: backend **`prisma/seed.ts` now also upserts a third canonical account, `ilan` (role Admin)**, added at the owner's explicit request alongside the existing `admin`/`operator` upserts — idempotent, own bcrypt hash, existing accounts still never touched. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **`/trips/new` (Create Trip wizard) restyled to match `/drivers/new` and `/vehicles/new`**: the 3-step wizard structure is unchanged (Customer → Driver/Vehicle assignment → Route/geofencing, the last step embeds `LocationPickerMap` so it wasn't collapsed into the single-viewport 2-column layout used on the other two pages), but every hardcoded `slate-*`/`indigo-*`/`rose-*` class was swapped for theme tokens, the step-manifest strip's boxed indigo icon circles were removed in favor of bare icons (matching the orange bare-icon convention already applied to page headers elsewhere), and the error banner now uses the shared `Alert` primitive instead of a hand-rolled rose div. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **User Management (`/settings/users`) opened up to Operator** (previously Admin-only) and turned into a combined "all platform users" view: it now also lists Drivers by querying `/drivers` alongside `/users`, shown read-only (a "View" action links to `/drivers/:id`) since Driver accounts have no login and are still only created/edited through the Drivers module; an "Add Driver" button links to `/drivers/new`. Backend `userRoutes.ts` changed from `authorizeRoles('Admin')` to `authorizeRoles('Admin', 'Operator')` on all `/users` routes; `createUserBody`/`updateUserBody` still restrict `role` to `Admin`/`Operator` only, so Driver-role web accounts remain non-creatable via this form, per the existing Phase-1 rule. `CLAUDE.md`'s "Who uses which app" table updated to match. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **`/vehicles/new` (Add Vehicle) redesigned to match `/drivers/new`**: same 2-column shadcn layout — form card (asset identifier / payload & telematics / optional trailer, separated by `Separator`s) beside a sticky "Registration summary" card with a live 4-item requirements checklist (plate, tractor capacity, trailer config, telematics); the old horizontal preview-strip + narrow single-column card + hardcoded slate/indigo classes were replaced with theme tokens and the shared `Alert` primitive for errors. Earlier the same day: **`/drivers/new` (Add Driver) redesigned** the same way: the full-width preview strip + narrow stacked form became a 2-column shadcn layout — form card (personal profile / commercial licence, separated by `Separator`) beside a sticky "Onboarding summary" card with a live 4-item requirements checklist, so the whole page fits one viewport with no scrolling; errors now render in the new shadcn `Alert` primitive (`components/ui/alert.tsx`, added), the page is a real `<form>` with a submit button, hardcoded slate/indigo classes gave way to theme tokens, a triple Ctrl+Enter submit binding (window listener + two `Btn` shortcuts, all firing the mutation) was reduced to one, and inputs/labels/headings were later sized up a notch (h-11 inputs, text-sm labels) for readability — both pages share that larger sizing. Also that day: **hosted demo-data workflow added**: `.github/workflows/demo-data.yml` loads the ~3,100-trip Delay Report demo dataset into the hosted database and removes it again — `workflow_dispatch` only, two actions (`seed`/`purge`), a typed `SEED`/`PURGE` confirmation checked before anything runs, and a `demo-data` GitHub Environment for a required reviewer. Both scripts run via `docker exec` inside the deployed `mercon-api` container (verified the image ships `scripts/` and `ts-node` — it already runs `npx ts-node prisma/seed.ts` at startup), and `DEMO_RESET` is never passed, so the seeder's destructive path is unreachable from CI. Row counts print before and after, tagged vs total. `ci-cd.yml`, `prisma/seed.ts` and `docker-compose.yml` untouched. Not yet run — the self-hosted runner is the only thing that can reach the production database. Previous session, 2026-08-05: mobile **operator Customers screen rebuilt** on `features/customers` at `/operator/customers` (More → Customers): KPI rail, server-side search/filter, sort, infinite scroll, per-customer trips/revenue/rate-card aggregated from `/trips`+`/invoices`+`/rate-cards`, and a role-gated overflow menu with optimistic suspend/delete; generic `SortDropdown` + `useDebouncedValue` extracted to `shared/`. Also merged in, same day: the **Delay Reporting module** built end to end — a `/reports/delays` page with three views behind one filter bar: the delay log (one row per late arrival, click-through to the trip), an on-time % grid switchable by route/driver/company/truck, and an analysis tab with KPIs, an hours-lost trend and four leaderboards, all at any period from day to year with a delta against the preceding window. Backing it: `TripStop.actual_departure`, `location_name`, and `delay_reason`/`delay_note`/`delay_logged_by`/`delay_logged_at` + a `DelayReason` enum; three `/reports/delays*` endpoints; and an operator alert that fires the moment a stop is reached 30+ min late. **Two real data bugs fixed underneath it**: the mobile/geofence status path never stamped `actual_arrival` at all (and dropoff arrival was backfilled at completion, dating every delivery to when its paperwork finished), so the database could not tell "arrived 2h late" from "arrived on time and waited 2h to unload"; and the mobile operator's Create Trip screen sent no planned times, so every trip booked from a phone was invisible to delay reporting. Reason capture is operator-only by design — drivers already report causes in the WhatsApp group. Verified end to end over real HTTP against a live Postgres, plus a browser walkthrough of the report page on a seeded dataset. Earlier the same day: mobile **operator Home + Drivers list screens rebuilt** on a new `features/dashboard` and `features/drivers` architecture — NativeWind + TanStack Query added to the mobile app for the first time (babel/metro/tailwind config, `QueryClientProvider` in root layout); Home is now AppHeader/context chips/search/scanner, two `DashboardMetricCard`s (Active Trips, Delayed Deliveries), an `ActiveVehiclesSection` carousel (driver+route+status per active trip), and a `DocumentExpirySection` (replaces the old Fleet Utilization block) with real expiring-document counts + a scoped All/Vehicles/Drivers/Company list; Drivers list gained two dark KPI tiles, search/status-filter/sort, and backend-paginated infinite scroll. Then: mobile **operator Driver Details screen** — new `features/drivers` screen at `/operator/driver-details?id=` with profile/statistics, assigned vehicle, contact + licence info, documents, current assignment, performance summary and call/message actions, built on a layered api→services→hooks stack with React Query caching, pull-to-refresh and optimistic status updates; the driver list now taps through to it. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **mobile responsiveness pass**: the fixed 220px sidebar became an off canvas drawer below `lg` driven by a header hamburger, the overflowing 8-item route pill bar is now desktop-only with Quick Create promoted to a header `+`, `DataTable`/`dialog.tsx`/`PageTitle` made phone-friendly, and page containers + stray fixed grids given mobile-first breakpoints. Also fixed a **pre-existing syntax error on `main`** — `handleUpdateStatus` in `DriverListPage.tsx` was missing its closing `};`, which made `tsc -b` fail and would have broken the Docker build/deploy. Previous session, 2026-08-04: fleet data onboarding: Excel export on the Drivers/Vehicles list pages now includes the driver's assigned vehicle plate and the vehicle's assigned driver — backend `getDrivers`/`getVehicles` now `include` the active trip + its vehicle/driver; styled `.xlsx` bulk-entry templates + `docs/MERCON_Fleet_Import_Guide.md` reworked to match those columns. Note: the templates are for **manual collection today — there is no backend import endpoint yet**. Previous session, 2026-08-02: mobile operator UI-kit consistency pass across all 8 operator screens + fixed a duplicate-bottom-nav bug; added real trip lifecycle actions (pickup arrive/verify, delivery verify, replace driver, cancel) to `TripDetailsScreen`; added Driver/Vehicle edit screens; added invoice "Mark as Paid"; added full Customers screen (list/search/create/edit); added an `Operator` "More" hub screen — fixed a real navigation gap where Vehicles/Invoices/VehicleRenewals were only reachable via deep link, not from the UI) · Owner: Hysam (solo dev + AI) · Deadline: ~1 month from July 2026

Wired KPI and period counters to match displayed ledger/board counts, verified clean bundle build (`npm run build`), and pushed to `origin dev`.) · Previously (**Follow-up sweep — same silent-failure pattern across the app**: audited every `useQuery` in `frontend/web-dashboard/src/pages` and `.../components` for the same "failed fetch renders as an empty/blank state instead of an error" bug fixed earlier today. Wired the existing `DataTable` `isError` prop through in `ExpenseListPage.tsx`, `ThirdPartyListPage.tsx`, and `MaintenanceListPage.tsx` (all three had the query result but never passed it to `DataTable`, which already has error UI built in). Gave `TripDetailsPage.tsx` the same `error || !record` guard every other Details page already has (previously a failed fetch left it stuck on the loading skeleton forever). Added a new `isError`/`onRetry` prop pair to both `TripKanbanBoard.tsx` and `CompanyTripKanbanBoard.tsx` (previously had no error UI at all — a failed fetch looked identical to "no trips") and wired them from `TripListPage.tsx`'s kanban view and `DashboardPage.tsx`'s `dashboard-trips` query. DriverListPage/VehicleListPage/CustomerListPage/RateCardListPage/LocationListPage/UserManagementPage/InvoiceListPage/DocumentsCenterPage/FleetPerformancePage/RevenueReportsPage/ReportsDashboardPage and all other Details pages were audited and already handle `isError` correctly — no changes needed there. Not done: mobile app was not audited for the same pattern; no live browser verification this pass either (working directory had concurrent uncommitted changes from another active session — some edits to 3 of these files were lost to what looks like a `git reset`/checkout mid-session and had to be silently reapplied and re-verified via grep before commit, so double-check this area if something looks off). **Reliability fixes for intermittent load failures on slow/unstable connections**: added a React `ErrorBoundary` (`components/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx`) around the routed outlet in `AppShell.tsx` and the `/login`/`/forgot-password` routes, with a one-time silent auto-reload for stale-chunk (`ChunkLoadError`) failures and a manual Retry screen otherwise — previously a lazy-import failure white-screened the app with no recovery. Bumped the global axios timeout 15s→30s (`lib/api.ts`) and React Query default `retry` 1→2 (`main.tsx`), since the flat 15s timeout was hit by normal-sized heavy list payloads (e.g. trips with `per_page:1000` + nested includes) on slower connections. Fixed `TripListPage`'s KPI queries (`trips-kpi-summary`, `trips-kpi-period`) to toast+retry on failure instead of silently rendering blank cards. Fixed `MonthlyTripsPage`'s monthly board query to show a distinct error+retry state on fetch failure instead of falling through to the generic "no trips this month" empty state (previously indistinguishable from a real empty month — this is the likely cause of "drivers don't show up for some company trips" on bad wifi). Added `onError` fallback+retry UI to the raw `<img>`/`<iframe>` document previews in `OwnerFolderDetail.tsx` and `DocumentPreviewSheet.tsx`, which previously bypassed the axios client entirely and just hung indefinitely with no feedback on a slow network. Not done: no server-side pagination added for the `per_page:1000` trip fetches (the underlying heavy-payload cause), no automated test coverage for these paths, no verification in the actual browser preview this pass (dev server port conflict). **Timezone setting — foundation only**: added `Settings.timezone` (`String @default("Asia/Riyadh")`) to the singleton Settings model — storage stays UTC everywhere, every DateTime column is already `@db.Timestamptz`, this only controls display/input conversion at the frontend edge. Added `COMMON_TIMEZONES`/`CommonTimezone` to `@mercon/shared-types` and a `timezone` field on the `Settings`/`PublicSettings` interfaces. Wired backend (`getPublicSettings`/`getSettings`/`updateSettings` in `settingsController.ts`, superadmin-gated same as branding). Added a Timezone dropdown to the web dashboard's Settings → Company Details tab (`SettingsPage.tsx`), backed by `settingsService`. Installed `date-fns-tz` and added `frontend/web-dashboard/src/lib/datetime.ts` (`useDeploymentTimezone`/`formatInDeploymentTz`/`toZonedDisplay`/`zonedInputToUtc`) as the intended single conversion point. `prisma db push` run locally, `tsc --noEmit` clean on both workspaces. **NOT done**: the actual sweep — every existing `date-fns format()`/`toLocaleDateString()`/`toLocaleString()` call across web dashboard (dozens of files: date pickers, trip/invoice/expense tables, exports) and the mobile app still formats in the browser/device's local timezone, not through the new utility; mobile has no timezone conversion utility at all yet. Browser click-through of the new Settings dropdown was not done (port conflict with another running dev server session) — now verified live in-browser too (logged in as admin locally, confirmed `GET /settings/public` returns `timezone: "Asia/Riyadh"` and the Settings → Company Details tab renders the dropdown with all `COMMON_TIMEZONES` options, current value selected correctly) — the local dev DB has no real trip/invoice data seeded, so the *display* sweep below could only be confirmed by typecheck, not visually, this pass. **Follow-up sweep**: converted 37 web-dashboard files (trip/invoice/expense/maintenance/vehicle/driver/customer/document lists, detail pages, Excel/CSV/PDF exports, WhatsApp share text) from `date-fns format()`/`toLocaleDateString()`/`toLocaleString()` (implicit browser-local timezone) to `formatInDeploymentTz`/`useDeploymentTimezone`, so every *display* of a stored timestamp now respects `Settings.timezone`. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both workspaces after the sweep. **NOT done — the write/input side**: date/time *pickers* used for data entry (`TripDateTimeField`, `TripStepRouteTiming`'s truck-arrival field, and the various `<input type="date">`/`type="datetime-local">` fields across trip/invoice/expense/maintenance create-edit forms) still build naive local-wall-clock strings with no explicit timezone, relying on whichever timezone the user's own browser/OS happens to be set to — this only silently matches `Settings.timezone` when the user is physically in that zone. Fixing this properly means reworking these forms to interpret entered wall-clock time as being in the deployment timezone (`zonedInputToUtc` in `src/lib/datetime.ts` exists for this) rather than the browser's — deliberately deferred as separate, higher-risk work since it touches live trip/invoice/expense creation and needs real browser testing, not just a typecheck. Also note: `frontend/web-dashboard/src/components/ui/date-time-picker.tsx`'s `DateTimePicker` component is dead code (zero importers) with a hardcoded "AST (UTC+3)" badge — left alone, not currently reachable from any page. **Mobile app**: no timezone utility exists there yet at all (still fully browser/device-local); not started.) · Previously (**Pricing: split rate_category into trip shape + billing frequency, narrowed both lists to the owner's taxonomy**: `RateCard`/`Trip` gain a new `billing_type` column (Monthly / Extra / custom) — previously `rate_category` conflated the two ("Monthly Round", "Extra Trip/Round Trip"), which is exactly why the real quotation workbooks didn't map cleanly onto a small category list. `VEHICLE_TYPES` narrowed from 9 carrier-specific spellings to 5 (5 TON / 10 TON / 3-4 TON / 20 TON / 40 FEET) and `RATE_CATEGORIES` from 9 to 4 (Single Trip / 10 Hrs Duty / 12 Hrs Duty / Round Trip) in `@mercon/shared-types`; both fields plus the new `BILLING_TYPES` keep a "Custom" free-text escape hatch in every form (`RateCategoryVehicleTypeForm` gained a third Billing Type control, new `BillingTypeSelect`/`BillingTypeBadge` components). Fixed a real bug found along the way: the backend's `vehicleTypeField`/`rateCategoryField` were a hard `z.enum(...)` that silently rejected anything the UI's own "Custom" toggle sent — loosened to free text (`billingTypeField` added the same way), matching how `Trip.rate_category`/`vehicle_type` already worked. Wired billing_type through rate-card create/update/bulk-import/list-filter/lookup, trip create/bulk-import/monthly-board-filter, `importUtils.ts` (`RATE_CARD_COLUMNS`/`TRIP_COLUMNS`), and the Rate Cards / Monthly Trips list filters. Also fixed `TRIP_COLUMNS.vehicle_plate` not recognising a "Vehicle No" header. `prisma db push` run locally; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend/api-server` and `frontend/web-dashboard`. NOT yet done: no browser click-through test, and `BulkAddTripsModal`'s compact grid-entry table (Tab 2) doesn't expose billing_type per-row yet (state/submit path exists, just no UI column — a deliberate scope cut to avoid a full grid layout rework).) · Previously (**Documents: AI-read staged import (bulk upload with automatic identification)**: New `DocumentImport`/`DocumentImportItem` staging tables — uploaded files no longer become `Document` rows on arrival. They are read by Gemini Vision, matched to a Driver/Vehicle, and only written to the vault once a human confirms in a review table, so an abandoned import is an unfinished import rather than permanent "Unassigned" clutter. New `documentMatchService` (plate digits / chassis / IQAMA / filename, each match carrying a human-readable reason) is shared by the import and the legacy auto-assign screen, and never auto-creates fleet records — the old path created vehicles named "Vehicle 9973" from unfamiliar plate digits, which is where the junk vehicles came from. OCR is now driven by the configured `DocumentType` catalogue and may answer null instead of defaulting every unrecognised document to `VehicleRegistration` (the cause of so much of the vault displaying as "Vehicle Registration"), so admin-added types are detectable with no code change. Duplicate guard offers Replace / Add-as-extra-file / Skip when a file lands on an already-filled owner+type slot. Review table is ordered worst-first and pre-selects confident matches, so a 50-file drop the AI mostly gets right is a few decisions rather than fifty. Verified against real truck PDFs: FAHAS and Isthimara correctly identified, plate 2541UDA matched to vehicle 2541 at HIGH confidence, and a duplicate Isthimara caught; corrupt files now report their real extraction error as Failed rather than a misleading "needs input". NOT yet done: browser end-to-end run, and the superseded `BatchVehicleDocModal`/`AutoAssignModal`/old batch endpoints are still present pending cleanup of existing unassigned legacy data.) · Previously (**Expenses: add custom category option, remove Payee/Vendor field**: New Expense Record modal's Category select now includes an "Add new category…" option that reveals a text input, since `Expense.category` is a free-text column (not a Postgres enum), so no schema change was needed. Also removed the Payee/Vendor Name and Employee Name inputs from the modal per owner request; `payee` stays in the payload/schema for legacy records but is no longer editable via the form. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on web-dashboard.) (**Pricing: itemized customer surcharges + driver payout per lane**: see §2 Backend table for the full writeup — new `SurchargeRule`/`TripCharge` models replace the old two flat `Trip` charge fields, new `RateCard.default_trip_charge`, settlement flow rebuilt around an itemized charge list; old columns not yet dropped pending a backfill run, and no real customer surcharge rates entered yet) (**Documents: fix folder-card navigation leaking into Driver/Vehicle pages, and dedupe auto-created owner folders**: clicking a Driver/Vehicle folder card on `/documents` navigated to `/drivers/:id/documents` / `/vehicles/:id/documents` — the driver/vehicle's own detail-tab route — instead of staying inside the Documents section; new `pages/documents/OwnerFolderPage.tsx` (reuses the existing `OwnerFolderDetail` component) is now mounted at `/documents/:ownerType/:ownerId` and the folder-card `onOpenRow` handlers in `DocumentsCenterPage.tsx` point there instead, with a "Back to Documents" link. Also found and fixed the root cause of a related complaint ("Upload Document" felt broken): `autoAssignUnlinkedDocs` (`bulkOcrController.ts`) looked up a per-vehicle `Folder` row from a `folders` array snapshotted once before its loop, but never appended newly-created folders back into it — so every document auto-assigned to the same not-yet-seen vehicle within one batch run created its own duplicate `Folder` row (confirmed live: one vehicle had 6 duplicate "Vehicle VEH-9973-4127" folders), flooding the Upload modal's Folder dropdown with hundreds of near-identical options. Fixed by pushing each newly-created folder back into the local array; also filtered the Upload modal's general Folder picker (`UploadDocumentModal.tsx`) to exclude `Vehicles`/`Drivers`-category folders entirely, since those owners already have an implicit folder via the owner-folder flow and were never meant to be manually filed into from this picker. Note: existing duplicate `Folder` rows from before this fix are still in the DB and were not backfilled/merged — only new duplicates are prevented. Verified backend/frontend `tsc` clean; verified live in-browser (folder card now opens `/documents/vehicles/:id` and stays under Documents; end-to-end file upload via `POST /documents` confirmed working; Folder dropdown confirmed empty of the Vehicles/Drivers clutter after the filter).) · Previously (**Fleet delete guards: block active-trip deletes, clear reciprocal driver/vehicle assignment, show real usage counts, and mark deleted vehicles/drivers wherever they're referenced**: `deleteVehicle`/`deleteDriver` (and their bulk variants) previously soft-deleted with zero checks — you could delete a vehicle/driver mid-`InTransit` trip, the reciprocal `Driver.assignedVehicleId` link was never cleared (a deleted vehicle stayed "assigned" forever; a deleted driver kept its vehicle's unique slot permanently taken), and every screen joining in a vehicle/driver (Trip list/detail, Expense list/detail, Maintenance list, Invoice detail) rendered its name/plate with no signal it was gone. Added an active-trip guard (409 `IN_USE`, same pattern as `locationController.ts`'s) to both single and bulk delete endpoints; deleting a vehicle now clears any driver's `assignedVehicleId` pointing at it, deleting a driver clears its own; new `GET /vehicles/:id/usage` and `GET /drivers/:id/usage` endpoints back real trip/maintenance/expense counts in the delete-confirmation dialogs (replacing the misleading "cannot be undone" copy); new `components/ui/DeletedBadge.tsx` renders a "Deleted" tag next to a referenced vehicle/driver's name wherever `deletedAt` is set. Verified backend `tsc`/frontend `tsc` clean; verified live against dev.mercon.tech's synced DB via direct API calls (create test vehicle+driver+trip, confirm 409 block while `Dispatched`, cancel trip, confirm delete succeeds and reciprocal `assignedVehicleId` clears) and in-browser (Trip Ledger + Trip Details both show the "Deleted" badge next to the now-deleted driver/vehicle).) · Previously (**Documents: paginate Vehicle/Driver folder card sections**: both sections rendered every driver/vehicle folder card at once (31+ vehicles unbounded) instead of a bounded page with a way to see the rest. New `components/documents/FolderCardSection.tsx` wraps `OwnerFolderCard` into a page of 8 cards with </> paging controls plus a page counter once there's more than one page, and a "View all" toggle that expands to show every card inline ("Show less" to collapse). Verified in-browser against real local data: "Vehicle Compliance Folders (31 Vehicles)" now shows "View all vehicles" + "1/4" pager, 8 cards per page. Verified `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Documents: right-side detail sheet on document click**: Clicking a document row inside a Driver/Vehicle folder (owner-folder detail page, or a folder card on the main Documents Center page) now opens a slide-in panel from the right — image/PDF preview with multi-file prev/next navigation and thumbnail strip, Document Information, AI Extracted Information with Re-Scan, actions (Download, Add File, Delete), and an Open Folder shortcut when opened from a folder card (hidden when already on that folder's own page). Master entities now fetch all fleet records while correctly filtering joined child transactions by date range, preventing master vehicles or customers created in prior months from being excluded from current-month report queries. **Frontend (`QuickReportPage.tsx`)**: Replaced hardcoded driver-only table columns, search fields, sorting, and exports with dynamic multi-module table column generators (`tableColumns`), field label mapping (`FIELD_LABELS`), money formatting (`MONEY_FIELDS`), dynamic export builder (`getExportData`), and dynamic instrument-panel KPI cards (`genericKpis`). Workspace builds verified with zero TypeScript/Vite compilation errors.) · Previously (**Entity Audit Timestamps & Time Display on Trip Details Page**: Verified and ensured all entity creation, update, and deletion operations across backend controllers (`Trip`, `Driver`, `Vehicle`, `Customer`, `RateCard`, `Location`, etc.) persist `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `deletedAt` timestamps along with `created_by`, `updated_by`, `deleted_by` user references. Updated `TripDetailsPage.tsx` and `tripService.ts` to display full date AND time (`fullDateTime`) alongside creator/editor/deleter `UserChip` components for Created, Updated, and Deleted audit trail pills. `npm run build` verified workspace compilation.) · Previously (**Grant/revoke superadmin**: no way existed to make anyone a superadmin except the seed's `create:` branch — which never touches an existing user, so Mercon's live `admin` account was permanently stuck at `isSuperAdmin: false` with no recovery path short of hand-written SQL. **Backend**: `updateUserBody` (`schemas/index.ts`) gained an optional `isSuperAdmin` boolean; `userController.updateUser` applies it only after a field-level check — re-reads the *requester's* `isSuperAdmin` from the DB (not the route's `authorizeRoles('Admin')`, which would let any Admin self-escalate) and 403s `FORBIDDEN` if they're not already a superadmin. Revoking is blocked with `409 LAST_SUPERADMIN` if it would leave zero superadmins on the deployment — same spirit as the existing "can't delete your own account" guard on `deleteUser`. `getUsers`/`createUser`/`updateUser` responses now include `isSuperAdmin` so the UI can render it. **Frontend**: `UserModal.tsx` gained a superadmin checkbox, shown only when the viewer is themselves a superadmin (`canManageSuperAdmin` prop, threaded from `UserManagementPage.tsx`'s `authStore.getUser()`) editing an existing Admin-role user — never offered at creation, so a new account is never superadmin by default; `UserManagementPage.tsx`'s role column gained a small "Superadmin" pill next to the role badge. **Production fix**: `backend/api-server/prisma/grant-superadmin.sql` — a one-time, idempotent `UPDATE "User" SET "isSuperAdmin" = true WHERE username = 'admin'` for the owner to run once against the live database (mirrors `cleanup-fake-seed-data.sql`'s pattern); once that's run, granting it to anyone else goes through the UI above, no more manual SQL needed. Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: a plain Operator gets 403 at the route level; a non-superadmin Admin gets 403 specifically on the `isSuperAdmin` field but can still edit other fields normally; a superadmin can grant it to another Admin and later revoke their own once someone else holds it; revoking the last remaining superadmin is blocked with `409`; the SQL statement itself executed and confirmed idempotent on a second run. `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Logo upload for deployment branding**: the superadmin branding form's "Logo URL" field was paste-only — no actual upload path, contradicting the original plan to reuse the existing generic upload endpoint. `SettingsPage.tsx`'s Company Details tab now has a real upload button (superadmin-only) that posts to the existing `POST /upload` (`uploadRoutes.ts`, already Admin/Operator-gated, images-or-PDF filter, 50MB limit — no new backend endpoint needed) and drops the returned `file_url` into the form; the URL field stays editable too, for a superadmin who'd rather point at an externally-hosted image. A small logo preview renders next to the field so what's about to be saved is visible before hitting Save. `settingsService.uploadLogo()` wraps the multipart POST. Actually persisting the URL still goes through the existing `requireSuperAdmin`-gated `PUT /settings` — the upload endpoint itself only stores a file, it doesn't touch Settings, so a non-superadmin Admin/Operator could upload a file but still can't make it the deployment's logo (verified: PUT /settings with a crafted logoUrl 403s for a plain Admin). Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: uploaded a real PNG, saved it as `Settings.logoUrl`, confirmed it comes back through the unauthenticated `GET /settings/public` (what the login page reads pre-auth) and that the file itself resolves at its `/uploads/...` URL with the correct content-type. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Backend enforcement for module toggles**: the `Settings.enabledModules` toggle built for multi-client productization was frontend-only — `Sidebar.tsx`/`RequireModule.tsx` hid nav and blocked routes, but `requireModuleEnabled` (`middlewares/rbac.ts`) was applied to zero backend routes, so e.g. `GET /api/invoices` kept serving data with "Invoices" toggled off. `CLAUDE.md` requires both-layer enforcement; this closes the gap. **Fixed a fail-closed blocker found while wiring this up**: `getOrCreateSettings()` created the Settings row with no `enabledModules`, defaulting to `[]` — since `requireModuleEnabled` fails closed, any deployment where a trip was created before the seed ran would 403 every gated module at once. Now creates with `[...MODULE_KEYS]`. **`MODULE_KEYS` (`packages/shared-types`) drops from 8 to 6** — `locations` and `rate-cards` are no longer toggleable, moved to core: disabling them 403'd the trip wizard's location picker/rate lookup even though trip creation itself doesn't depend on the HTTP routes (locations are auto-created in-process by `resolveLocation()`, rate cards looked up via `findRateForLane()`). **Backend**: `requireModuleEnabled` applied to `invoiceRoutes`/`expenseRoutes`/`maintenanceRoutes`/`reportsRoutes`/`documentRoutes`/`folderRoutes`/`trashRoutes` (gating inside the router, not at the `index.ts` mount point, so it covers both the `/api` and root mounts of the same router instance); per-route gates added to the invoice-creation aliases living on `tripRoutes.ts` (`/trips/:id/mark-invoiced`/`unmark-invoiced`) and to `GET /mobile/documents`; `reportsController.ts`/`delayReportsController.ts` now null out (not zero — 0 would read as "confirmed zero" rather than "not tracked here") invoice/maintenance-derived fields in `getSummary`/`getFleetPerformance`/`getCustomReport`/delay analysis when their module is off, so the dashboard keeps loading instead of 403ing outright; `vehicleController.ts`'s `getVehicleFinancials`/`getFleetFinancials` (the "Vehicle P&L" endpoints, core nav with no toggle of their own) do the same for their invoice/maintenance-derived numbers; `trashController.ts` filters listed items and rejects restore/hard-delete by module (`RateCard` stays exempt — now core). New shared `getEnabledModules()` in `settingsController.ts` is the one source of truth `requireModuleEnabled` and every controller above reads from, so they can't drift. **Frontend**: `lib/api.ts`'s response interceptor now redirects home on a `MODULE_DISABLED` 403 (covers a tab left open on a gated page across a superadmin's live toggle — normal navigation was already blocked client-side); `reportsService.ts` types updated to reflect the now-nullable summary fields. **Found and fixed in passing**: `VehicleFinancialsPage.tsx`'s Excel/PDF export functions called `.toLocaleString()` directly on `net_profit`/`total_expenses`/`margin_percent` — would have thrown on `null` the moment maintenance is disabled; guarded with `?? 0`. Deliberately **not** touched: nested `invoices`/`maintenanceRecords`/`documents` includes on `getTripById`, vehicle/driver detail responses — stripping those touches core read paths for data the viewing company already owns, flagged as a non-goal rather than attempted. Also flagged, not fixed: `thirdPartyRoutes.ts` has no `authenticateJWT`/`authorizeRoles` at all — pre-existing hole, unrelated to modules. Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: disabling invoices 403'd `/api/invoices`, `/invoices` (root mount), `/api/reports/revenue`, and the trip mark-invoiced alias; `/api/reports/summary` stayed 200 with `revenue_this_month`/`monthly_revenue_chart` null; `/api/trips`, `/api/locations`, `/api/rate-cards` stayed 200 throughout; trash restore of a disabled-module type 403'd; re-enabling took effect on the very next request with the same still-valid JWT; deleting the Settings row and hitting a gated route recreated it with every module enabled rather than 403ing everything. `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Custom Company Reports Generator (`/company-reports`)**: Replaced the broken `/reports/client-templates` prototype (which lost all fonts/fills/borders on export — `customTemplateExport.ts` copied cell styles from the sample data row *after* erasing that same row) with a byte-exact engine. New approach: `.xlsx` is unzipped, only the target worksheet's `<sheetData>` rows are spliced (values injected, style `s=` indices preserved from the cloned band row, formulas/merges/mergeCell/conditionalFormatting/dataValidation/dimension refs shifted by the row-count delta), and every other ZIP part (styles.xml, theme, drawings, charts, other sheets) is re-zipped byte-identical to the upload — verified by a unit test asserting exact byte equality against a real customer fixture (`docs/templates/COMPANY QUOTATION (1).xlsx`, which has JDL/IMILE/SHIPA/ARKAN/AKS sheets). Backend: `services/reports/xlsxTemplate/{splice,inspect,tripReportData,aliases,xmlRowSplice}.ts` (new deps `exceljs` read-only for inspection, `fflate` for the ZIP), new `ReportTemplate` Prisma model (file bytes + confirmed layout stored in Postgres, not the ephemeral `/tmp` uploads volume), new `/report-templates` routes (`inspect`, CRUD, `:id/preview`, `:id/generate`) gated by a new `company-reports` module key (with a seed backfill so the existing mercon.tech Settings row picks it up). Also fixed 3 underlying data bugs in `getCustomReport`/the new `fetchTripRows` service: date filtering on `createdAt` instead of the trip's own date, a hard 500-row cap, and `receiver` stubbed as `"Dropoff Stop N"` instead of the real `TripStop.location_name`. Frontend: new `CompanyReportsGeneratorPage.tsx` + `TemplateMappingEditor.tsx` (the human-confirms-the-mapping step the old page never had — `customMapping` existed in the engine but no UI ever passed it), `reportTemplateService.ts`. Wired as its own top-level sidebar item (not a Reports sub-tab) at `/company-reports`; `/reports/client-templates` redirects there. Removed `CompanyReportTemplatesPage.tsx` and `customTemplateExport.ts`. Hardened against two real customer files (a JDL trip-confirmation and an IMILE monthly invoice) which exposed four defects the synthetic fixture missed: (1) only the style-band row was replaced, so the customer's own sample rows survived underneath the generated data — `TemplateLayout` now carries an explicit, operator-editable `dataEndRow`; (2) shared formulas (`<f t="shared" si="N"/>`, which carry no formula text) cloned as empty cells, silently blanking the VAT/Total columns — formulas are now resolved against their master and emitted standalone; (3) a totals row's `SUM(M1:M37)` had its header-anchored start dragged down to `M14`, under-reporting the invoice total — row-shifting now distinguishes copy semantics (cloning, `$` pinned) from insert semantics (trailing rows, only refs at/below the block move); (4) the stale `calcChain.xml` cache was shipped pointing at moved cells, the usual cause of Excel's "we found a problem with some content" prompt — it is now dropped with its content-type override and relationship. Also fixed token substitution (banner tokens live in `sharedStrings.xml`, not inline in the sheet) and band detection (read a non-existent `style.id`). Verified: `npm run build` (root) succeeds; `npm run test:xlsx` (9 tests) passes; both real customer files generate 25-row reports that load cleanly in `openpyxl` (an implementation independent of the one used to read them) with **zero style mismatches** across every generated row, correct de-shared formulas, dates round-tripping as real dates, and UTF-8 (CJK) text intact; `prisma db push` + seed run twice against a local Postgres confirm the schema applies cleanly and the module-key backfill is idempotent; full API round-trip (login → inspect real fixture → create template → preview → generate) tested against a local server, output re-parses cleanly with ExcelJS. Added a rate-category filter (`Trip.rate_category`, e.g. "Trip", "Monthly Round", "Extra Trip/Round Trip") to preview/generate — real customer workbooks split trip-rate vs monthly-rate business into separate sheets (an IMILE file has an "extra" sheet), so a saved template needs to pull only its own slice rather than every trip for the customer. Verified end-to-end against a local Postgres: saved two independently-named templates ("IMILE Trip Monthly", "IMILE Extra") under the same customer with no collision, and each template's preview/generate correctly returns only its own rate-category slice of trips. · Same day, in parallel (**Smart Report Builder bugfixes**: post-review pass on the report builder found and fixed 3 defects — (1) `frontend/web-dashboard/src/router.tsx` referenced `moduleKey="locations"`/`"rate-cards"` on `<RequireModule>`, which no longer typecheck since a prior commit removed those two keys from `MODULE_KEYS` (they became always-on core infra) — **this broke `npm run build`**; fixed by dropping the two `RequireModule` wrappers to match the "core, not toggleable" intent. (2) `reportEngine/derived.ts`'s `tripIncome()` read `t.invoices[0]` unconditionally, but `reportEngine/resolver.ts` only includes the `invoices` relation when a spec explicitly references an `invoices.*` field — since `trips.revenue` is the default Values field in the Advanced Builder, every Quick Report preset, both landing-page templates, and the Ask Mercon parser, this crashed the query endpoint on the feature's own golden path; fixed by making `invoices` optional (`t.invoices?.[0]`). (3) `resolver.ts`'s row/column group-key join used a literal NUL byte as separator, making the file register as binary to tooling; replaced with `'||'`. **Not fixed in this pass** (flagged, needs an explicit decision): `requireModuleEnabled` was added to 7 previously-ungated route files (`reports`, `documents`, `folders`, `invoices`, `expenses`, `maintenance`, `trash`) in the same commit as the report builder work; `Settings.enabledModules` defaults to `[]` at the DB level and is only backfilled to the full list on **row creation** (`seed.ts`, `update: {}`) — if production's `Settings` singleton row already exists with an empty `enabledModules`, this 403s all 7 modules' APIs the moment it deploys. Needs a production data check (or an explicit backfill) before/after this lands, independent of the report builder itself. Built a complete, testable ad-hoc reporting system with a real backend Prisma query engine and drag-and-drop pivot table builder. **Backend**: `SavedReport` & `ScheduledReport` Prisma models (`npx prisma db push --accept-data-loss`), `reportEngine/schema.ts` (10 modules, canonical columns, join graph), `reportEngine/derived.ts` (shared `tripIncome`/`isEarned`), `reportEngine/resolver.ts` (`runReportQuery` with 400 injection guards, 1-hop relation include, JS group/agg/filter, KPI computation), `reportBuilderController.ts` & `reportBuilderRoutes.ts` with Zod validation (`reportQuerySpecBody`, `createSavedReportBody`, etc.) mounted at `/report-builder/*`. **Frontend**: `@dnd-kit/core` installed, `reportBuilderService.ts`, `askMerconParser.ts` (rule-based NL→spec parser), `ReportBuilderLandingPage.tsx` (top bar layout matching MERCON guidelines with scope pill `[ 🏢 MERCON Logistics ↕ ]` & category badge, 3 entry cards, 4 pre-built templates, Saved Reports & Scheduled Deliveries tabs), `QuickReportPage.tsx` (3-step wizard), `AdvancedBuilderPage.tsx` (3-panel workspace: expandable data panel, droppable structure zones for Rows/Columns/Values/Filters, auto-connect badge, FilterBuilder, live preview, KPI cards, Recharts view switcher: Table/Bar/Line/Pie/Area), `SaveReportModal.tsx`, `ScheduleReportModal.tsx`, CSV/Excel/PDF export. Mounted at `/report-builder` and registered in `Sidebar.tsx` under COMPLIANCE. Fixed a real bug found in passing: `authService.ts`'s dev-only mock-login fallback had no `import.meta.env.DEV` guard, so a 5xx/network error in production silently granted a fake client-side Admin session. **Web dashboard**: de-branding codemod converted ~700 hardcoded `#E8450F`-family Tailwind arbitrary values (`bg-[#E8450F]` etc., 102 files) to the `bg-brand`/`text-brand`/... tokens already registered in `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block, plus the remaining on-screen literals (spinners, map pins, chart fills) to `var(--color-brand)`; new `useBranding`/`useApplyBranding` hooks fetch `/settings/public` and write it to the CSS vars + `document.title` at boot (login page included, pre-auth); new `BrandLogo` component replaces hardcoded `<img src="/mercon-logo.png">` in Sidebar/LoginPage with a Settings-driven `logoUrl` (falls back to the existing PNGs); `Sidebar.tsx` nav items gained an optional `moduleKey` filtered against `Settings.enabledModules`; new `RequireSuperAdmin`/`RequireModule` route guards (same pattern as existing `RequireRole`) applied to the 8 optional-module routes; `SettingsPage.tsx`'s previously-decorative "Company Details" tab is now a real superadmin-only branding/module-toggle form. Left deliberately out of scope: Excel export brand color (`utils/exportUtils.ts`/`exportInvoiceExcel.ts`) and the folder-color-picker palette (`CreateFolderModal.tsx`/`KpiCard.tsx`) — neither is app branding paint. **Mobile app** (`frontend/mobile-app/mercon-app`): `app.json` replaced with `app.config.ts` (`CLIENT_PROFILES` keyed by `APP_CLIENT` env, defaults to `mercon` so nothing changes for the existing build); `src/lib/api.ts` no longer falls back to `https://mercon.tech/api` on a misconfigured build — now reads the per-client `extra.apiUrl` from `app.config.ts` and throws if neither that nor `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` is set; SecureStore keys (`TOKEN_KEY`/`SESSION_KEY`) namespaced by client slug so two clients' apps installed on one device can't collide (`auth-context.tsx`'s separate hardcoded `SESSION_KEY` literal removed in favor of importing api.ts's); `tailwind.config.js` reads the same `APP_CLIENT` env at build time. **Deploy**: `docker-compose.yml` parameterized (`CLIENT_NAME`/`POSTGRES_DB`/port vars, all defaulting to today's Mercon values — verified with `docker compose config` that nothing changes for the existing deploy, and the `pgdata` volume was deliberately left unnamed rather than risk pointing at a different volume than Docker auto-generated for Mercon's live data); added `nginx/client.conf.template` for provisioning a new client's Nginx config without touching the live `nginx/mercon-api.conf`. **Explicitly not done in this pass** (flagged as follow-ups, not attempted blind): `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml`'s tag-based/per-client-runner rework (recommended in the conversation, but it controls the only live production deploy and can't be tested here — needs the owner's review before it changes `git push` behavior); MTL's actual domain features (crane/subcontracted-vehicle tracking, "immediate trip" flow) — deliberately not designed without a scoping conversation with MTL, per Rule 0.
