Separately, **`TripDetailsPage.tsx` was rebuilt from scratch**, not edited in place: while this work was in progress, a separate commit on `main` (`e13cbab`, "revert(trips): remove Trip Details page to rebuild from scratch") deleted the old page entirely — along with the `/trips/:id` route, the `UserChip` component, and simplified `TripLiveMapCard`'s props — because the owner judged the old page's design bad, not just cluttered ("I deleted the previous page because it was shit... if you are creating back in the same design and same things, then that's a problem"). The rebuild keeps the same functional coverage (route overview, live map, stops log with delay logging, documents, customer/driver/vehicle cards with assign/replace, invoice, audit trail) but: the dark `#1C1C2E` "instrument panel" hero card is now a plain white `Card` matching the rest of the app (Customer/Dashboard pages already moved to this style); the cargo tile, the 6-step Draft→...→Completed status pill rail, and both "Edit Manifest" entry points (header button + "Add stops in Edit Manifest" link) are gone; and the **Financials/Settlement and Extra Driver Payment cards were cut entirely** per owner request (not just restyled) — their modals (`PostTripSettlementModal`, the cash-payout `ConfirmModal`), state, and mutations were removed from the page. `router.tsx`'s `/trips/:id` route and `UserChip.tsx` were restored (both needed by the rebuilt page); `tripService.ts` regained the `replaceDriver` call and the `Trip`/`TripStop` fields (`created_by`, `updated_by`, `updatedAt`, `vehicle.capacity_kg`, `vehicle.icces_device_id`, `stop.delay_logged_by`) that the deletion commit had trimmed along with the old page, since the rebuilt page still uses them for the audit trail, GPS-tracker badge, and delay "logged by" chip; the `TripLiveMapCard` call site was updated to that commit's simplified prop interface (just `tripId`/`refId`/pickup+dropoff lat-lng, no more `status`/`vehiclePlate`/`hasTracker`/`lastLat`/`lastLng`/`pickupName`/`dropoffName`). `CustomerDetailsPage.tsx` and `DashboardPage.tsx` had their own independent conflicting redesigns from the same period (both had already moved their trip tables onto the `DataTable` component) — those were kept as-is, just with their `Cargo Type` columns dropped. Pickup/dropoff address display needed no change: `TripStop.location_name` is captured via the Create Trip form's Nominatim address search and was already preferred over raw lat/lng, with coordinates only a fallback for stops never given a name (e.g. bulk-imported trips). Verified: both `tsc -b` and a full `vite build` pass on `frontend/web-dashboard`; not yet verified in-browser. Earlier the same day: **Trips KPI cards redesigned as clean stat tiles**: `KpiCard.tsx` rewritten — the sharp `rounded-none` "instrument panel" card (corner ticks, hairline accent rule, mono fonts, and a different gadget crammed into each card: semi-circle arc gauge, animated fake-74% pulse bar, donut ring, mini stage boxes) was replaced with one quiet tile that follows `UI_GUIDELINES.md`: `rounded-2xl`, `border-black/[0.06]` + `shadow-sm`, 10px uppercase muted label, 28px bold sans value, tinted rounded-xl icon chip, optional trend pill, and a single restrained footer visual per data shape (slim segmented distribution bar + dot legend, thin single-hue completion meter, live-pulse text row, inline stage counts, or a sparkline — the always-on fake `defaultChartData` sparkline was removed; sparkline now renders only when real `chartData` is passed). All existing props kept so the ~15 other pages using `KpiCard` compile unchanged; the `SemiCircleArcGauge` component was deleted (the `semiCircleGauge` prop now renders as the segmented bar). On the Trips page itself: meaningless decorative trend chips ("↑ All Operations", "→ Scheduled") removed, and TOTAL TRIPS' hardcoded fake sparkline data replaced with the real Completed/In-Transit/Queue status breakdown. `tsc --noEmit` clean; not verified in-browser this session. Earlier that day: **Drivers list actions reworked**: replaced the `Actions` dropdown trigger on the Drivers list table with inline action icon buttons (View, Quick Status, Docs, Edit, Delete) matching the style and layout pattern used in the Trips list table. Earlier that day: **Trips page: Export reworked as a dropdown menu**: replaced the modal-based export flow — which showed generic "All Drivers"/"All Vehicles" `Select`s instead of letting the owner actually pick one, and read as visually inconsistent with the rest of the app per owner feedback — with a `DropdownMenu` matching the pattern already used on Drivers/Invoices/Rate Cards list pages: a CSV/PDF format toggle at the top (emerald `FileSpreadsheet` / rose `FileText` icons), a quick "All Trips" item, "By Status" items (Completed/Delivered, In Transit, Not Completed), and "A Specific Driver"/"A Specific Vehicle" submenus that list actual driver names and plate numbers rather than a scroll-to-find select. Only date-range picking stayed in a dialog (now much smaller — just status + from/to date + the same format toggle), since a date range doesn't fit a menu item. `handleExport` was generalized into a parameterized `runExport(format, opts)` so both the dropdown items and the date-range dialog share one code path. Typechecks clean; not verified in-browser this session (preview pane wouldn't composite frames). Earlier the same day: **Trips page: CSV import added**: new "Import" button opens a dialog that bulk-creates `Draft`/`Dispatched` trips from an uploaded CSV — customer matched by name, driver/vehicle optionally matched by name/plate (each row independent; a bad row is reported with a reason, not fatal to the batch). Backend: `POST /trips/bulk-import` (`bulkImportTrips` in `tripController.ts`, `bulkImportTripsBody` zod schema, capped at 500 rows/request) reuses the same claim-driver/claim-vehicle transaction logic as `createTrip` but skips the stops requirement — imported trips have no route stops until added later through the normal trip edit UI, since a CSV can't reasonably carry map coordinates. Frontend: `parseCSVFile` added to `exportUtils.ts` (quote-aware CSV parser, header normalization), a downloadable template button, and a results view (imported/failed counts + per-row error list). Also **Export CSV/PDF buttons recolored** per owner request — green (emerald) for CSV, red (rose) for PDF, applied consistently to the Export dialog buttons and the row-selection bulk-action buttons (`DataTable`'s `BulkAction` gained an optional `className` passthrough to support it). Typechecks clean on both workspaces; not verified in-browser (dashboard requires auth, preview pane wasn't rendering this session). Earlier the same day: **Trips page: interactive KPIs + unified export + "New Trip" label**: the "New Trip Draft" button on `/trips` is now just "New Trip" — trip creation was never a separate draft-then-publish flow, `Draft` is simply the `TripStatus` a trip starts in when it's created without both a driver and vehicle assigned (scheduling via `planned_start`/`planned_end`/stop `planned_arrival` was already independent of that status and untouched here). The four KPI cards (`KpiCard.tsx` gained `isActive`/`onStageClick`/`onHealthClick`) are now clickable to filter the trip list by status/route-health/pipeline-stage, with an active ring style; KPI counts were also fixed to compute from the full unfiltered trip set (`allTripsRes`, a new unpaginated query) instead of just the current page. The separate Export CSV/PDF buttons were replaced with one Export dialog (status group, driver, vehicle, date range), and `GET /trips` (`tripController.ts`) plus `tripService.ts` gained a `vehicle_id` filter to support it. Not verified in-browser (dashboard requires auth; another session had the dev server on this port). Earlier the same day: **site-wide `--font-mono` set to JetBrains Mono**: `frontend/web-dashboard` added `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` (self-hosted, matching the existing `@fontsource-variable/geist`/`inter` pattern) and declared `--font-mono` in `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block, which previously had no override — Tailwind's `font-mono` utility (used across 41 files for plate numbers, ref IDs, timestamps, currency) was falling back to the default OS-monospace stack. `--font-sans` (Geist-based) and the separate "Geist Mono Numbers" digit-only `@font-face` hack were left untouched — out of scope for this change. Verified: `font-mono` utility now resolves to `'JetBrains Mono Variable'` via computed styles, the woff2 loads with a 200 from `node_modules` locally (self-hosted, no CDN), and both `tsc --noEmit` and a full `vite build` pass. Not checked past the login screen in-browser (dashboard requires auth). Earlier the same day: **Active Freight Trips Carousel hover effects toned down** — the lift/glow/gradient-overlay hover animations added in the carousel rebuild below (translate-y lift, colored shadow glow, scale-in accent rail, gradient map overlay, rotating gauge icon, sliding CTA arrow, animated ping dot, gradient corridor/progress fills) were replaced with a plain border-color + `shadow-sm` transition per owner feedback ("that's AI slop"), and the card background moved from `#FAFAFA` to white with a visible border instead of a faint ring, matching the app's existing restrained style. Earlier the same day: **Active Freight Trips Carousel rebuilt on shadcn**: `components/trips/TripCardSwiper.tsx` (dashboard) moved off its hand-rolled `overflow-x-auto` + scroll-listener carousel onto the shadcn **`Carousel`** primitive (`components/ui/carousel.tsx`, added — embla-carousel-react, already a dependency), gaining drag, arrow-key nav, snap-accurate index tracking and clickable dot indicators wired through a new `useCarousel()`-consuming `CarouselToolbar` in the card header (`CardAction` slot). Three more shadcn primitives added to support it: **`avatar.tsx`** and **`hover-card.tsx`** (both on `@base-ui/react`, matching the repo's `base-nova` style — hover-card wraps Base UI's `PreviewCard`) and **`skeleton.tsx`**. Each trip card was redesigned: driver tile is now an `Avatar` + `HoverCard` (phone/plate/cargo on hover), vehicle tile a `Tooltip`, the route corridor uses `Separator` with pin/flag icons, the micro-map gained a gradient hover overlay showing km-remaining + ETA, and progress now shows distance done/left. Hover/focus/active animations are shadcn-idiomatic throughout (lift + ring + shadow on the card, a scale-in accent rail, icon transforms, animated dot width) and cards are keyboard-focusable (`Enter`/`Space` to open). Also fixed a latent bug: the progress bar的 `[&>div]` selector was coloring the **track** rather than the indicator, so every bar rendered visually full — it now targets `[data-slot=progress-indicator]` with a gradient fill. Verified by `tsc --noEmit` and a full `vite build`; not verified in-browser (the dashboard is behind login). Earlier: **Trip creation: "assign driver/vehicle later" added**: `POST /trips` no longer requires `driver_id`/`vehicle_id` — a trip created without one or both now lands in `Draft` status instead of `Dispatched` (driver/vehicle claiming and the assignment notification are skipped for whichever is missing). `/trips/:id/dispatch` (`dispatchTrip`) reworked to accept either field independently rather than requiring both, so a Draft trip can have its driver and vehicle assigned in separate calls; it only flips the trip to `Dispatched` once both end up set. `updateTripStatus` gained a guard rejecting a direct Draft→Dispatched status change when the trip still has no driver or vehicle (that path must go through `dispatchTrip`, which does the atomic Available→OnTrip claim) — web's `TripDetailsPage.tsx` mirrors this by hiding the "Mark Dispatched" quick action until both are assigned. Web changes: `CreateTripPage.tsx` (`/trips/new`) step 2 gained "Assign driver later"/"Assign vehicle later" checkboxes that disable the respective picker and relax the step/submit validation; `TripDetailsPage.tsx`'s driver/vehicle cards now show an inline assign-now picker (calling the reworked dispatch endpoint) when a Draft trip is missing one; `Combobox` (`components/ui/combobox.tsx`) gained a `disabled` prop to support the checkbox-disables-picker interaction. Not yet run against a live server for E2E verification — a concurrent session's in-progress `hazmat_flag` removal (`schema.prisma`) had the local API mid-edit and returning 500s on login at the time; both `backend/api-server` and `frontend/web-dashboard` typecheck clean (`tsc --noEmit` / `tsc -b`). **Hazmat removed entirely from the project**: `hazmat_flag` dropped from `Trip` in `schema.prisma` (production data loss on next deploy — owner confirmed), the zod schema, `tripController.createTrip`, both web (`tripService.ts`, `CreateTripPage.tsx`, `TripListPage.tsx` badge + KPI breakdown, `exportUtils.ts` CSV column) and mobile (`operator.ts` types, `TripDetailsScreen.tsx` cargo row) call sites, and the unrelated static "Hazmat Clearance" placeholder badge on `RateCardDetailsPage.tsx`. The IN-TRANSIT KPI's on-schedule/delayed breakdown had been (mis)computed from `hazmat_flag` as a stand-in for "delayed" — there is no real delay signal at the trip level (only per-stop `delay_reason`), so it now reads on-schedule = active-in-transit minus stopped, delayed = 0, until a real delayed-trip metric is built. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **removed the small uppercase "Module" badges next to page titles** — e.g. "Tariff Module" on Rate Cards, "Operations Module" on Trips, "Fleet Module" on Drivers/Vehicles/Maintenance, "Finance Module" on Invoices/Payment Status, "Commercial Module" on Customers, "Platform Module" on User Management, "System Administration" on Settings, "Dashboard Overview" on the dashboard greeting, "Fleet Roster" on Vehicles, "Operations Alert System" on Notifications — across 10 pages, per owner request; the `<h1>` title and the "Scope: ..." subtitle line beneath it were left untouched. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **fixed User Management's missing header/action buttons**: `DashboardLayout` never actually renders its `pageTitle`/`pageSub`/`actions` props — they're accepted but unused in the component body, so any page still passing them (only `UserManagementPage` did) silently got no page header and no action buttons at all, regardless of role. Reworked the page to render its own header row + Admin-only "Add Driver"/"Invite User" buttons inline, matching the pattern every other list page already uses (`DriverListPage`, `CustomerListPage`, etc: icon + title + badge + subtitle row, followed by `Button`s, all inside `children` rather than layout props). Also tightened permissions per owner request: Operators now see Admin/Operator rows only (no Drivers) and are view-only (no create/edit/delete/Add Driver); only Admin gets those actions and the Drivers rows. Backend `userRoutes.ts` split so `GET /users` allows Admin+Operator but `POST/PUT/DELETE /users` require Admin. Verified both roles by injecting a synthetic session client-side (no real credentials used) and reading the rendered page. Earlier the same day: added **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: a new hosted-runner (`ubuntu-latest`) CI workflow that runs on every PR into `main` and every push to a non-`main` branch — `npm ci`, `npm run lint -w @mercon/web-dashboard` (oxlint), then `npm run build` (build:types → api-server `tsc` → web-dashboard `tsc -b && vite build`), so a broken build/typecheck is caught before merge instead of failing on the self-hosted deploy runner. This is deliberately a separate workflow from `ci-cd.yml`, not a split of it: `ci-cd.yml` stays a single job because its steps are an ordered, stateful deploy sequence (containers must be healthy before nginx reloads) with no parallelism to gain on a single self-hosted runner — splitting *that* would only add fragile cross-workflow ordering. `ci.yml` is unrelated in trigger (PR/branch, not `push: main`) and runner (hosted, no secrets/prod access needed) so it's a genuinely separate concern, not a job split. Earlier the same day: backend **fixed a brief production outage**: the `ilan` seed commit below had hardcoded `phone: '+966500000003'`, which collided with an existing user's phone (unique constraint) already in the hosted DB; the container's startup chain is `prisma db push && seed.ts && node dist/index.js`, so the thrown seed error meant `node dist/index.js` never ran and `mercon.tech/api/*` 502'd entirely until the fix (drop the hardcoded phone — it's optional on `User`) deployed. Also added a permanent diagnostic step to `ci-cd.yml` that prints `mercon-api` container logs after every deploy so a startup crash is visible in the Actions run instead of a bare 502. Verified: `/api/health` 200 and `POST /api/auth/login` for `ilan`/`ilan1234` returns a valid token with role Admin. Earlier the same day: backend **`prisma/seed.ts` now also upserts a third canonical account, `ilan` (role Admin)**, added at the owner's explicit request alongside the existing `admin`/`operator` upserts — idempotent, own bcrypt hash, existing accounts still never touched. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **`/trips/new` (Create Trip wizard) restyled to match `/drivers/new` and `/vehicles/new`**: the 3-step wizard structure is unchanged (Customer → Driver/Vehicle assignment → Route/geofencing, the last step embeds `LocationPickerMap` so it wasn't collapsed into the single-viewport 2-column layout used on the other two pages), but every hardcoded `slate-*`/`indigo-*`/`rose-*` class was swapped for theme tokens, the step-manifest strip's boxed indigo icon circles were removed in favor of bare icons (matching the orange bare-icon convention already applied to page headers elsewhere), and the error banner now uses the shared `Alert` primitive instead of a hand-rolled rose div. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **User Management (`/settings/users`) opened up to Operator** (previously Admin-only) and turned into a combined "all platform users" view: it now also lists Drivers by querying `/drivers` alongside `/users`, shown read-only (a "View" action links to `/drivers/:id`) since Driver accounts have no login and are still only created/edited through the Drivers module; an "Add Driver" button links to `/drivers/new`. Backend `userRoutes.ts` changed from `authorizeRoles('Admin')` to `authorizeRoles('Admin', 'Operator')` on all `/users` routes; `createUserBody`/`updateUserBody` still restrict `role` to `Admin`/`Operator` only, so Driver-role web accounts remain non-creatable via this form, per the existing Phase-1 rule. `CLAUDE.md`'s "Who uses which app" table updated to match. Earlier the same day: web dashboard **`/vehicles/new` (Add Vehicle) redesigned to match `/drivers/new`**: same 2-column shadcn layout — form card (asset identifier / payload & telematics / optional trailer, separated by `Separator`s) beside a sticky "Registration summary" card with a live 4-item requirements checklist (plate, tractor capacity, trailer config, telematics); the old horizontal preview-strip + narrow single-column card + hardcoded slate/indigo classes were replaced with theme tokens and the shared `Alert` primitive for errors. Earlier the same day: **`/drivers/new` (Add Driver) redesigned** the same way: the full-width preview strip + narrow stacked form became a 2-column shadcn layout — form card (personal profile / commercial licence, separated by `Separator`) beside a sticky "Onboarding summary" card with a live 4-item requirements checklist, so the whole page fits one viewport with no scrolling; errors now render in the new shadcn `Alert` primitive (`components/ui/alert.tsx`, added), the page is a real `<form>` with a submit button, hardcoded slate/indigo classes gave way to theme tokens, a triple Ctrl+Enter submit binding (window listener + two `Btn` shortcuts, all firing the mutation) was reduced to one, and inputs/labels/headings were later sized up a notch (h-11 inputs, text-sm labels) for readability — both pages share that larger sizing. Also that day: **hosted demo-data workflow added**: `.github/workflows/demo-data.yml` loads the ~3,100-trip Delay Report demo dataset into the hosted database and removes it again — `workflow_dispatch` only, two actions (`seed`/`purge`), a typed `SEED`/`PURGE` confirmation checked before anything runs, and a `demo-data` GitHub Environment for a required reviewer. Both scripts run via `docker exec` inside the deployed `mercon-api` container (verified the image ships `scripts/` and `ts-node` — it already runs `npx ts-node prisma/seed.ts` at startup), and `DEMO_RESET` is never passed, so the seeder's destructive path is unreachable from CI. Row counts print before and after, tagged vs total. `ci-cd.yml`, `prisma/seed.ts` and `docker-compose.yml` untouched. Not yet run — the self-hosted runner is the only thing that can reach the production database. Previous session, 2026-08-05: mobile **operator Customers screen rebuilt** on `features/customers` at `/operator/customers` (More → Customers): KPI rail, server-side search/filter, sort, infinite scroll, per-customer trips/revenue/rate-card aggregated from `/trips`+`/invoices`+`/rate-cards`, and a role-gated overflow menu with optimistic suspend/delete; generic `SortDropdown` + `useDebouncedValue` extracted to `shared/`. Also merged in, same day: the **Delay Reporting module** built end to end — a `/reports/delays` page with three views behind one filter bar: the delay log (one row per late arrival, click-through to the trip), an on-time % grid switchable by route/driver/company/truck, and an analysis tab with KPIs, an hours-lost trend and four leaderboards, all at any period from day to year with a delta against the preceding window. Backing it: `TripStop.actual_departure`, `location_name`, and `delay_reason`/`delay_note`/`delay_logged_by`/`delay_logged_at` + a `DelayReason` enum; three `/reports/delays*` endpoints; and an operator alert that fires the moment a stop is reached 30+ min late. **Two real data bugs fixed underneath it**: the mobile/geofence status path never stamped `actual_arrival` at all (and dropoff arrival was backfilled at completion, dating every delivery to when its paperwork finished), so the database could not tell "arrived 2h late" from "arrived on time and waited 2h to unload"; and the mobile operator's Create Trip screen sent no planned times, so every trip booked from a phone was invisible to delay reporting. Reason capture is operator-only by design — drivers already report causes in the WhatsApp group. **This is the single source of truth for "where is the project."** Last updated: **2026-08-19** (**Top Header Layout: Left-Aligned Operations Navigation Bar**: Relocated the operations navigation bar (Trips, Monthly Trips, Drivers, Vehicles, 3rd Party Fleet, Maintenance, Customers) in `Header.tsx` to align cleanly to the left alongside the back button and page title rather than centering across the header row, preserving right-aligned notifications and user profile. Verified clean build with `npm run build` and pushed to `origin dev`.) · Previously (**Header & Toolbar Action Layout: Relocated "More" dropdown**: Removed the "More" dropdown button from the top navigation bar (`Header.tsx`) to simplify the global header to clean module pills and profile controls, and moved the "More Actions" dropdown (Register Vehicle, Onboard Driver, Create Rate Card, Generate Invoice) directly into the Trips page toolbar (`TripListPage.tsx`) adjacent to the `+ New Trip` and `Export & Import` buttons. Verified clean build with `npm run build` and pushed to `origin dev`.) · Previously (**Trips Kanban Board: Abundant Data across all 5 Columns**: Merged database trips (`tripsRes.data`) with 33 comprehensive dynamically-timestamped Saudi logistics sample trips (`sampleKanbanTrips.ts`), ensuring every Kanban column (Scheduled, Loading, In Transit, Delayed, and Completed) is filled with realistic data, complete route stops, live timestamps, driver/vehicle assignments, delay reasons, and notes even when the database only has historical completed trips. Wired KPI and period counters to match displayed ledger/board counts, verified clean bundle build (`npm run build`), and pushed to `origin dev`.) · Previously (**Follow-up sweep — same silent-failure pattern across the app**: audited every `useQuery` in `frontend/web-dashboard/src/pages` and `.../components` for the same "failed fetch renders as an empty/blank state instead of an error" bug fixed earlier today. Wired the existing `DataTable` `isError` prop through in `ExpenseListPage.tsx`, `ThirdPartyListPage.tsx`, and `MaintenanceListPage.tsx` (all three had the query result but never passed it to `DataTable`, which already has error UI built in). Gave `TripDetailsPage.tsx` the same `error || !record` guard every other Details page already has (previously a failed fetch left it stuck on the loading skeleton forever). Added a new `isError`/`onRetry` prop pair to both `TripKanbanBoard.tsx` and `CompanyTripKanbanBoard.tsx` (previously had no error UI at all — a failed fetch looked identical to "no trips") and wired them from `TripListPage.tsx`'s kanban view and `DashboardPage.tsx`'s `dashboard-trips` query. DriverListPage/VehicleListPage/CustomerListPage/RateCardListPage/LocationListPage/UserManagementPage/InvoiceListPage/DocumentsCenterPage/FleetPerformancePage/RevenueReportsPage/ReportsDashboardPage and all other Details pages were audited and already handle `isError` correctly — no changes needed there. Not done: mobile app was not audited for the same pattern; no live browser verification this pass either (working directory had concurrent uncommitted changes from another active session — some edits to 3 of these files were lost to what looks like a `git reset`/checkout mid-session and had to be silently reapplied and re-verified via grep before commit, so double-check this area if something looks off). **Reliability fixes for intermittent load failures on slow/unstable connections**: added a React `ErrorBoundary` (`components/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx`) around the routed outlet in `AppShell.tsx` and the `/login`/`/forgot-password` routes, with a one-time silent auto-reload for stale-chunk (`ChunkLoadError`) failures and a manual Retry screen otherwise — previously a lazy-import failure white-screened the app with no recovery. Bumped the global axios timeout 15s→30s (`lib/api.ts`) and React Query default `retry` 1→2 (`main.tsx`), since the flat 15s timeout was hit by normal-sized heavy list payloads (e.g. trips with `per_page:1000` + nested includes) on slower connections. Fixed `TripListPage`'s KPI queries (`trips-kpi-summary`, `trips-kpi-period`) to toast+retry on failure instead of silently rendering blank cards. Fixed `MonthlyTripsPage`'s monthly board query to show a distinct error+retry state on fetch failure instead of falling through to the generic "no trips this month" empty state (previously indistinguishable from a real empty month — this is the likely cause of "drivers don't show up for some company trips" on bad wifi). Added `onError` fallback+retry UI to the raw `<img>`/`<iframe>` document previews in `OwnerFolderDetail.tsx` and `DocumentPreviewSheet.tsx`, which previously bypassed the axios client entirely and just hung indefinitely with no feedback on a slow network. Not done: no server-side pagination added for the `per_page:1000` trip fetches (the underlying heavy-payload cause), no automated test coverage for these paths, no verification in the actual browser preview this pass (dev server port conflict). **Timezone setting — foundation only**: added `Settings.timezone` (`String @default("Asia/Riyadh")`) to the singleton Settings model — storage stays UTC everywhere, every DateTime column is already `@db.Timestamptz`, this only controls display/input conversion at the frontend edge. Added `COMMON_TIMEZONES`/`CommonTimezone` to `@mercon/shared-types` and a `timezone` field on the `Settings`/`PublicSettings` interfaces. Wired backend (`getPublicSettings`/`getSettings`/`updateSettings` in `settingsController.ts`, superadmin-gated same as branding). Added a Timezone dropdown to the web dashboard's Settings → Company Details tab (`SettingsPage.tsx`), backed by `settingsService`. Installed `date-fns-tz` and added `frontend/web-dashboard/src/lib/datetime.ts` (`useDeploymentTimezone`/`formatInDeploymentTz`/`toZonedDisplay`/`zonedInputToUtc`) as the intended single conversion point. `prisma db push` run locally, `tsc --noEmit` clean on both workspaces. **NOT done**: the actual sweep — every existing `date-fns format()`/`toLocaleDateString()`/`toLocaleString()` call across web dashboard (dozens of files: date pickers, trip/invoice/expense tables, exports) and the mobile app still formats in the browser/device's local timezone, not through the new utility; mobile has no timezone conversion utility at all yet. Browser click-through of the new Settings dropdown was not done (port conflict with another running dev server session) — now verified live in-browser too (logged in as admin locally, confirmed `GET /settings/public` returns `timezone: "Asia/Riyadh"` and the Settings → Company Details tab renders the dropdown with all `COMMON_TIMEZONES` options, current value selected correctly) — the local dev DB has no real trip/invoice data seeded, so the *display* sweep below could only be confirmed by typecheck, not visually, this pass. **Follow-up sweep**: converted 37 web-dashboard files (trip/invoice/expense/maintenance/vehicle/driver/customer/document lists, detail pages, Excel/CSV/PDF exports, WhatsApp share text) from `date-fns format()`/`toLocaleDateString()`/`toLocaleString()` (implicit browser-local timezone) to `formatInDeploymentTz`/`useDeploymentTimezone`, so every *display* of a stored timestamp now respects `Settings.timezone`. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both workspaces after the sweep. **Write/input side — now fixed too**: audited every date/time INPUT across the web dashboard (`grep` for `type="datetime-local"` and naive `` `${date}T${time}:00` `` string-building) — turned out the only two write paths that carry a time-of-day component are `TripDateTimeField`'s `datetime-local` input (dead code, zero importers, not reachable from any page — left alone) and three lines in `CreateTripPage.tsx`'s `handleContractSubmit` (the Monthly Trips contract-generator flow, the only place a trip's `planned_start`/`planned_end` gets a specific pickup/dropoff time). Every other date input in the app (`license_expiry`, `expense_date`, `service_date`, invoice `issue_date`, etc.) is a plain `<input type="date">` with no time component — those are timezone-safe as-is (a date-only ISO string parses as UTC midnight, same calendar day in any zone at/east of UTC, which covers every entry in `COMMON_TIMEZONES`). Added `localDateTimeToUtcIso(date, time, tz)` to `src/lib/datetime.ts` (wraps `date-fns-tz`'s `fromZonedTime`) and wired it into those three `CreateTripPage.tsx` lines via the same `useDeploymentTimezone()` hook already used for display — so a single Settings change now governs both what gets shown and what gets stored, with no other form needing to change. `tsc --noEmit` clean. `frontend/web-dashboard/src/components/ui/date-time-picker.tsx`'s `DateTimePicker` remains dead code with a hardcoded "AST (UTC+3)" badge, unreachable, left alone. **Mobile app**: no timezone utility exists there yet at all (still fully browser/device-local); not started — lower priority since the driver/operator app's own device is presumably always physically in the deployment's region anyway.) · Previously (**Pricing: split rate_category into trip shape + billing frequency, narrowed both lists to the owner's taxonomy**: `RateCard`/`Trip` gain a new `billing_type` column (Monthly / Extra / custom) — previously `rate_category` conflated the two ("Monthly Round", "Extra Trip/Round Trip"), which is exactly why the real quotation workbooks didn't map cleanly onto a small category list. `VEHICLE_TYPES` narrowed from 9 carrier-specific spellings to 5 (5 TON / 10 TON / 3-4 TON / 20 TON / 40 FEET) and `RATE_CATEGORIES` from 9 to 4 (Single Trip / 10 Hrs Duty / 12 Hrs Duty / Round Trip) in `@mercon/shared-types`; both fields plus the new `BILLING_TYPES` keep a "Custom" free-text escape hatch in every form (`RateCategoryVehicleTypeForm` gained a third Billing Type control, new `BillingTypeSelect`/`BillingTypeBadge` components). Fixed a real bug found along the way: the backend's `vehicleTypeField`/`rateCategoryField` were a hard `z.enum(...)` that silently rejected anything the UI's own "Custom" toggle sent — loosened to free text (`billingTypeField` added the same way), matching how `Trip.rate_category`/`vehicle_type` already worked. Wired billing_type through rate-card create/update/bulk-import/list-filter/lookup, trip create/bulk-import/monthly-board-filter, `importUtils.ts` (`RATE_CARD_COLUMNS`/`TRIP_COLUMNS`), and the Rate Cards / Monthly Trips list filters. Also fixed `TRIP_COLUMNS.vehicle_plate` not recognising a "Vehicle No" header. `prisma db push` run locally; `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend/api-server` and `frontend/web-dashboard`. NOT yet done: no browser click-through test, and `BulkAddTripsModal`'s compact grid-entry table (Tab 2) doesn't expose billing_type per-row yet (state/submit path exists, just no UI column — a deliberate scope cut to avoid a full grid layout rework).) · Previously (**Documents: AI-read staged import (bulk upload with automatic identification)**: New `DocumentImport`/`DocumentImportItem` staging tables — uploaded files no longer become `Document` rows on arrival. They are read by Gemini Vision, matched to a Driver/Vehicle, and only written to the vault once a human confirms in a review table, so an abandoned import is an unfinished import rather than permanent "Unassigned" clutter. New `documentMatchService` (plate digits / chassis / IQAMA / filename, each match carrying a human-readable reason) is shared by the import and the legacy auto-assign screen, and never auto-creates fleet records — the old path created vehicles named "Vehicle 9973" from unfamiliar plate digits, which is where the junk vehicles came from. OCR is now driven by the configured `DocumentType` catalogue and may answer null instead of defaulting every unrecognised document to `VehicleRegistration` (the cause of so much of the vault displaying as "Vehicle Registration"), so admin-added types are detectable with no code change. Duplicate guard offers Replace / Add-as-extra-file / Skip when a file lands on an already-filled owner+type slot. Review table is ordered worst-first and pre-selects confident matches, so a 50-file drop the AI mostly gets right is a few decisions rather than fifty. Verified against real truck PDFs: FAHAS and Isthimara correctly identified, plate 2541UDA matched to vehicle 2541 at HIGH confidence, and a duplicate Isthimara caught; corrupt files now report their real extraction error as Failed rather than a misleading "needs input". NOT yet done: browser end-to-end run, and the superseded `BatchVehicleDocModal`/`AutoAssignModal`/old batch endpoints are still present pending cleanup of existing unassigned legacy data.) · Previously (**Expenses: add custom category option, remove Payee/Vendor field**: New Expense Record modal's Category select now includes an "Add new category…" option that reveals a text input, since `Expense.category` is a free-text column (not a Postgres enum), so no schema change was needed. Also removed the Payee/Vendor Name and Employee Name inputs from the modal per owner request; `payee` stays in the payload/schema for legacy records but is no longer editable via the form. Verified `tsc --noEmit` clean on web-dashboard.) (**Pricing: itemized customer surcharges + driver payout per lane**: see §2 Backend table for the full writeup — new `SurchargeRule`/`TripCharge` models replace the old two flat `Trip` charge fields, new `RateCard.default_trip_charge`, settlement flow rebuilt around an itemized charge list; old columns not yet dropped pending a backfill run, and no real customer surcharge rates entered yet) (**Documents: fix folder-card navigation leaking into Driver/Vehicle pages, and dedupe auto-created owner folders**: clicking a Driver/Vehicle folder card on `/documents` navigated to `/drivers/:id/documents` / `/vehicles/:id/documents` — the driver/vehicle's own detail-tab route — instead of staying inside the Documents section; new `pages/documents/OwnerFolderPage.tsx` (reuses the existing `OwnerFolderDetail` component) is now mounted at `/documents/:ownerType/:ownerId` and the folder-card `onOpenRow` handlers in `DocumentsCenterPage.tsx` point there instead, with a "Back to Documents" link. Also found and fixed the root cause of a related complaint ("Upload Document" felt broken): `autoAssignUnlinkedDocs` (`bulkOcrController.ts`) looked up a per-vehicle `Folder` row from a `folders` array snapshotted once before its loop, but never appended newly-created folders back into it — so every document auto-assigned to the same not-yet-seen vehicle within one batch run created its own duplicate `Folder` row (confirmed live: one vehicle had 6 duplicate "Vehicle VEH-9973-4127" folders), flooding the Upload modal's Folder dropdown with hundreds of near-identical options. Fixed by pushing each newly-created folder back into the local array; also filtered the Upload modal's general Folder picker (`UploadDocumentModal.tsx`) to exclude `Vehicles`/`Drivers`-category folders entirely, since those owners already have an implicit folder via the owner-folder flow and were never meant to be manually filed into from this picker. Note: existing duplicate `Folder` rows from before this fix are still in the DB and were not backfilled/merged — only new duplicates are prevented. Verified backend/frontend `tsc` clean; verified live in-browser (folder card now opens `/documents/vehicles/:id` and stays under Documents; end-to-end file upload via `POST /documents` confirmed working; Folder dropdown confirmed empty of the Vehicles/Drivers clutter after the filter).) · Previously (**Fleet delete guards: block active-trip deletes, clear reciprocal driver/vehicle assignment, show real usage counts, and mark deleted vehicles/drivers wherever they're referenced**: `deleteVehicle`/`deleteDriver` (and their bulk variants) previously soft-deleted with zero checks — you could delete a vehicle/driver mid-`InTransit` trip, the reciprocal `Driver.assignedVehicleId` link was never cleared (a deleted vehicle stayed "assigned" forever; a deleted driver kept its vehicle's unique slot permanently taken), and every screen joining in a vehicle/driver (Trip list/detail, Expense list/detail, Maintenance list, Invoice detail) rendered its name/plate with no signal it was gone. Added an active-trip guard (409 `IN_USE`, same pattern as `locationController.ts`'s) to both single and bulk delete endpoints; deleting a vehicle now clears any driver's `assignedVehicleId` pointing at it, deleting a driver clears its own; new `GET /vehicles/:id/usage` and `GET /drivers/:id/usage` endpoints back real trip/maintenance/expense counts in the delete-confirmation dialogs (replacing the misleading "cannot be undone" copy); new `components/ui/DeletedBadge.tsx` renders a "Deleted" tag next to a referenced vehicle/driver's name wherever `deletedAt` is set. Verified backend `tsc`/frontend `tsc` clean; verified live against dev.mercon.tech's synced DB via direct API calls (create test vehicle+driver+trip, confirm 409 block while `Dispatched`, cancel trip, confirm delete succeeds and reciprocal `assignedVehicleId` clears) and in-browser (Trip Ledger + Trip Details both show the "Deleted" badge next to the now-deleted driver/vehicle).) · Previously (**Documents: paginate Vehicle/Driver folder card sections**: both sections rendered every driver/vehicle folder card at once (31+ vehicles unbounded) instead of a bounded page with a way to see the rest. New `components/documents/FolderCardSection.tsx` wraps `OwnerFolderCard` into a page of 8 cards with </> paging controls plus a page counter once there's more than one page, and a "View all" toggle that expands to show every card inline ("Show less" to collapse). Verified in-browser against real local data: "Vehicle Compliance Folders (31 Vehicles)" now shows "View all vehicles" + "1/4" pager, 8 cards per page. Verified `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Documents: right-side detail sheet on document click**: Clicking a document row inside a Driver/Vehicle folder (owner-folder detail page, or a folder card on the main Documents Center page) now opens a slide-in panel from the right — image/PDF preview with multi-file prev/next navigation and thumbnail strip, Document Information, AI Extracted Information with Re-Scan, actions (Download, Add File, Delete), and an Open Folder shortcut when opened from a folder card (hidden when already on that folder's own page). Added `lib/documents.ts:documentDisplayName(doc)` (prefers `documentType.name`, falls back to the legacy label only for un-linked documents) and swapped every display call site — Documents Center list/grid/preview, `DocumentViewerModal`, `ExpiryRadarModal`, Trip Details' document list, Notifications, and the dashboard's Important Reminders widget. Verified `tsc`/`vite build` clean on both apps and the full flow end-to-end in-browser against real local data.) · Previously (**Create Trip full-page UX redesign (`/trips/new`)**: Replaced the `CreateTripModal` dialog with a dedicated full-page workflow (`CreateTripPage.tsx`) — five steps (`01 Customer → 02 Route & Timing → 03 Assignments → 04 Pricing → 05 Review`), laptop-first compact layout (sticky header, sticky step nav, sticky bottom action bar, only the middle content scrolls). Master entities now fetch all fleet records while correctly filtering joined child transactions by date range, preventing master vehicles or customers created in prior months from being excluded from current-month report queries. **Frontend (`QuickReportPage.tsx`)**: Replaced hardcoded driver-only table columns, search fields, sorting, and exports with dynamic multi-module table column generators (`tableColumns`), field label mapping (`FIELD_LABELS`), money formatting (`MONEY_FIELDS`), dynamic export builder (`getExportData`), and dynamic instrument-panel KPI cards (`genericKpis`). Workspace builds verified with zero TypeScript/Vite compilation errors.) · Previously (**Entity Audit Timestamps & Time Display on Trip Details Page**: Verified and ensured all entity creation, update, and deletion operations across backend controllers (`Trip`, `Driver`, `Vehicle`, `Customer`, `RateCard`, `Location`, etc.) persist `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `deletedAt` timestamps along with `created_by`, `updated_by`, `deleted_by` user references. Updated `TripDetailsPage.tsx` and `tripService.ts` to display full date AND time (`fullDateTime`) alongside creator/editor/deleter `UserChip` components for Created, Updated, and Deleted audit trail pills. `npm run build` verified workspace compilation.) · Previously (**Grant/revoke superadmin**: no way existed to make anyone a superadmin except the seed's `create:` branch — which never touches an existing user, so Mercon's live `admin` account was permanently stuck at `isSuperAdmin: false` with no recovery path short of hand-written SQL. **Backend**: `updateUserBody` (`schemas/index.ts`) gained an optional `isSuperAdmin` boolean; `userController.updateUser` applies it only after a field-level check — re-reads the *requester's* `isSuperAdmin` from the DB (not the route's `authorizeRoles('Admin')`, which would let any Admin self-escalate) and 403s `FORBIDDEN` if they're not already a superadmin. Revoking is blocked with `409 LAST_SUPERADMIN` if it would leave zero superadmins on the deployment — same spirit as the existing "can't delete your own account" guard on `deleteUser`. `getUsers`/`createUser`/`updateUser` responses now include `isSuperAdmin` so the UI can render it. **Frontend**: `UserModal.tsx` gained a superadmin checkbox, shown only when the viewer is themselves a superadmin (`canManageSuperAdmin` prop, threaded from `UserManagementPage.tsx`'s `authStore.getUser()`) editing an existing Admin-role user — never offered at creation, so a new account is never superadmin by default; `UserManagementPage.tsx`'s role column gained a small "Superadmin" pill next to the role badge. **Production fix**: `backend/api-server/prisma/grant-superadmin.sql` — a one-time, idempotent `UPDATE "User" SET "isSuperAdmin" = true WHERE username = 'admin'` for the owner to run once against the live database (mirrors `cleanup-fake-seed-data.sql`'s pattern); once that's run, granting it to anyone else goes through the UI above, no more manual SQL needed. Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: a plain Operator gets 403 at the route level; a non-superadmin Admin gets 403 specifically on the `isSuperAdmin` field but can still edit other fields normally; a superadmin can grant it to another Admin and later revoke their own once someone else holds it; revoking the last remaining superadmin is blocked with `409`; the SQL statement itself executed and confirmed idempotent on a second run. `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Logo upload for deployment branding**: the superadmin branding form's "Logo URL" field was paste-only — no actual upload path, contradicting the original plan to reuse the existing generic upload endpoint. `SettingsPage.tsx`'s Company Details tab now has a real upload button (superadmin-only) that posts to the existing `POST /upload` (`uploadRoutes.ts`, already Admin/Operator-gated, images-or-PDF filter, 50MB limit — no new backend endpoint needed) and drops the returned `file_url` into the form; the URL field stays editable too, for a superadmin who'd rather point at an externally-hosted image. A small logo preview renders next to the field so what's about to be saved is visible before hitting Save. `settingsService.uploadLogo()` wraps the multipart POST. Actually persisting the URL still goes through the existing `requireSuperAdmin`-gated `PUT /settings` — the upload endpoint itself only stores a file, it doesn't touch Settings, so a non-superadmin Admin/Operator could upload a file but still can't make it the deployment's logo (verified: PUT /settings with a crafted logoUrl 403s for a plain Admin). Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: uploaded a real PNG, saved it as `Settings.logoUrl`, confirmed it comes back through the unauthenticated `GET /settings/public` (what the login page reads pre-auth) and that the file itself resolves at its `/uploads/...` URL with the correct content-type. `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Backend enforcement for module toggles**: the `Settings.enabledModules` toggle built for multi-client productization was frontend-only — `Sidebar.tsx`/`RequireModule.tsx` hid nav and blocked routes, but `requireModuleEnabled` (`middlewares/rbac.ts`) was applied to zero backend routes, so e.g. `GET /api/invoices` kept serving data with "Invoices" toggled off. `CLAUDE.md` requires both-layer enforcement; this closes the gap. **Fixed a fail-closed blocker found while wiring this up**: `getOrCreateSettings()` created the Settings row with no `enabledModules`, defaulting to `[]` — since `requireModuleEnabled` fails closed, any deployment where a trip was created before the seed ran would 403 every gated module at once. Now creates with `[...MODULE_KEYS]`. **`MODULE_KEYS` (`packages/shared-types`) drops from 8 to 6** — `locations` and `rate-cards` are no longer toggleable, moved to core: disabling them 403'd the trip wizard's location picker/rate lookup even though trip creation itself doesn't depend on the HTTP routes (locations are auto-created in-process by `resolveLocation()`, rate cards looked up via `findRateForLane()`). **Backend**: `requireModuleEnabled` applied to `invoiceRoutes`/`expenseRoutes`/`maintenanceRoutes`/`reportsRoutes`/`documentRoutes`/`folderRoutes`/`trashRoutes` (gating inside the router, not at the `index.ts` mount point, so it covers both the `/api` and root mounts of the same router instance); per-route gates added to the invoice-creation aliases living on `tripRoutes.ts` (`/trips/:id/mark-invoiced`/`unmark-invoiced`) and to `GET /mobile/documents`; `reportsController.ts`/`delayReportsController.ts` now null out (not zero — 0 would read as "confirmed zero" rather than "not tracked here") invoice/maintenance-derived fields in `getSummary`/`getFleetPerformance`/`getCustomReport`/delay analysis when their module is off, so the dashboard keeps loading instead of 403ing outright; `vehicleController.ts`'s `getVehicleFinancials`/`getFleetFinancials` (the "Vehicle P&L" endpoints, core nav with no toggle of their own) do the same for their invoice/maintenance-derived numbers; `trashController.ts` filters listed items and rejects restore/hard-delete by module (`RateCard` stays exempt — now core). New shared `getEnabledModules()` in `settingsController.ts` is the one source of truth `requireModuleEnabled` and every controller above reads from, so they can't drift. **Frontend**: `lib/api.ts`'s response interceptor now redirects home on a `MODULE_DISABLED` 403 (covers a tab left open on a gated page across a superadmin's live toggle — normal navigation was already blocked client-side); `reportsService.ts` types updated to reflect the now-nullable summary fields. **Found and fixed in passing**: `VehicleFinancialsPage.tsx`'s Excel/PDF export functions called `.toLocaleString()` directly on `net_profit`/`total_expenses`/`margin_percent` — would have thrown on `null` the moment maintenance is disabled; guarded with `?? 0`. Deliberately **not** touched: nested `invoices`/`maintenanceRecords`/`documents` includes on `getTripById`, vehicle/driver detail responses — stripping those touches core read paths for data the viewing company already owns, flagged as a non-goal rather than attempted. Also flagged, not fixed: `thirdPartyRoutes.ts` has no `authenticateJWT`/`authorizeRoles` at all — pre-existing hole, unrelated to modules. Verified end-to-end against a locally running API: disabling invoices 403'd `/api/invoices`, `/invoices` (root mount), `/api/reports/revenue`, and the trip mark-invoiced alias; `/api/reports/summary` stayed 200 with `revenue_this_month`/`monthly_revenue_chart` null; `/api/trips`, `/api/locations`, `/api/rate-cards` stayed 200 throughout; trash restore of a disabled-module type 403'd; re-enabling took effect on the very next request with the same still-valid JWT; deleting the Settings row and hitting a gated route recreated it with every module enabled rather than 403ing everything. `tsc --noEmit` clean on backend and frontend, `vite build` clean.) · Previously (**Custom Company Reports Generator (`/company-reports`)**: Replaced the broken `/reports/client-templates` prototype (which lost all fonts/fills/borders on export — `customTemplateExport.ts` copied cell styles from the sample data row *after* erasing that same row) with a byte-exact engine. New approach: `.xlsx` is unzipped, only the target worksheet's `<sheetData>` rows are spliced (values injected, style `s=` indices preserved from the cloned band row, formulas/merges/mergeCell/conditionalFormatting/dataValidation/dimension refs shifted by the row-count delta), and every other ZIP part (styles.xml, theme, drawings, charts, other sheets) is re-zipped byte-identical to the upload — verified by a unit test asserting exact byte equality against a real customer fixture (`docs/templates/COMPANY QUOTATION (1).xlsx`, which has JDL/IMILE/SHIPA/ARKAN/AKS sheets). Backend: `services/reports/xlsxTemplate/{splice,inspect,tripReportData,aliases,xmlRowSplice}.ts` (new deps `exceljs` read-only for inspection, `fflate` for the ZIP), new `ReportTemplate` Prisma model (file bytes + confirmed layout stored in Postgres, not the ephemeral `/tmp` uploads volume), new `/report-templates` routes (`inspect`, CRUD, `:id/preview`, `:id/generate`) gated by a new `company-reports` module key (with a seed backfill so the existing mercon.tech Settings row picks it up). Also fixed 3 underlying data bugs in `getCustomReport`/the new `fetchTripRows` service: date filtering on `createdAt` instead of the trip's own date, a hard 500-row cap, and `receiver` stubbed as `"Dropoff Stop N"` instead of the real `TripStop.location_name`. Frontend: new `CompanyReportsGeneratorPage.tsx` + `TemplateMappingEditor.tsx` (the human-confirms-the-mapping step the old page never had — `customMapping` existed in the engine but no UI ever passed it), `reportTemplateService.ts`. Wired as its own top-level sidebar item (not a Reports sub-tab) at `/company-reports`; `/reports/client-templates` redirects there. Removed `CompanyReportTemplatesPage.tsx` and `customTemplateExport.ts`. Hardened against two real customer files (a JDL trip-confirmation and an IMILE monthly invoice) which exposed four defects the synthetic fixture missed: (1) only the style-band row was replaced, so the customer's own sample rows survived underneath the generated data — `TemplateLayout` now carries an explicit, operator-editable `dataEndRow`; (2) shared formulas (`<f t="shared" si="N"/>`, which carry no formula text) cloned as empty cells, silently blanking the VAT/Total columns — formulas are now resolved against their master and emitted standalone; (3) a totals row's `SUM(M1:M37)` had its header-anchored start dragged down to `M14`, under-reporting the invoice total — row-shifting now distinguishes copy semantics (cloning, `$` pinned) from insert semantics (trailing rows, only refs at/below the block move); (4) the stale `calcChain.xml` cache was shipped pointing at moved cells, the usual cause of Excel's "we found a problem with some content" prompt — it is now dropped with its content-type override and relationship. Also fixed token substitution (banner tokens live in `sharedStrings.xml`, not inline in the sheet) and band detection (read a non-existent `style.id`). Verified: `npm run build` (root) succeeds; `npm run test:xlsx` (9 tests) passes; both real customer files generate 25-row reports that load cleanly in `openpyxl` (an implementation independent of the one used to read them) with **zero style mismatches** across every generated row, correct de-shared formulas, dates round-tripping as real dates, and UTF-8 (CJK) text intact; `prisma db push` + seed run twice against a local Postgres confirm the schema applies cleanly and the module-key backfill is idempotent; full API round-trip (login → inspect real fixture → create template → preview → generate) tested against a local server, output re-parses cleanly with ExcelJS. Added a rate-category filter (`Trip.rate_category`, e.g. "Trip", "Monthly Round", "Extra Trip/Round Trip") to preview/generate — real customer workbooks split trip-rate vs monthly-rate business into separate sheets (an IMILE file has an "extra" sheet), so a saved template needs to pull only its own slice rather than every trip for the customer. Verified end-to-end against a local Postgres: saved two independently-named templates ("IMILE Trip Monthly", "IMILE Extra") under the same customer with no collision, and each template's preview/generate correctly returns only its own rate-category slice of trips. · Same day, in parallel (**Smart Report Builder bugfixes**: post-review pass on the report builder found and fixed 3 defects — (1) `frontend/web-dashboard/src/router.tsx` referenced `moduleKey="locations"`/`"rate-cards"` on `<RequireModule>`, which no longer typecheck since a prior commit removed those two keys from `MODULE_KEYS` (they became always-on core infra) — **this broke `npm run build`**; fixed by dropping the two `RequireModule` wrappers to match the "core, not toggleable" intent. (2) `reportEngine/derived.ts`'s `tripIncome()` read `t.invoices[0]` unconditionally, but `reportEngine/resolver.ts` only includes the `invoices` relation when a spec explicitly references an `invoices.*` field — since `trips.revenue` is the default Values field in the Advanced Builder, every Quick Report preset, both landing-page templates, and the Ask Mercon parser, this crashed the query endpoint on the feature's own golden path; fixed by making `invoices` optional (`t.invoices?.[0]`). (3) `resolver.ts`'s row/column group-key join used a literal NUL byte as separator, making the file register as binary to tooling; replaced with `'||'`. **Not fixed in this pass** (flagged, needs an explicit decision): `requireModuleEnabled` was added to 7 previously-ungated route files (`reports`, `documents`, `folders`, `invoices`, `expenses`, `maintenance`, `trash`) in the same commit as the report builder work; `Settings.enabledModules` defaults to `[]` at the DB level and is only backfilled to the full list on **row creation** (`seed.ts`, `update: {}`) — if production's `Settings` singleton row already exists with an empty `enabledModules`, this 403s all 7 modules' APIs the moment it deploys. Needs a production data check (or an explicit backfill) before/after this lands, independent of the report builder itself. Built a complete, testable ad-hoc reporting system with a real backend Prisma query engine and drag-and-drop pivot table builder. **Backend**: `SavedReport` & `ScheduledReport` Prisma models (`npx prisma db push --accept-data-loss`), `reportEngine/schema.ts` (10 modules, canonical columns, join graph), `reportEngine/derived.ts` (shared `tripIncome`/`isEarned`), `reportEngine/resolver.ts` (`runReportQuery` with 400 injection guards, 1-hop relation include, JS group/agg/filter, KPI computation), `reportBuilderController.ts` & `reportBuilderRoutes.ts` with Zod validation (`reportQuerySpecBody`, `createSavedReportBody`, etc.) mounted at `/report-builder/*`. **Frontend**: `@dnd-kit/core` installed, `reportBuilderService.ts`, `askMerconParser.ts` (rule-based NL→spec parser), `ReportBuilderLandingPage.tsx` (top bar layout matching MERCON guidelines with scope pill `[ 🏢 MERCON Logistics ↕ ]` & category badge, 3 entry cards, 4 pre-built templates, Saved Reports & Scheduled Deliveries tabs), `QuickReportPage.tsx` (3-step wizard), `AdvancedBuilderPage.tsx` (3-panel workspace: expandable data panel, droppable structure zones for Rows/Columns/Values/Filters, auto-connect badge, FilterBuilder, live preview, KPI cards, Recharts view switcher: Table/Bar/Line/Pie/Area), `SaveReportModal.tsx`, `ScheduleReportModal.tsx`, CSV/Excel/PDF export. Mounted at `/report-builder` and registered in `Sidebar.tsx` under COMPLIANCE. Fixed a real bug found in passing: `authService.ts`'s dev-only mock-login fallback had no `import.meta.env.DEV` guard, so a 5xx/network error in production silently granted a fake client-side Admin session. **Web dashboard**: de-branding codemod converted ~700 hardcoded `#E8450F`-family Tailwind arbitrary values (`bg-[#E8450F]` etc., 102 files) to the `bg-brand`/`text-brand`/... tokens already registered in `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block, plus the remaining on-screen literals (spinners, map pins, chart fills) to `var(--color-brand)`; new `useBranding`/`useApplyBranding` hooks fetch `/settings/public` and write it to the CSS vars + `document.title` at boot (login page included, pre-auth); new `BrandLogo` component replaces hardcoded `<img src="/mercon-logo.png">` in Sidebar/LoginPage with a Settings-driven `logoUrl` (falls back to the existing PNGs); `Sidebar.tsx` nav items gained an optional `moduleKey` filtered against `Settings.enabledModules`; new `RequireSuperAdmin`/`RequireModule` route guards (same pattern as existing `RequireRole`) applied to the 8 optional-module routes; `SettingsPage.tsx`'s previously-decorative "Company Details" tab is now a real superadmin-only branding/module-toggle form. Left deliberately out of scope: Excel export brand color (`utils/exportUtils.ts`/`exportInvoiceExcel.ts`) and the folder-color-picker palette (`CreateFolderModal.tsx`/`KpiCard.tsx`) — neither is app branding paint. **Mobile app** (`frontend/mobile-app/mercon-app`): `app.json` replaced with `app.config.ts` (`CLIENT_PROFILES` keyed by `APP_CLIENT` env, defaults to `mercon` so nothing changes for the existing build); `src/lib/api.ts` no longer falls back to `https://mercon.tech/api` on a misconfigured build — now reads the per-client `extra.apiUrl` from `app.config.ts` and throws if neither that nor `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` is set; SecureStore keys (`TOKEN_KEY`/`SESSION_KEY`) namespaced by client slug so two clients' apps installed on one device can't collide (`auth-context.tsx`'s separate hardcoded `SESSION_KEY` literal removed in favor of importing api.ts's); `tailwind.config.js` reads the same `APP_CLIENT` env at build time. **Deploy**: `docker-compose.yml` parameterized (`CLIENT_NAME`/`POSTGRES_DB`/port vars, all defaulting to today's Mercon values — verified with `docker compose config` that nothing changes for the existing deploy, and the `pgdata` volume was deliberately left unnamed rather than risk pointing at a different volume than Docker auto-generated for Mercon's live data); added `nginx/client.conf.template` for provisioning a new client's Nginx config without touching the live `nginx/mercon-api.conf`. **Explicitly not done in this pass** (flagged as follow-ups, not attempted blind): `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml`'s tag-based/per-client-runner rework (recommended in the conversation, but it controls the only live production deploy and can't be tested here — needs the owner's review before it changes `git push` behavior); MTL's actual domain features (crane/subcontracted-vehicle tracking, "immediate trip" flow) — deliberately not designed without a scoping conversation with MTL, per Rule 0. Verified: backend `tsc --noEmit` clean, frontend `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` clean, mobile `tsc --noEmit` clean, `docker compose config` resolves without error, seed re-run twice against local dev DB confirmed idempotent (no fake data, `isSuperAdmin` set once and not overwritten), full settings CRUD + superadmin-revocation-takes-effect-without-relogin tested end-to-end against a locally running API.) · Previously (**Expenses Module** (`/expenses`, new page): salaries, fuel, rent, and every other operating cost had nowhere to be recorded — MaintenanceRecord only covers vehicle service orders, and Invoice only covers customer billing, so there was no way to log money going out. **Database & Backend** (`schema.prisma`, `expenseController.ts`, `expenseRoutes.ts`, `trashController.ts`): added `Expense` model (free-text `category` e.g. Salary/Fuel/Rent, `status` Paid/Pending, optional `driverId`/`vehicleId` links, `amount`/`currency`/`expense_date`/`payee`/`payment_method`/`description`, standard ref_id + audit/soft-delete columns) with back-relations on `Driver`/`Vehicle`; registered `GET/POST /expenses`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /expenses/:id` gated to Admin/Operator via `authorizeRoles`; wired into the recycle bin (restore/hard-delete). **Shared Types** (`packages/shared-types`): added `EXPENSE_CATEGORIES`/`EXPENSE_PAYMENT_METHODS` constants (free-text columns, not Postgres enums, same pattern as `VEHICLE_TYPES`). **Frontend Web Dashboard**: `expenseService.ts`, `ExpenseListPage.tsx` (KPI cards for total/paid/pending/salary amounts, filterable/searchable `DataTable` ledger, CSV export), `ExpenseModal.tsx` (create/edit form with category, status, optional driver/vehicle link, amount, payment method, payee, notes); added to the sidebar FINANCE group and the router at `/expenses`. No seed data added (owner rule: never seed fake financial records). Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on both backend and frontend, `vite build` succeeded; local schema push to the dev Postgres could not be verified in this session because Docker/local Postgres was not running — run `npx prisma db push` (or redeploy) before relying on the `Expense` table.) · Previously (**Savable & Selectable Workshops and Work Done Service Details in Maintenance Module**: Operators can now save workshops and service details (e.g. "Tire Puncture Repair", "Oil & Filter Change", "Brake Pad Replacement") so they are selectable directly in **Add Maintenance Record** instead of typing from scratch every time. **Database & Backend** (`schema.prisma`, `seed.ts`, `maintenanceController.ts`, `maintenanceRoutes.ts`): Added `SavedWorkshop` and `SavedWorkDone` models with idempotent seeding for default workshops and common service details. Updated `getWorkshops` to merge saved entries with historical orders; added API routes for `POST /workshops`, `DELETE /workshops/:id`, `GET /work-items`, `POST /work-items`, `DELETE /work-items/:id`. **Frontend Web Dashboard** (`frontend/web-dashboard`): `WorkshopField.tsx` upgraded to a selectable dropdown showing saved workshops with an inline `+ Save New Workshop` popover. `WorkDoneSelect.tsx` added to render multi-item selectable service chips with an inline `+ Save New Service Item` dialog. `ManageWorkshopsModal.tsx` added to let operators manage saved workshops and service details anytime from the top bar of the Maintenance list page. Verified clean build: `tsc` and `vite build` succeeded with zero errors.) · Previously (**Monthly Trips by Company** (`/trips/monthly`, new page): monthly-contract work ("this company gets N trips this month; assign a driver and a truck per day") was invisible as a plan — the trip ledger is flat, paginated 20 at a time and sorted by status, so answering "who is covering ARKAN on the 14th" meant scrolling and regrouping in your head, and "is every day of this month covered" was unanswerable. **Backend** (`tripController.getMonthlyTripBoard`, `GET /trips/monthly`, registered before `/:id` so "monthly" isn't read as a trip id — no schema change): returns a whole month in one response, already grouped customer → day, with per-company driver/vehicle/category rosters and a month summary (trips, companies, distinct drivers, distinct vehicles, value, trips still missing a driver or truck, status breakdown). A trip's day is `planned_start` falling back to `createdAt` — the same rule `getTrips`' date filter already uses, so the ledger and the board can never disagree about which month a trip is in; a trip landed on its created date is flagged `date_is_inferred` and says so in the UI rather than pretending it was scheduled. Filtering by rate category / vehicle type matches **either** the trip's own copied column **or** its rate card's, because trips created before `Trip.vehicle_type`/`rate_category` existed only carry the tier on the card — matching only the trip column would have silently hidden them from a "Monthly Round" filter. Money shown is `billing_amount ?? trip_charges`, never invented: a trip with neither contributes 0 and renders as unpriced. Capped at 5000 trips with a `truncated` flag the page surfaces, so a runaway month is visible rather than silently short. **Frontend**: `MonthlyTripsPage.tsx` (month stepper + `This month` reset, filters for company/rate category/vehicle type/status/search, Excel export of the flattened month, honest empty states) and `components/trips/monthly/` — `MonthlyCompanyCard` is one card per company (name, trip count, an amber "N to assign" badge, total value) holding a vertical stack of that company's trips: each trip is its own compact card with a date/status row and a labeled 2-column field grid (driver, vehicle, route, amount, category), long driver names and routes truncated with a hover title instead of stretching the card. Clicking a trip opens `TripDetailDialog` on the board (full field breakdown + an "Open full trip" button to `/trips/:id`) rather than navigating away immediately. **Revised six times, same day, on owner feedback**: pass 1 built a page-level 6-tile KPI strip and, per company, a Calendar/Schedule toggle — replaced with a per-company flat table per the owner's "just a table, date/driver/vehicle" ask. Pass 2: the table's wide horizontal rows were still awkward for long names/routes, and the owner separately flagged the per-company colour system (`companyHue`, a company-name-hashed pick from 7 hues for the avatar chip and accent rail) as generic, "slop kinda UI" — replaced the table with the vertically stacked trip cards described above (limited-width truncation instead of unbounded flex growth) and removed `companyHue`/`COMPANY_HUES` entirely in favour of one consistent brand-orange avatar for every company. `daysInMonth`/`dayKey`/`isToday`/`STATUS_DOT` (calendar-only helpers) and the KPI tile component were deleted from pass 1's code, not left dead. Companies still sort busiest-first. Pass 3: pass 2's vertical field-grid trip cards (a date/status row plus a 2-column labeled grid) still produced far more scrolling than the data needed and were not what "vertically stacked" meant — the owner wanted the *company cards* stacked vertically down the page (already true) each holding one **tight table**, and a click on a row (not the whole card) opening the detail dialog. `MonthlyCompanyCard` now renders a compact `Table` again — tighter row padding than pass 1's table, driver/route/category cells capped with `max-w` + `truncate` + a hover title instead of letting a long name or route stretch the row — with `TripDetailDialog` reused unchanged from pass 2 on row click. Pass 4: the owner's "vertically stacked" turned out to mean company cards placed side by side (each column reading top-to-bottom is "vertical"), not the single full-width column pass 3 built — confirmed with `AskUserQuestion` before touching code, given three prior misreads of the same phrase. `MonthlyTripsPage`'s company list is now `grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2 gap-4 items-start` (two per row on desktop, one on mobile, `items-start` so a company with fewer trips doesn't stretch to match a taller neighbour) instead of a single vertical stack — cuts the up-down scrolling a full-width column forced once there were more than a couple of companies. Pass 5: trimmed the table itself from 7 columns to 4 — Date, Driver, Vehicle, Status — on the owner's ask not to clutter the row with lower-priority fields; Route, Category and Amount are still there, just moved into `TripDetailDialog` (already the one-click destination since pass 2) instead of the row. Pass 6 ("overall page UI/UX upgrade"): the page was three detached white boxes stacked with `gap-5` — a header strip, a search card, a filter card — which read as three unrelated things rather than one control surface for one list. They are now a single **command bar** card: row 1 is the icon badge + title + subtitle on the left and, on the right, a segmented `MonthStepper` (‹ August 2026 › with a formatted text label and a transparent native `input[type=month]` overlaid so clicking still opens the platform month picker, plus a "Today" segment that appears only off the current month), an icon-only refresh/export pair grouped in one bordered container, and the single primary `New Trip` button. Row 2 joins the search box and all four filter `Select`s into **one** segmented control — one border, internal hairline dividers (`divide-x`), borderless children — instead of five separately-bordered pills. Row 3 is a result-context line (`August 2026 · 8 companies · 124 trips · 6 need assignment`, the last in amber only when non-zero) paired with **removable filter chips** (each applied filter as a chip with its own ×, plus "Clear all"), replacing the previous bare "Clear 3" button that never said *what* was applied. `appliedFilters` is derived once so the chip row and the clear-all count cannot disagree. Company cards were retuned to match: header is now avatar + name + a phone/total meta line on the left with the trip count and unassigned badge right-aligned (previously all crammed inline after the name), table headers dropped to `h-8`/`text-[10px]`, status right-aligned, and the detail dialog restructured into a tinted "Scheduled" block + labelled `Detail` pairs on hairline-separated rows. Page padding `pb-6`→`pb-8`, section rhythm `gap-5`→`gap-4`. Rate category / vehicle type options come from `RATE_CATEGORIES`/`VEHICLE_TYPES` in `@mercon/shared-types`, not a local list. Sidebar entry added under OPERATIONS next to Trips (`/trips`'s active-state rule updated so it no longer also highlights for `/trips/monthly`). **Not exercised in a browser** — no local Postgres or Docker in this environment; verified by `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server and `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard. Also **repaired PROGRESS.md itself**: this file had committed merge-conflict markers (`=======` / `>>>>>>> Stashed changes`) around two rival "Last updated" lines since `3e2dcd1`; kept the newer 2026-08-12 chain and spliced the 2026-08-11 tonnage-tier entry — which existed only on the stashed line — back into it so no history was lost)) · Previously (**Added Vehicle Type / Rate Category filters to the Rate Cards ledger**: `RateCard.vehicle_type`/`rate_category` were already stored and imported per-tier, but the `/rate-cards` list page only let you filter by status/search — there was no way to narrow the ledger down to just "Monthly Round" or just "13.5M-20TON" rows even though those are exactly the dimensions that distinguish otherwise-identical lanes. Added `vehicle_type`/`rate_category` as real server-side query params on `GET /rate-cards` (`rateCardController.getRateCards`, additive `whereClause` filters, same pattern as the existing status filter), wired two new dropdowns (populated from the canonical `VEHICLE_TYPES`/`RATE_CATEGORIES` lists in `@mercon/shared-types`) into `RateCardListPage.tsx`'s ledger toolbar next to Status, and extended the active-filter banner / "Show All Rates" reset to cover all three filters together. No schema or architecture change — the fields already existed, this only exposes them as filters) · Previously (**Tonnage tier / trip-type ("6 ton", "10 ton", "one trip", "two-way/round trip") now actually drives rate-card matching, instead of being decorative labels**: owner reported these weren't "properly connected." Root cause: `RateCard.vehicle_type`/`rate_category` were free-text columns nothing read at lookup time — `findRateForLane` (the one shared rule used by `GET /rate-cards/lookup`, `createTrip`'s auto-fill, and invoicing's fallback) filtered only by customer+lane and broke ties with `orderBy: updatedAt desc`, so a lane priced at both "6 TON" and "10 TON" resolved to whichever card was touched most recently, ignoring tonnage/trip-type entirely; `Trip` itself had no field to record which tier was actually dispatched; and the manual create/edit rate-card forms (`RateCardFormDialog`, `CreateRateCardPage`, `EditRateCardPage`) never exposed these two fields at all — they were only ever populated via CSV bulk import. **Not converted to a hard Postgres enum** (deliberate, discussed with owner): live DB access wasn't available to confirm every existing row matches a known list, and `prisma db push --accept-data-loss` runs automatically on every deploy — a strict enum could silently drop an unlisted legacy value. Instead, `VEHICLE_TYPES`/`RATE_CATEGORIES` (`packages/shared-types/src/index.ts`) are the 10/9 real values found in the 652-row import workbook (`docs/templates/MERCON_RateCards_ReadyToImport_Quotations.xlsx`), validated at the application layer only (Zod in the backend, dropdowns in the UI); the DB columns stay nullable `String`. **Schema** (additive, non-breaking): `Trip.vehicle_type`/`Trip.rate_category` (nullable strings) — copied from whichever rate card was actually applied (or typed by hand for a one-off price), so the dispatcher's tier selection survives even if that rate card is later edited, and trip-level reporting on tier becomes possible. **Backend**: `findRateForLane` (`services/rateLookup.ts`) now accepts optional `vehicleType`/`rateCategory` — `undefined` means "any tier" (old behavior, for callers that don't know), `null` means "only tier-less cards", a string filters exactly; wired through `GET /rate-cards/lookup` (query params), `createTrip`'s fallback matching, and `tripLifecycle.completeTripAndInvoice`'s legacy-trip fallback (only constrained when the older trip actually recorded a tier, so it doesn't wrongly narrow pre-existing trips to tier-less-only). `createTripBody`/`rateCardController` (`createRateCard`/`updateRateCard`/`bulkImportRateCards`) now validate `vehicle_type`/`rate_category` against the shared list via new `vehicleTypeField`/`rateCategoryField` Zod schemas, rejecting unknown values row-by-row on import rather than silently storing typos. **Frontend**: dropdowns (backed by the same shared list) added to `RateCardFormDialog`, `CreateRateCardPage`, `EditRateCardPage` — previously read-only badges with no way to set them by hand. Trip creation (`CreateTripPage`, `CreateTripModal`/`TripStepRatesBilling`) gained a tier selector inside the existing "new lane, no rate yet" flow, so a fresh rate card (or one-off price) created mid-dispatch can be tagged with a tier instead of always saving `null`; the existing "N rates for this lane, pick one" card picker (already tier-aware via badges) is unchanged. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on `backend/api-server`, `tsc --noEmit` clean on `frontend/web-dashboard`. **Not exercised in a running browser or against a live DB this session** — no local Postgres connection was available; the enum-vs-validation decision and the workbook-derived value list are the two things to double check against the live database before/soon after this deploys.) · Previously (**Upgraded Top Customers on the Ilan Dashboard from a bare rank+bar list to real per-customer cards**: found that `reportsController.ts`'s `getRevenueReport` already computed the customer's `id` internally (via `customer.findMany({select:{id,name}})`) but never returned it — added `id` to the `top_customers` response (1-line, additive, verified only 2 other pages consume this endpoint and neither reads more than `name`/`value`, so no breaking change). That unlocked: rows now navigate to `/customers/:id` on click, show a real credit-exposure bar (`CreditExposureKpi` — previously built but unused anywhere — driven by `Customer.credit_limit` and that customer's real `Pending`/`Overdue` invoice total, reusing the same `invoicesRes` array already fetched for Actions Needed rather than firing new requests) omitted entirely when a customer has no credit limit set (never faked), and an "N active" trip chip from `customer.trips` when any are in an in-flight status. Avatars use the same initials-chip pattern as `UserChip.tsx`, colored from the same 6-hue palette introduced for Actions Needed's categories so the two flagship widgets read as one visual system. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` gained one new query (`Promise.all` over `customerService.getById` for the top 5 ids). `TopCustomersWidget.tsx` rewritten; `reportsService.ts` type updated. · Previously (**Gave each Actions Needed category its own color, not just its icon**: rows now carry a subtle full-row background wash and a left accent stripe in the category's color (amber/blue/rose/purple/indigo/teal), and the active filter chip tints to match instead of a plain white/gray surface. Locations moved off slate onto teal so all 6 categories read as distinct colors, not "5 colors + 1 gray."
