Severity pills (rose/amber/slate) and the brand-orange hero count are untouched — this only adds a second, category-level color dimension on top of the existing severity one. `components/dashboard/ActionsNeededWidget.tsx` only. · Previously (**Rebuilt Actions Needed into an "Action Center" on the Ilan (Admin) Dashboard**: was a 2-category (documents expiring, maintenance due) split-panel card; now a unified, urgency-sorted list across 6 real categories with filter chips (All / Documents / Maintenance / Delays / Invoices / Rate Cards / Locations), a brand-orange hero count + severity breakdown, and a "You're all caught up" empty state. Four new categories, each reusing an existing predicate/endpoint rather than inventing one: **delay stops needing a reason** (`reportsService.getDelayLog({ needs_reason: 'true' })` — server-computed, previously shown read-only only on the full Delay Report page), **overdue invoices** (same `due_date < now && status !== 'Paid'` check `InvoiceListPage.tsx` already uses), **rate cards not linked to a lane** and **locations missing an address** (same predicates `RateCardListPage.tsx`/`LocationListPage.tsx` already compute). Explicitly left out: unsettled post-trip charges (already its own widget/KPI card on this page — would've duplicated), driver risk-score/stuck-in-Draft/stuck-in-Maintenance signals (no existing threshold convention to anchor a number to), ICCES device staleness (`Vehicle.last_seen_at` is never read anywhere in the frontend today, unverified as populated), payment-approval-pending (no server-side filter, needs backend work), and snooze/dismiss (confirmed nowhere in the codebase — resolving means navigating to the real page, or for delays, a small inline action). That last case needed one: `logStopDelay` and the `DelayReason` types were fully built server-side but **never called from any frontend component** — the Delay Report page only showed `needs_reason` as a read-only badge. Added `components/trips/LogDelayReasonModal.tsx` (mirrors `PostTripSettlementModal`'s click-row→modal→submit pattern) so this is now a genuinely resolvable action instead of a dead end. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` gained 4 new queries (delay log, invoices, rate cards, locations) and the widget moved to its own full-width row (Top Customers also promoted to its own row) instead of squeezed into a shared half-width card. · Previously (**Gave the Ilan (Admin) Dashboard's Settlement Queue and Revenue KPI cards their own footer instead of a reskinned truck**: the animated route/truck footer (from `/vehicles`) fits Active Trips and Fleet Available — both literally about vehicles — but Settlement Queue and Revenue aren't vehicle concepts, so recoloring the same truck animation for them was decoration with no meaning. Settlement Queue now shows a real aging distribution (`KpiCard`'s native `progressSegments`) bucketing pending trips by days-since-completion (≤1d / 2-3d / 4d+, amber→rose), computed from `unsettledTrips` client-side. Revenue now shows a real 6-month sparkline (`KpiCard`'s native `chartData`) from `summary.monthly_revenue_chart`, replacing the truck footer — also less code, since both are `KpiCard` features already built for this rather than new custom SVG. Active Trips and Fleet Available keep the truck/route `KpiRouteFooter`. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` only. · Previously (**Redesigned the Admin Dashboard header row**: replaced the bare title-and-button strip with one matching the main `/` Dashboard's own header convention — a tinted icon badge, a 2xl title with an "Admin Only" pill (this page is unlisted from the sidebar, worth signposting), a subtitle, a live "Updated Xm ago" freshness label driven by `useIsFetching({ queryKey: ['admin-dashboard'] })`, and the same pill-shaped "Refresh All" button style as the main dashboard (was a mismatched `Btn` "secondary" pill before). The hand-rolled inline success banner (which pushed the KPI strip down whenever it appeared) was replaced with the main dashboard's fixed bottom-right slide-in toast instead, so header spacing stays stable regardless of toast state. `AdminDashboardPage.tsx` only — no other page touched. · Previously (**Moved the Admin Dashboard off the sidebar to a direct-link-only route** (`/admin-dashboard` → `/Ilan`), per owner request, and restyled its KPI strip to match `/vehicles`'s instrument-panel cards — uppercase title, count+unit-label value, `trend`/`trendValue` chip, a `kpi-icons.tsx` line icon (not lucide), and the same animated "route map" `customFooter` (grid + street-line network + dashed route + pulsing 3D truck), now extracted into a shared `components/ui/KpiRouteFooter.tsx` instead of hand-duplicated per card. Unlike the Vehicles page, these cards are informational only (no `isActive`/`onClick` filter toggle — nothing on this page to filter into). `Sidebar.tsx`'s Admin-only entry was removed (page keeps `RequireRole roles={['Admin']}`, just unlisted). · Previously (**Added the Admin Dashboard** (`/admin-dashboard`, Admin-only): a new, separate page — the existing `/` dashboard is untouched — built from the owner's prioritized widget list. **Active Trips** (`ActiveTripsWidget`) shows a live in-flight count/list via `tripService.getAll({ status: 'Dispatched,AtPickup,InTransit,AtDelivery' })`, deliberately not the "total trips" KPI already on the main dashboard. **Post-Trip Settlement Queue** (`LaborChargeQueueWidget`) consolidates the main dashboard's three scattered unsettled-trip UI patterns (banner + insights alert + ledger card) into one `DataTable` with an "Enter Labor Charges" action per row, still driving the existing `PostTripSettlementModal`/`tripService.getUnsettled`/`updateFinancials` — no lifecycle change: there is no "Delivered" status, `AtDelivery→Completed` still happens automatically from the driver's mobile "Complete Delivery" action, and `is_post_trip_settled` is still a separate flag, not a gate. **Top Customers** (`TopCustomersWidget`) reads `reportsService.getRevenueReport().top_customers`. **Actions Needed** (`ActionsNeededWidget`) merges expiring documents (`documentService.getAll({ expiring_within_days: 30 })`) and maintenance due soon (`maintenanceService.getAll` filtered/sorted by `next_service_due`) into one triage panel, reusing the previously-unused `ExpiryCountdownKpi`. A small KPI strip (fleet available/on-trip, revenue trend) reuses `KpiCard`/`CustomKpiWidgets`. Gated with `RequireRole roles={['Admin']}` + an Admin-only sidebar entry (`Sidebar.tsx`), same pattern as `/settings/users`. No backend or schema changes — every widget calls existing service functions. · Previously (**Removed the simulated "Driver live dispatch chat" widget from the web dashboard**: the WhatsApp-style chat panel on the Dashboard (Chats/Updates/Calls tabs, the in-memory driver message simulation, the "Send Mobile Dispatch Message" modal, and the "Contact Driver" button on Active Transit Status) was mocked UI with no backend — no real-time driver messaging exists in Mercon. Removed the whole component along with its dead state/handlers (messages, activeChatDriverName, chat send/reply simulation, drivers-for-messaging query) from `frontend/web-dashboard/src/pages/dashboard/DashboardPage.tsx`; the Labor Charges Ledger card now spans the full row in its place. No backend or schema changes. · Previously (**Search normalisation across every ledger and search bar**: searching only worked if you typed the value exactly the way it was stored — one whole-string `contains` per field. Typing a full name found nothing (the driver roster only searched `first_name`), word order mattered, and a plate or reference typed without its dash ("ksa1029", "drv0001") matched nothing. Search is now normalised in one place per side: `backend/api-server/src/utils/search.ts` (`buildSearchAnd`) builds the Prisma filter for the server-paginated ledgers (trips, drivers, vehicles, customers, invoices, maintenance, locations), and `lib/search.ts` in the web dashboard and the mobile app (`matchesSearch`) filters the client-side lists. The query is split into words; every word must match at least one field (AND across words, OR across fields), so word order stops mattering and "john riyadh" works. Case is ignored, and punctuation is bridged both ways — on the client by comparing separator-stripped text, on the server by also splitting a word at letter/digit boundaries and requiring each part in the same field. The client copies additionally fold accents and Arabic-Indic digits. Field coverage was filled in where it was plainly missing: drivers now search last name, ref id, phone, licence and assigned plate; customers search the contact phone; invoices search the trip ref; locations search the address. Verified against the local database: "drv0001" → DRV-1008's roster row, "najjar sami" → Sami Al-Najjar, "ksa1029" → KSA-1029, "trp 3009" → TRP-3009. Known gap: on server-filtered ledgers a *word* typed without its punctuation ("alnajjar" for "Al-Najjar") still misses, because Postgres can't strip the separator out of the stored value through Prisma; the client-side lists handle that case. No schema or API-shape changes.) · Previously (**Rate card import**: `RateCard` schema gained `vehicle_type`/`rate_category`/`via_location` — free text, not enums, since each customer's quotation names its own vehicle tiers ("5 TON", "DYNA 3 TON", "13.5M-20TON"). New `POST /rate-cards/import` (flat one-row-per-lane-×-vehicle-type sheet: Customer/Rate Category/Origin/Via/Destination/Vehicle Type/Price/Currency, mirrors `bulkImportCustomers`) wired to an "Import Excel" button on the Rate Cards list via the shared `ExcelImportDialog`; the customer must already exist (matched by name — a rate card import can't create one, `Customer.contact_phone` is required and isn't on this sheet); rows with `rate_category` "Surcharge" skip lane resolution entirely for flat fees (labour charge, per-stop fee) that aren't an origin→destination lane. Blank template at `frontend/web-dashboard/public/templates/MERCON_RateCards_Import_Template.xlsx`; the owner's actual 7-carrier quotation workbook (`docs/templates/COMPANY QUOTATION (1).xlsx` — JDL/IMILE/SHIPA/RTL/HORIZON/ARKAN BARWAN/AKS, each becoming a Customer) was converted to a ready-to-import flat file at `docs/templates/MERCON_RateCards_ReadyToImport_Quotations.xlsx` (652 rows) for the owner to sanity-check before it's actually imported — not yet imported. Schema change not yet pushed to any database (needs `prisma db push`, which happens automatically on next deploy).) · Previously (**Web dashboard build unblocked, list toolbars unified, mobile safe areas fixed**: the web dashboard build was failing on `main` — the deprecated `ScheduleMaintenanceModal` shim re-exported a name (`MaintenanceRecordModal`) that `MaintenanceRecordModal.tsx` never exports as a named export (it is the default export, plus a `ScheduleMaintenanceModal` alias), so `tsc -b` failed with TS2724 and the CI/CD deploy could not build the image; the shim now re-exports the default together with the existing `ScheduleMaintenanceModal` alias. Documents Center and the Invoices ledger each had their own toolbar row holding a search box and a status filter above the table, duplicating what `DataTable` already renders — both now pass their filter through `DataTable`'s `filterElement` and the standalone toolbars are gone (Documents keeps a grid-mode-only bar for bulk actions). On mobile, every screen switched from React Native's built-in `SafeAreaView` (which only insets on iOS) to the `react-native-safe-area-context` version, so Android screens no longer render under the status bar; the operator More screen shows the signed-in role instead of a hardcoded "Operator", and vehicle renewals whose vehicle was deleted are dropped instead of listing as "Unknown Vehicle". The desktop sidebar rail toggle also moved out of the header (where it shifted the header contents on every toggle) onto the sidebar's own right edge, vertically centred, so it stays put whether the rail is expanded or collapsed — superseding the "Menu" button described in the entry below. It is a 28px circle carrying a double chevron (a panel action, not a back arrow) with a 44px click target, and ⌘/Ctrl+B toggles it as well. No schema or API changes.) · Previously (**Maintenance details page rebuilt as a unified dashboard**: `/maintenance/:id` had a header, a separate lifecycle rail, a 4-tile metrics row, a work-report card, two side cards (Vehicle, Workshop) and a full-width Invoice card stacked below — six blocks pushing billing off-screen. Header, lifecycle rail and the four metrics are now one hero card with an integrated stat strip and a brand accent rail; the work report and billing/invoice sections are joined into one tabbed card ("Work Report" / "Billing & Invoice", with an invoice-count badge) so billing no longer needs a second scroll; Vehicle and Workshop are joined into one "Service Provider" card with divided sections. Borders/shadows moved to the same softer layered style used on the driver details page. No data or API changes.) · Previously (**Driver details page rebuilt as a modern dashboard**: `/drivers/:id` was four stacked blocks (header card, a 4-tile KPI row, scheduled-days card, trips table, documents card, plus two small right-column cards) that pushed the real content below the fold. Identity, actions and the four stats are now one hero card with an integrated stat strip and a brand accent rail; the icon-only actions (refresh/export/vault/delete) are a single segmented control. Scheduled days and dispatch history are joined into one tabbed "Trip Operations" card with counts on each tab and internal scroll instead of page scroll, and Credentials + Assigned Vehicle + Documents are joined into one right-hand "Driver Profile" card with divided sections. A license-expiry banner (expired / ≤ 30 days) appears only when it applies, the assigned plate is a chip that opens the vehicle, and borders/shadows moved to a softer layered style. No data or API changes.) · Previously (**Desktop sidebar collapses to an icon rail**: the dashboard sidebar can now be shrunk with a "Menu" button in the header, next to Back — it animates between 220px and a 76px icon-only rail (labels, group headings and the user block fade out; unread counts become a dot; every item keeps a tooltip), the main content reflows with it, and the choice is remembered in `localStorage` across pages and reloads. Rail mode is desktop-only (lg and up); the mobile off-canvas drawer is untouched.) · Previously (**Map tracking icons upgraded to transparent 3D assets**: replaced generic map markers with transparent 3D orange/green warehouses and 3D truck, removing boxy backgrounds and dark borders per user feedback) · Previously (**Workshops are remembered and offered for reuse**: every service-order form asked for the workshop name as a blank text box, so the same workshop got retyped — and mistyped into near-duplicates — on every order. `GET /api/maintenance/workshops` now returns the distinct workshops already used (case-insensitive, newest spelling and contact win, with an order count), derived from the service orders themselves — no Workshop table, no schema change. `components/fleet/WorkshopField.tsx` replaces the plain input everywhere a workshop is entered (Send to Workshop dialog, maintenance create/edit form, maintenance details edit, vehicle details Log Maintenance): it stays free text, but suggests saved workshops as you type and fills in the workshop's contact number when you pick one. A workshop typed once is offered from then on; the `workshops` query is invalidated after every service-order write.) · Previously (**"Mark Maintenance" now opens a real service order**: the Vehicles page wrote `Vehicle.status = 'Maintenance'` directly, so a vehicle could sit in the workshop with nothing on the Maintenance page to show for it — and no order to later mark completed. The workshop state is now owned by the service order end to end. `SendToWorkshopDialog` (`components/fleet/SendToWorkshopDialog.tsx`) collects workshop, type, odometer and reason and creates an `In_Progress` record; the API's `syncVehicleMaintenanceStatus` moves the vehicle to Maintenance off the back of it. The reverse is `POST /api/maintenance/vehicles/:vehicleId/return-to-service`, which closes every open order (Completed, `end_date` now) and releases the vehicle. Every entry point routes through those two: the row actions, the status-badge quick dropdown, the bulk actions, and the details-page banner and dropdown. On the vehicles list the workshop toggle is now a one-click button in the Actions column ("To workshop" / "Return to service") instead of being buried in the ⋮ menu, which also gained a "Service History" link — `/maintenance?vehicle=<plate>` pre-fills the maintenance search. Bulk "Mark Maintenance"/"Mark Available" became "Send to Workshop"/"Return to Service".) · Previously (**Vehicles stopped getting stuck in "Maintenance"**: a vehicle only left `Vehicle.status = 'Maintenance'` when a service order was *edited* from an open state to Completed/Cancelled. Every other path left it in the workshop forever — logging an already-completed order (the Log Maintenance modal on the vehicle details page defaults to Completed), deleting the open order, or moving an order to another vehicle — so the Vehicles list, its Maintenance Bay KPI card and the vehicle details badge all kept reading `Maintenance`. Vehicle status is now re-derived in one place, `utils/vehicleMaintenanceStatus.ts` → `syncVehicleMaintenanceStatus(vehicleId)`: any open order puts the vehicle in `Maintenance`, none releases it back to `Available` (a vehicle that is `OnTrip`/`Inactive` is never touched). It is called from create, update and delete in `maintenanceController`. The legacy `In Progress` (space) spelling now counts as open everywhere, including the list filter. Existing production data is repaired by an idempotent seed step, `releaseVehiclesStuckInMaintenance()`, which normalises `In Progress` → `In_Progress` and releases vehicles that have service history, nothing open, and have not been touched since their last order changed — that last condition is what stops it clobbering a deliberate "Mark Maintenance" from the Vehicles page. The maintenance list and details pages now also invalidate the vehicle caches so the change shows without a reload.) · Previously (**Create Trip step 3 re-laid out around the two stops**: the step stacked three full-width blocks — a pricing-lane box, then an "exact pickup" block and an "exact dropoff" block that each carried a permanently-open 220px Leaflet map plus four separate inputs (address search, stop name, address textarea, plus the hub combobox in a different section). The schedule and the price sat far below the fold and one stop's fields were split across two places. Step 3 is now a two-column grid of `TripStopCard` (`components/trips/TripStopCard.tsx`), one card per stop holding everything about it in the order a dispatcher thinks: pricing hub → exact point → planned time. `LocationPickerMap` gained a `compact` mode where the address search is the only field on screen; what the driver will receive (name, address, coordinates) is shown as a read-only summary line, and the map ("Adjust pin") and the manual name/address inputs ("Edit") fold open only when needed — the name inputs auto-open while the required name is still blank. Time presets trimmed from 9 chips to 7 shorter ones, the datetime picker's own preset popover already covers the rest. No behaviour, payload or validation change; the rate lookup, the pin-far-from-hub warning and the centroid-does-not-overwrite-a-searched-pin guard are unchanged. `LocationPickerMap`'s hardcoded light-mode hex colours were also swapped for theme tokens, which fixes its dark mode on the trip details stop editor too.) · Previously (**Vehicle P&L page rebuilt as a fleet profitability dashboard**: `/vehicles/financials` could only show one truck at a time — the owner could not see which vehicle earns the most or loses the most without clicking through every plate, and the page had no charts at all. The blocker was the API: `GET /vehicles/:id/financials` returns a single vehicle, so ranking the fleet was impossible client-side without one request per truck. **Backend** (`vehicleController.ts`, `vehicleRoutes.ts`, no schema change): new `GET /vehicles/financials/fleet` aggregates every non-deleted vehicle in three queries (vehicles, trips+invoices, maintenance) and returns one P&L row per vehicle — income, expenses, maintenance/renewal split, net profit, margin %, trip count — plus a `fleet_summary` (totals, profitable/loss-making/idle counts) and a fleet-wide `monthly` income/expense/profit series. The route is registered before `/:id` so the literal path is never captured as an id. The revenue rule that was inline in `getVehicleFinancials` is now the shared `tripIncome()` helper (`billing_amount` → invoice `total_amount` → `trip_charges`, counted only for `Completed`/`Invoiced`), so the fleet and per-vehicle reports can never drift. Both endpoints now accept optional `from`/`to` ISO bounds and both return a `monthly` series. **Frontend**: `VehicleFinancialsPage` is now two `Tabs` — *Fleet Comparison* (default at `/vehicles/financials`) and *Vehicle Detail* (default at `/vehicles/:id/financials`), with the tab switch rewriting the URL so both existing routes keep working. Fleet tab: 4 KPI cards, a **diverging horizontal bar chart** of net profit per vehicle (green/red, click a bar to drill in, capped at the 15 most significant), an expense-composition **donut** (maintenance vs renewals), a **stacked area** fleet income-vs-expense trend, *Most Profitable* / *Biggest Loss Makers* leaderboards with proportion bars, and a sortable full-fleet `DataTable` (sort by income/expenses/net/margin/trips; row click drills in). Vehicle tab: the plate `Select` became a searchable `Combobox` (a 100-item plain select was unusable), plus a `ComposedChart` of monthly income/expense bars with a profit line, a revenue-to-net breakdown bar, and the two itemized ledgers rebuilt as real `Table`s inside `ScrollArea` with totals footers. A `ToggleGroup` period preset (3M/6M/12M/All) drives the `from` bound on both tabs; Export Excel is tab-aware (fleet comparison sheet vs per-vehicle statement). New shadcn primitives scaffolded on the already-installed Base UI deps: `ui/scroll-area.tsx`, `ui/toggle-group.tsx`; `DataTable`'s `Column.header` widened from `string` to `React.ReactNode` so headers can carry sort buttons. Charts use the existing `ui/chart.tsx` + recharts — no new packages. **Not verified in a browser**: the local Postgres rejects the `mercon_user` credentials in `backend/api-server/.env` (`denied access on the database mercon_db.public`), so login fails locally; verification was limited to a clean `tsc` + `vite build` on both workspaces.) (**Maintenance module: sequential service-order numbers, date rules, uploadable workshop invoices, and a rebuilt details page**: owner reported four problems on `/maintenance` and `/maintenance/:id` — a new schedule accepted start/end dates years in the past, creating one returned a bare `Internal server error`, service orders had no human-readable number (the UI printed `#` + the first 8 chars of a UUID), and the details page was a tab bar (Work report / Invoice / Vehicle) with an oversized lifecycle stepper. **Schema** (`MaintenanceRecord.ref_id String? @unique`, hits production via `db push` on deploy): service orders are now numbered `MNT-001`, `MNT-002`, … through the existing `generateRefId` helper, which already gap-fills — so deleting `MNT-005` frees that number for the next order, exactly as asked. Because `ref_id` is unique across soft-deleted rows too, `deleteMaintenanceRecord` **clears** the ref_id on delete (otherwise the freed number would collide forever); `trashController`'s `MaintenanceRecord` restore branch therefore mints a fresh number when restoring an order whose ref_id was released. `prisma/seed.ts` gained an idempotent `backfillMaintenanceRefIds()` that numbers pre-existing records oldest-first and no-ops on every start after the first. **The 500 was never diagnosable from the response** — every `catch` in `maintenanceController.ts` swallowed the error and returned a generic `SERVER_ERROR`, so all five handlers now `logger.error({ err }, …)` and the real cause is visible in container logs. Three concrete 500 sources were fixed in the same pass: (1) `updateMaintenanceRecord` guarded its date conversion with `if (data.end_date)`, so an **empty string** — which is exactly what the edit dialog sends when a date is cleared — skipped the guard and was handed to Prisma as a `''` string for a `DateTime` column; a shared `toDate()` now maps `''`/`null`/`Invalid Date` to `null` for the nullable columns and drops the key entirely for the non-nullable `start_date`/`service_date`. (2) A `vehicle_id` that parsed as a UUID but pointed at a deleted/non-existent vehicle hit a Prisma FK violation; create now looks the vehicle up first and returns a 400 saying so. (3) `odometer_reading`/`cost` are now `Number.isFinite`-checked before reaching a `Float` column. **Date rules**, enforced in both places per CLAUDE.md: a new `validateDateWindow()` rejects a back-dated start or end on **create only** (an existing record legitimately holds old dates — editing an unrelated field must not fail because of them) and rejects `end_date < start_date` / `next_service_due < start_date` on both create and update; the web forms mirror it with `min` attributes (`min={TODAY_ISO}` on a new order's start date, `min={start_date}` on the end date) plus pre-submit checks, and changing the start date snaps a now-invalid end date forward rather than leaving it silently wrong. **`MaintenanceDetailsPage.tsx` rebuilt**: the three-tab switcher is gone — everything is on one page as sections. The 28px-circle stepper card was replaced by a 4px lifecycle rail with three milestone dots and labels under it, and the four 2xl KPI cards shrank to compact metric tiles. The work report is now laid out as an actual workshop document: a letterhead band (workshop name, type, plate, order no., service date), then "Scope of work performed" as accent-ruled body text, "Technician remarks", and a "Service record" field grid. Vehicle and Workshop are clean sidebar cards (the vehicle's plate gets a dedicated block); Invoice is a full-width section splitting billing details from **uploaded workshop invoices** — the new part: the invoice is issued by the workshop, so it's now uploadable via the existing `POST /documents` (`entity_type: 'MaintenanceRecord'`, `doc_type: 'Invoice'`), listed with open/remove actions and a confirm dialog. Added a Print action for the report. The list page gained an `Order #` column (and the grid cards a ref line), search now matches on `ref_id`, and CSV export carries `Order_No` instead of the raw UUID. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard. **Not exercised in a running browser** — Docker isn't running locally, so there is no local Postgres/API to log into; the create-path 500 in particular is fixed by reasoning plus the new logging, not by reproducing it.) Previously: **Fixed KPI counts changing when filtering on Vehicles, Drivers, and Invoices list pages**: clicking a KPI card or changing the status filter passed `status` to `getAll()`, which filtered the query response to only vehicles/drivers/invoices matching that status; because KPI values were computed from that filtered response, selecting any status caused all other KPI numbers to drop to 0 or collapse to the filtered count. Fixed by adding an un-filtered `kpiSummary` query (`['vehicles', 'kpi-summary']`, `['drivers', 'kpi-summary']`, `['invoices', 'kpi-summary']`) on `VehicleListPage.tsx`, `DriverListPage.tsx`, and `InvoiceListPage.tsx` — mirroring the pattern already used on `TripListPage.tsx` — so KPI instrument panel totals stay fixed and accurate across all cards while the list/grid/map filters independently. Verified: `tsc -b` and `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Fleet Excel bulk import shipped (Milestone 3.5)**: the templates, the field-mapping guide and the *export* side had existed for weeks; the import half did not — no `POST /drivers/import`, no `POST /vehicles/import`, no upload UI. Rows are parsed **in the browser** with ExcelJS (already a dependency for export) and posted as JSON, the same contract `/trips/bulk-import` already uses, so no file-upload handling or spreadsheet library was added server-side and the operator gets a real preview before anything is written. **Backend**: `bulkImportDrivers`/`bulkImportVehicles` + `bulkImportDriversBody`/`bulkImportVehiclesBody` (1000-row cap). Upserts on `phone_primary` for drivers (the only unique column — `license_number` is *not* unique in the schema) and `plate_number` for vehicles, so re-uploading a corrected file fixes records instead of duplicating them. **Status is deliberately never written**: a re-import must not flip a driver or truck that is out on a job back to Available. Both run **two passes** — the drivers sheet references vehicles by plate and the vehicles sheet references drivers by phone/name, so whichever is imported first has dangling references; pass 1 creates the records, pass 2 resolves the links. An unresolvable link is a per-row **warning** (record imported, assignment skipped) rather than an error, because 200 drivers should not fail over one mistyped plate. Driver phone matching is digits-only with suffix comparison (`+966 50 123 4567` / `+966501234567` / `0501234567` all match) and strips a trailing parenthetical, since the shipped template's own sample writes `+966 50 123 4567 (Ahmed)` — the file would otherwise have failed against itself. Ambiguous name matches are refused with an explanatory warning rather than guessing. **Frontend**: `importUtils.ts` (header matching that ignores case/`*`/bracketed units, accepts several spellings per field, locates the header row *by content* since the templates put banner rows above the table, and flattens Date/formula/rich-text cells — dates emitted from local parts, not `toISOString()`, which shifts UTC and can move a date back a day) plus a shared `ExcelImportDialog` (drag-drop, preview of row count / missing required columns / ignored headers / first 3 rows, then a created-updated-failed result panel splitting hard failures from warnings). Wired into the Drivers and Vehicles list pages. **Two real bugs found and fixed while verifying against the actual template files**: (1) the vehicles template **could not be opened by ExcelJS at all** — its sheet name `🚛 Vehicles & Trailers Directory` is 32 UTF-16 units (an emoji counts as two) against Excel's 31-char cap, so readers truncate it and then throw `worksheet name already exists`; shortened in `scripts/generate_excel_templates.py` and regenerated. (2) The generator wrote to `frontend/public/templates`, but this is a Vite app where only `frontend/web-dashboard/public` is served — the templates had been generated to a folder served to nobody, so every in-app template download link 404'd; paths are now repo-root-relative instead of hardcoded `d:\Mercon\...`. Verified end-to-end by parsing both shipped templates with the real matching rules: all driver fields map, all vehicle fields except the optional `trailer_type` (which has no column) map, header row located at row 7 in both, and the 50 blank spacer rows are correctly skipped leaving only the 3 sample rows. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Stop addresses are now visible and correctable on the web**: an operator could not see the address a driver had been given (`TripDetailsPage` rendered `location_name` only), and once a trip existed a wrong address was wrong forever — the only stop-level endpoint was `PATCH /trips/:id/stops/:stopId/delay`, and `EditTripPage` edited status/driver/vehicle and nothing else. New `PATCH /trips/:id/stops/:stopId` (`updateTripStop` + `updateTripStopBody`, every field optional since the usual case is fixing one address) updates the stop's label, full address, lane endpoint and pin. **Editable while the trip is in flight** — a wrong address is exactly what needs fixing mid-trip — and refused with 409 once the trip is Completed/Invoiced/Cancelled, because a finished trip is a record of what happened and its invoice is already priced off that lane. Timestamps are never touched: moving a pin does not un-arrive a driver, and the delay report reads arrival/departure, which stay as recorded. **Web**: `TripDetailsPage` now prints the stop's address under its name (falling back to the lane endpoint's address) and says *"No address — the driver only gets a map pin"* in amber when neither exists, rather than rendering a blank line; the lane endpoint's name is appended to the "Pickup Location"/"Drop-off Location" heading. New `StopAddressEditor` component drives a third section on `EditTripPage`, reusing `LocationCombobox` + the (already address-aware) `LocationPickerMap`; it saves per-stop on its own button rather than joining the manifest submit, since it hits a different endpoint and the common case is fixing one address without touching driver/vehicle/status. Closed trips render the stop read-only with an explicit reason. `TripStop` on the web service gained `location_address`/`locationId`/`location`. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Locations management page** (`/locations`, new): the `/locations` CRUD API shipped without any UI, so places could only ever be born inline from a dropdown — nobody could see the full list, fix a misspelling, fill in a missing address, or retire an entry typed by accident, and those mistakes are invisible at the point of creation. `getLocations` now returns `_count` for `originRateCards`/`destinationRateCards`/`tripStops` (all filtered to non-deleted), which is what makes the page answer its two real questions: which places are actually used, and which are incomplete or abandoned. New `LocationListPage` + `LocationFormDialog` (`components/locations/`): four derived KPIs (total / on a rate card / missing address / unused), a banner when any place has no address (a stop defaulting to one hands the driver coordinates and nothing to read), search, and All/Unused/Missing-address filters. Renaming is the fix for a typo — the API already cascades the new name into every rate card quoting it, and the dialog warns before you do it; it is **not** a merge, so if both spellings already exist the rename is refused as a duplicate and the rate cards must be re-pointed by hand (noted as a gap, not built). Delete surfaces the API's own 409 verbatim ("used by N rate cards") rather than a generic failure, and warns separately when trip stops reference the place — those trips keep their own address and coordinates, only the picker entry goes. Sidebar entry added under FINANCE next to Rate Cards, since a location exists to be one end of a priced lane. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard. **Not visually verified in a browser** — the local dev API refused connections, so login couldn't complete.) Previously: **Driver app now receives real from/to addresses; duplicate planned-start removed; Google Maps scoped**: owner reported that a dispatched trip reaching the driver's phone showed "no proper address", asked whether Google Maps is connected (**it is not — nothing in the codebase calls Google**), and flagged the same date being asked for twice on Create Trip. Root cause of the address gap was **not** the backend: `mobileTripController` has always returned full stop rows including `location_name`, but the mobile `TripStop` interface (`lib/trips.ts`) declared only `location_lat`/`location_lng`, so the app literally could not see the field and every driver screen rendered coordinates or nothing. **Schema** (additive, nullable): `TripStop.location_address` (full postal address of the exact yard — kept separate from `location_name`, which is the short label reports group by, because truncating an address into a label is lossy and irreversible) and `Location.address` (the verified address behind a lane endpoint, inherited by stops that default to that place). **Backend**: `mobileTripController` now has one shared `tripInclude` — its four endpoints each built their own before, some with `stops: true`, which returns stop columns but not the related `Location`, so *which endpoint the app happened to call* decided whether the driver saw a route or a pair of coordinates; stops now always carry `location {id,name,address}`. `createTripBody` accepts `location_address` (500-char cap, vs 120 for the name). **Driver app**: `TripStop` gained `location_name`/`location_address`/`location` plus `stopLabel()`/`stopAddress()` helpers (most-specific-first fallback: the stop's own address → its lane endpoint's address → null). Home screen's route timeline showed only "PICKUP"/"DELIVERY" and a time — it now leads with the place name and address; `LiveNavigationScreen`'s bottom card gained a "PICKING UP AT"/"DELIVERING TO" block (it was a blue line and a distance that never named the destination); `PickupVerificationScreen` gained a location card so the driver can confirm they're at the right yard before photographing cargo; `DeliveryVerificationScreen` shows "Delivered to" + address in the review step; the trips list gained a route line, since a customer name alone doesn't distinguish two runs for the same customer on the same day. **Web**: `LocationPickerMap` kept the full Nominatim `display_name` instead of discarding it after extracting the short label (this discard was the original reason no address ever reached a driver), and gained an editable address textarea so a hand-dropped pin can still be given one; Create Trip sends `location_address` per stop and pre-fills it from the chosen `Location`. **Duplicate date removed**: step 1's "Planned Trip Start (Optional)" block deleted entirely and `Trip.planned_start` now derives from the step 3 pickup arrival time — one moment, entered once, where previously the two were separate fields that could silently disagree. **Google Maps decision**: addresses only (Places API (New) + Geocoding) — map *display* stays on OpenStreetMap tiles because Google bills per map load and the app renders maps on tracking, trip details and several KPI cards. Not yet wired: needs an owner-provisioned key (account creation + billing is theirs to do); `docs/technical/GOOGLE_MAPS_SETUP.md` documents exactly which two APIs to enable, the referrer/API key restrictions, the budget alert, that Vite inlines the var at build time so it must also be a compose build arg, and the requirement that absence of the key falls back to Nominatim rather than breaking. **Known remaining risk, deliberately out of scope**: driver turn-by-turn still routes via `router.project-osrm.org`, a public demo server with no SLA. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server and mobile app, `tsc -b` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Rate cards rebuilt around priced lanes, and wired to trip creation and invoicing**: owner reported the rate card module and its connection to Create Trip were wrong end to end. Root cause: `RateCard.route_origin`/`route_destination` were free text, and `CreateTripPage` matched them with a substring test against the *stop's* name — "Khamis Sorting Center" never contains "Riyadh", so matching fell through to "any active card for this customer" and finally to `rateCards[0]`, meaning the price shown was frequently not the price for that route. `tripLifecycle.completeTripAndInvoice()` then re-guessed the rate with a *different* rule (and a hardcoded `1000.0` fallback), so the invoice could quote a price the dispatcher was never shown. **Schema** (`schema.prisma`, hits production via `db push` on deploy): new **`Location`** model (`name`, `slug` unique = lowercased/whitespace-collapsed name so "Madina"/"Madinah"/"riyadh " can't become separate lanes, optional default `lat`/`lng`, soft-delete); `RateCard` gained `originLocationId`/`destinationLocationId` FKs (`route_origin`/`route_destination` kept as a denormalised copy of the names so old rows, exports and invoices still render) plus an index on `[customerId, originLocationId, destinationLocationId]`; `Trip` gained `rateCardId`; `TripStop` gained `locationId` (the lane endpoint "Riyadh", distinct from `location_name`/lat/lng which are the exact yard the driver navigates to). **Pricing rule, now single-sourced** in `services/rateLookup.ts` (`findRateForLane`, deliberately a service not a controller — importing a controller from `tripLifecycle` would pull `index.ts` and the express app in at module load): a card with `customerId: null` is the **standard** price for a lane and applies to every customer; a card with a customer **overrides** it for that customer only; order is customer's card → standard card → nothing. `createTrip` and `completeTripAndInvoice` both call it, and the flat `1000.0` invoice fallback was removed (an invented price is indistinguishable from an agreed one; an unpriced trip now invoices 0, which is visibly wrong and gets corrected). **Backend**: new `locationController`/`locationRoutes` (`/locations`, Admin+Operator) with `resolveLocation()` that creates-or-revives by slug and is shared with the rate card controller; renaming a location cascades into every rate card's denormalised names; deleting one is refused (409) while a live rate card still prices it. `rateCardController` rewritten — customer is now optional, lanes resolve from ids *or* names (so the trip wizard can name a new place inline), duplicate lanes per scope are refused with a message naming the clashing card, plus new `GET /rate-cards/lookup` and `POST /rate-cards/:id/assign` (copy a lane's price onto N customers; customers who already price that lane come back in `skipped`, never silently overwritten). `createTripBody` accepts `rate_card_id` and `stops[].location_id`. **Frontend**: new `locationService`, `LocationCombobox` (searchable, creates a place inline from the typed text), `RateCardFormDialog` (one add/edit form shared by the rate cards list, the details page and the customer page, so the standard-vs-customer choice is asked identically everywhere) and `AssignRateCardDialog`. Create/Edit Rate Card pages rebuilt on lane pickers with an explicit "applies to" choice (the create form previously *required* a customer, so the standard-lane case the schema already supported was unreachable from the UI). **Create Trip step 3** now picks an Origin and Destination `Location` above each map (picking one moves the map pin to that place's default coordinates and pre-fills the stop name, both still editable), looks the rate up server-side as customer+lane change, labels which tier matched ("Customer rate"/"Standard rate"), and when the lane is unpriced offers to save the typed price as the standard rate / for this customer only / not at all — creating the card before the trip so `rateCardId` is recorded, and dispatching the trip anyway if that save fails. Customer details page now lists the customer's own rates *and* the standard lanes they inherit (suppressing any standard lane they've overridden), with add/edit/override inline. **Invented content removed** (same rule as the maintenance page): `RateCardDetailsPage`'s "~480 km", "Est. Transit: 6h 15m", "24.0 Metric Tons", "Heavy Flatbed / Reefer / Lowbed", detention/overweight/night-premium fee schedule and "VERIFIED" badge — none of those fields exist in the schema and they printed identically on every card — replaced by a real "other rates on this lane" comparison; the customer page's rate block was rendering `rc.origin_city`/`rc.destination_city`/`rc.asset_type`, none of which exist on a rate card, so it had been showing "undefined ➔ undefined / Equipment: Flatbed"; the list page's fabricated KPIs (`'Riyadh → Jeddah'` default lane, `tripCount={42}`, `+4.2%`, `percentage: 90`, `|| 85`) and the create form's fake name/city presets ("SABIC Dammam Route", "Saudi Aramco Jubail Express"). Rate cards whose endpoints predate lanes are flagged "Not linked" in the list, on the details page and in the editor, because they can never match a trip until re-pointed. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on api-server, `tsc -b` + `vite build` clean on web-dashboard.) Previously: **Maintenance details page (`/maintenance/:id`) rebuilt on real data only**: owner flagged the page for duplicated branding chrome and invented content. Removed from `frontend/web-dashboard/src/pages/maintenance/MaintenanceDetailsPage.tsx`: the "MERCON Logistics" chip and "Fleet Operations" badge in the header (the app shell already brands every page), the entire **"Itemized Checklist" tab** — its five rows ("Engine Oil & Filter Replacement", "Brake Pad & Rotor Inspection", …) were a hardcoded array rendered through `DataTable` as if they were recorded service items, when no such data exists in the `MaintenanceRecord` model — the "Compliance & Warranty → VALID/APPROVED" KPI (derived from nothing but `maintenance_type === 'Renewal'`), the "100% Tax Deductible" / "OPERATIONAL EXPENSE" / "Synced to Vehicle P&L" claims in the financial tab (no tax or ledger field exists anywhere in the schema), the "Commercial Heavy Commercial Workshop" descriptor printed under every workshop name, the fabricated `progressPercent` (100/50/15 by status) KPI, and the "Standard routine service and diagnostic inspection performed…" fallback that made a record with no `work_done` look like it had one — empty fields now render an em dash or an explicit empty state. Rebuilt UI on shadcn primitives: KPI row is now Cost / Odometer at service (with real km-driven-since delta vs `vehicle.current_odometer`) / Downtime (computed from `start_date`→`end_date`) / Next service due (the previously-unused `next_service_due` field), followed by a new Scheduled→In Progress→Completed lifecycle stepper driven by the record's actual status (with a distinct cancelled state) replacing the fake progress bar; tabs cut from four to three (Work report / Invoice / Vehicle), the raw `<textarea>` and hand-rolled skeleton markup swapped for the `Textarea` and `Skeleton` primitives, and status/type badge `switch` ladders collapsed into two lookup maps. The edit dialog gained the three fields it was silently dropping (`invoice_number`, `next_service_due`, `remarks`) and the invoice tab now surfaces `invoice_url` as a link when present. Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean on `frontend/web-dashboard`; not exercised in a running browser (no maintenance records exist locally — the seed deliberately creates no demo data). Earlier, 2026-08-08: **Driver now has a persistent "assigned vehicle"**: owner asked that picking a driver on the Create Trip wizard auto-fill that driver's usual vehicle instead of re-picking it every time. Added `Driver.assignedVehicleId` (optional, `@unique` so one vehicle maps to at most one driver at a time) as a named 1:1 relation to `Vehicle` in `schema.prisma` — a real schema change, hits production on next deploy. Backend: `createDriverBody`/`updateDriverBody` (`schemas/index.ts`) gained `assigned_vehicle_id` (uuid, nullable, optional); `driverController.ts`'s `getDrivers`/`getDriverById` now `include: { assignedVehicle: true }`, `createDriver` accepts it, and `updateDriver` (previously a blind `...req.body` spread) now explicitly maps the snake_case API field to the camelCase Prisma column since the two no longer match — both create/update paths surface a friendly "That vehicle is already assigned to another driver" message on the `P2002` unique-constraint hit. Frontend: `driverService.ts`'s `Driver`/`CreateDriverPayload` types gained `assignedVehicleId`/`assignedVehicle`/`assigned_vehicle_id`; `AddDriverPage.tsx` and `EditDriverPage.tsx` each gained an "Assigned vehicle" `Combobox` field (same pattern as the trip wizard的 vehicle picker); `CreateDriverModal.tsx` (the inline "Add driver" dialog used mid-trip-dispatch) gained the same field so a driver created on the fly can also get a default vehicle immediately. `CreateTripPage.tsx` gained a `useEffect` that sets `vehicleId` from the selected driver's `assignedVehicleId` when it's still in the `Available` vehicle list — pre-fills but doesn't lock the field, so the dispatcher can still override per trip. Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `backend/api-server` and `frontend/web-dashboard`; Prisma client TS types regenerated successfully, but the native query-engine binary swap (`prisma generate`) was blocked by a file lock from another session's running dev server — not restarted since it isn't this session's process to kill; not yet exercised in a running browser this session. Earlier the same day: **Trip Details styling pass to match `UI_GUIDELINES.md` (`frontend/web-dashboard/UI_GUIDELINES.md`)**: the owner said the page's cards/borders/shadows/curves "felt generic" — on inspection, all three previous Trip Details rewrites had used stock Tailwind `slate-*` grays (`text-slate-900/500/400`, `border-slate-200/100/300`) and `rounded-3xl`/`shadow-md` instead of this app's actual documented design system, which the rest of the app already follows: near-black/gray text tokens (`#111111` primary / `#6E6E80` secondary / `#9898A4` tertiary, not Tailwind slate), translucent-black borders (`border-black/[0.04]` to `/[0.08]`, never solid gray), `rounded-2xl` for cards (not `rounded-3xl`), `shadow-sm` only (never `shadow-md`/`lg`), and labels styled as `text-[10px] font-bold uppercase tracking-wider text-[#9898A4]`. Swept every card border, body/label text color, and label typography in `TripDetailsPage.tsx` to match (labels went from an inconsistent mix of `text-[11px] font-medium`/`text-[10px] font-semibold` to the one canonical label style used everywhere else in the app), downgraded the embedded `TripLiveMapCard`'s `shadow-md` to `shadow-sm` via its `className` override for this page, and swapped a couple of stray `bg-slate-100`/`bg-slate-50` fills for the app's `bg-black/[0.0x]` tint convention. Decorative multi-color icon chips (blue/violet/emerald for Customer/Driver/Vehicle) were left on Tailwind's palette since `Header.tsx`'s nav pills already establish that as an accepted pattern for categorical icon coloring, not covered by the gray/border rules. Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean; grepped for leftover `slate-` classes (one intentional exception remains — a neutral gray stop-marker circle for non-pickup/dropoff stop types, not a text/border violation). Still not exercised in a running browser this session. Earlier the same day: **Real bug found and fixed from a live screenshot review**: after the reference-screenshot rebuild deployed, the owner posted actual screenshots of `mercon.tech`'s Trip Details page for a real trip (Kozhikode → Kochi) — which surfaced that `TripLiveMapCard.tsx` always drew the hardcoded demo Riyadh↔Jeddah polyline and flew the camera to a demo truck's simulated position (`fleet.find(...) || fleet[0]` — the `|| fleet[0]` fallback ran for any trip that wasn't one of the ~4 canned demo trip refs), so the map showed an unrelated Saudi Arabia route/city label ("RIYADH") for a trip whose real pickup/dropoff pins were in India — a real bug pre-dating this rebuild, not introduced by it, just newly visible once someone looked at a non-demo trip. Fixed: `hasRealCoords` now gates behavior — when real pickup/dropoff coordinates are supplied and don't match a demo fleet entry, the polyline is a straight line between the real coordinates, no live-truck marker is fabricated (better to show nothing than a truck in the wrong country), and a new `FitBounds` helper (`map.fitBounds`) frames the real pickup/dropoff pins instead of flying to a fake position; marker popups also gained optional `pickupLabel`/`dropoffLabel` props so they read the trip's real `location_name` instead of the hardcoded "Riyadh Dry Port"/"Jeddah Islamic Port" strings. The canned demo trips (matched by `tripId`/`refId`) keep full simulated-telemetry behavior unchanged. Everything else from the screenshot review checked out correctly against the reference: status pill colors, the Stops table's per-row Pending/Upcoming logic, and the Activity panel's per-step icons (green check / blue-truck "departed" / orange active / gray pending) all matched real trip state exactly as designed. Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean; still not exercised in a running browser this session (same stuck preview-tab permission check), but this fix was validated by reading the owner's actual `mercon.tech` screenshots rather than guessing. Earlier the same day: **Trip Details page rebuilt a third time to match an exact reference screenshot**: the owner shared a screenshot of the actual app shell (same dark sidebar/branding) with a specific target content layout and said "exactly like this" — a persistent 2-column layout (not tabs replacing the whole page): left column stacks a trip-id/status/customer-driver-vehicle-ETA header card, a chrome-free live map, a horizontal pickup→drop-off route row, and a Stops/Documents/Audit-Trail underline-tab switcher (Stops now a real `Table` with numbered stop-type-colored circles, Scheduled/Actual Time columns, a status pill, and a click-to-expand row for delay-reason logging — replacing the previous vertical dashed timeline); right column is a persistent sidebar (not per-tab) with a Trip Progress card (big `%`, bar, route text, ETA, Distance/Elapsed/Stops/Avg-Speed stat rows, Call Driver/View Map buttons) and an Activity card (4-checkpoint live feed with per-step icons — green check, blue truck for "departed", orange pulse for the active step, gray outline for pending). `TripLiveMapCard.tsx` gained `showHeader`/`showTelemetryBar`/`className`/`mapHeightClassName` props (default true, so its only other nothing-else-uses-it call site here is unaffected in spirit) to render a plain chrome-free map per the reference, plus the Leaflet zoom control moved to bottom-right via `ZoomControl` (was default top-left) — the unused `ShieldCheck` import was also dropped in the same edit. All real-data-only and full-functionality constraints from the two passes before this hold: nothing in the reference's mock copy (fake trip IDs, "IN PROGRESS" wording, timestamps) was hardcoded, every field still reads from `Trip`/`TripStop`, and every action from the original page (assign/replace driver via `Popover`, delay logging, document upload, status/cancel modals, invoice link, audit trail) is still present, just laid out to match the screenshot. Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean on both changed files. **Still not verified in a running browser across all three passes of this rebuild** — the preview tab has been stuck behind an unresolved per-origin permission check for the entire session; this is the biggest open risk on this change (real spacing/proportions/wrapping have not been eyeballed against the reference, only inferred from source). Earlier the same day: **Trip Details page compressed into a single-screen operational cockpit**: after reviewing the premium rebuild below, the owner judged it had become a "feature inventory page" — 1,900–2,000px tall, admin-template smell (large stacked cards, equal visual weight for unrelated info, a KPI strip that doesn't drive decisions) — for a page dispatchers open dozens of times a day, where the real question is just "where's the trip, what's its status, who's on it, is anything blocked." Rebuilt `TripDetailsPage.tsx` around that: a condensed status strip (trip id/status, customer/driver/vehicle/ETA in one row, a mini "created → departed → in transit" activity line, progress % + bar, and Distance/Elapsed/Stops/Avg-speed as inline text stats — replacing the old separate hero card + KPI strip + 4-step horizontal timeline), the live map promoted to the dominant element beside a compact 4-checkpoint "Activity" panel (replacing the separate Route-details card), and an amber inline banner with inline driver/vehicle `Combobox` assignment that only appears when a Draft trip is actually blocked (replacing the always-visible bulky driver/vehicle assignment cards). Stops Log, Documents, and Invoice/Audit-Trail/Vehicle/Customer records moved behind shadcn `Tabs` (Overview/Stops/Documents/Audit) so only the active tab's content renders — no functionality was cut, everything from the previous rebuild (assign/replace driver via a `Popover`, delay-reason logging, document upload, status/cancel modals, invoice link, audit trail) is still reachable, just relocated out of the default scroll path. Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean; not yet checked in a running browser — the preview tab has been stuck behind an unresolved per-origin permission check across this session, so no live screenshot or click-through has happened for either this pass or the premium-design pass before it. Earlier the same day: **Trip Details page rebuilt to match a premium enterprise fleet-ops reference design**: owner supplied a detailed reference spec (breadcrumb + Edit/Print/More-Actions bar, a single hero `Card` with trip ID/status/customer/driver/vehicle/schedule grid plus an orange "Trip Progress" panel, a Route-timeline + live-map split, a 6-column KPI strip, and a 4-step Live Timeline) built on `frontend/web-dashboard/src/pages/trips/TripDetailsPage.tsx` with shadcn `Card`/`Badge`/`Progress`/`DropdownMenu`/`Tooltip`/`Separator`. The reference itself used fabricated sample data and silently dropped real functionality, so before building, the owner confirmed two constraints: (1) every value is sourced from the real `Trip`/`TripStop` model or computed from it, nothing invented — "Average Speed" is `planned_distance ÷ elapsed hours`, "Billing Amount" replaces the reference's fictional "Fuel Consumed" tile (`invoice.total_amount` or `trip.billing_amount`), and the `%` progress reuses the existing `STAGE_ORDER` stage-index calc; (2) all prior interactive functionality was kept and just reskinned, not dropped — driver/vehicle assign & replace, per-stop delay-reason logging, document upload, status-transition/cancel confirm modals, invoice link, and audit trail are still present (an Assignment/Invoice/Audit-Trail row + the detailed Trip Stops Log + Documents cards sit below the new hero/route/KPI/timeline sections). Also found and fixed a **latent bug** while rebuilding: `DashboardLayout` accepts `pageTitle`/`breadcrumb`/`actions` props but never renders them (a post-refactor pattern change, `git log` shows `a2b4325` moved page-level headers into each page's own JSX) — so the old page's header action buttons never actually appeared on screen. The new page renders its own breadcrumb + action row directly in `children`, matching the pattern `TripListPage.tsx` already uses. Verified: `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` clean; not yet checked in a running browser (owner interrupted the in-browser verification step to request commit+push instead — still needs a real click-through). Earlier the same day: **Trip Details page visual pass — real hierarchy, not just less gray**: owner feedback after the gray-fill cleanup was that the page was now "very plain," not "well designed" — confirmed no other page in the app is the bar to copy, so this raises the bar directly on `TripDetailsPage.tsx` along four axes the owner picked: stronger orange accent, typography hierarchy, visual progress indicators, tighter density. Route Overview header: ref ID bumped to `text-lg font-extrabold` behind an orange-tinted icon chip (`bg-[#E8450F]/10`), with a new single-bar **trip progress indicator** underneath (stage index over `STAGE_ORDER` = Draft→...→Invoiced, flat orange fill on a `bg-black/[0.06]` track, no gradient/glow/animation — deliberately not the removed multi-pill rail). The pickup/dropoff route line now fills green from 0→50→100% as `pickup.actual_arrival`/`dropoff.actual_arrival` land, instead of a static gray line; pickup/destination place names bumped to `text-base font-extrabold`. Distance stat chip gained a `border-l-2 border-l-[#E8450F]` accent edge and bigger number. Stops Log: dots are now 3-state (green = arrived, **orange = the next stop still pending**, gray outline = future) instead of binary green/gray, stop titles bumped to `text-[13px] font-extrabold`. Every right-column card header (Customer, Driver, Vehicle, Invoice, Audit Trail, Documents) gained the same `w-6 h-6 rounded-md bg-[#E8450F]/10` icon-chip treatment used elsewhere in the app (previously plain uncolored icons), and each card's primary value (customer/driver name, plate, invoice ref) bumped from `text-sm font-bold` to `text-base font-extrabold` for contrast against the `text-[#9898A4]` labels. Card gaps/padding were left alone (the shared `Card` component is used by every other page — tightening it here would leak into the whole app); density instead comes from the tighter `Separator` margins (`my-4`→`my-3.5`) already in place. Verified: `tsc -b` and `vite build` clean; not yet checked in a running browser. Earlier the same day: after the rebuild below, the owner reported the page still "looked the same" and flagged three concrete issues, all fixed. (1) **Real bug found and fixed**: some trips showed raw lat/lng instead of a place name for pickup/dropoff — traced to `frontend/mobile-app/mercon-app/src/screens/operator/CreateTripScreen.tsx`, where `isValid` required trip coordinates but never required `pickupName`/`dropoffName` (unlike the web wizard, which does), so an operator could submit a trip with no location name at all; `isValid` now also requires both names, and their `Input` labels are marked `*` required. The web `createTrip` endpoint and `bulkImportTrips` were confirmed as not the cause (bulk-import creates no stops at all — renders as "No pickup/dropoff stop on manifest", not coordinates). (2) The "Carrier" stat tile (always reading `carrier_name` / defaulting to "MERCON LOGISTICS") was removed from the Route Overview card — not needed per owner; Distance is now the only stat shown. (3) Gray/tinted fills that read as "AI slop" were replaced with the plain white-card + hairline-border look already established elsewhere (`border-black/[0.06]`/`border-black/[0.08]`, no fill): the Distance tile's `bg-gray-50`, the stops-timeline's `border-gray-100`/`bg-gray-300` dot, and the Documents row's `bg-gray-50/60` were all removed. The GPS-tracker "No tracker" `Badge`'s `bg-gray-50` was deliberately left as-is — semantic badge tints (vs. whole-surface fills) are the one place `UI_GUIDELINES.md` explicitly allows tinted backgrounds. Driver/vehicle assignment display was confirmed already present and correct (owner initially thought it was missing, then retracted). Verified: `tsc -b`/`vite build` clean on web dashboard, `tsc --noEmit` clean on mobile; not yet verified in a running app. Earlier the same day: **This is the single source of truth for "where is the project."*4: Last updated: **2026-08-08** (**Excel/PDF report exports rebuilt on real generator libraries**: the shared `frontend/web-dashboard/src/utils/exportUtils.ts` previously faked both formats — "Excel" was an HTML `<table>` saved with a `.xls` extension (`application/vnd.ms-excel` MIME + MSO XML comments), and "PDF" just opened a new window and called `window.print()` for the user to manually Save-as-PDF. Replaced with `exceljs` (real `.xlsx`, added as a new dependency) and `jspdf`+`jspdf-autotable` (real generated `.pdf`, downloaded directly, no print dialog). Two shared generators added: `exportExcelTable`/`exportPDFTable` (explicit title+headers+rows, used by pages that build a custom column set) and `exportExcel`/`exportPDF` (derives headers from a plain object array, mirroring `downloadCSV`'s existing header/value logic). Both classify each cell (currency/number/date/status-good/status-bad/text) from the header text and value shape to apply brand-colored (`#E8450F`) header bands, zebra striping, bold double-bordered totals rows, right-aligned `SAR #,##0.00` currency formatting, green/red status pill fills, frozen header + autofilter (Excel), and a branded footer with page numbers (PDF). Rewired every existing Excel/PDF export call site: `VehicleListPage.tsx`, `DriverListPage.tsx`, `CustomerDetailsPage.tsx` (customer trip ledger), `CustomReportPage.tsx` (custom trip ledger) for Excel; `TripListPage.tsx` (both the filtered-export dialog and the row-selection bulk action) for PDF — all now produce `.xlsx`/`.pdf` filenames instead of `.xls`. CSV exports were left as-is (already a real, correctly-formatted CSV). Verified: `tsc -b` clean on `web-dashboard`; a Node smoke test (stubbed DOM globals) drove `exportExcelTable` directly and the resulting `.xlsx` was unzipped and confirmed to be a valid OOXML package with real `xl/styles.xml` fills/fonts/borders, `autoFilter`, and merged title/subtitle cells — full in-browser click-through wasn't done this session (local API dev server's `nodemon`→`ts-node` spawn wasn't resolving `ts-node` from `PATH` in the preview harness, a pre-existing local-env issue unrelated to this change; `jsPDF`'s Node smoke test hit an unrelated Node/browser package-export-condition mismatch that doesn't affect the real Vite/browser bundle actually used at runtime). CSV import mapping/template, `exportUtils.ts`, `tripService.ts`/`vehicleService.ts` types, `CreateTripPage.tsx`'s hardcoded default), and the mobile app (`CreateTripScreen.tsx`'s required "Cargo" text input + validation — the only real cargo-entry UI anywhere in the app — plus every display/type site: driver Home/Trips/TripCompleted/Pickup+DeliveryVerification screens, operator TripList/TripDetails screens, `lib/trips.ts`/`lib/operator.ts`, `features/drivers` and `features/dashboard` types/services; `dashboardService.ts`'s Active-Vehicles-carousel `originLabel` — previously mis-sourced from `cargo_type` — now uses the trip的 `ref_id` instead). `TripCardSwiper.tsx`'s `cargoType` was deliberately left alone — it's unrelated hardcoded demo telemetry, never wired to real trips. Docs (`MOBILE_API_SPEC.md`, `final_database_schema.md`) updated to match; `api-contracts.md`'s `cargo_type` reference is a separate Rate Card field, untouched. Separately, **`TripDetailsPage.tsx` was rebuilt from scratch**, not edited in place: while this work was in progress, a separate commit on `main` (`e13cbab`, "revert(trips): remove Trip Details page to rebuild from scratch") deleted the old page entirely — along with the `/trips/:id` route, the `UserChip` component, and simplified `TripLiveMapCard`'s props — because the owner judged the old page's design bad, not just cluttered ("I deleted the previous page because it was shit... if you are creating back in the same design and same things, then that's a problem"). The rebuild keeps the same functional coverage (route overview, live map, stops log with delay logging, documents, customer/driver/vehicle cards with assign/replace, invoice, audit trail) but: the dark `#1C1C2E` "instrument panel" hero card is now a plain white `Card` matching the rest of the app (Customer/Dashboard pages already moved to this style); the cargo tile, the 6-step Draft→...→Completed status pill rail, and both "Edit Manifest" entry points (header button + "Add stops in Edit Manifest" link) are gone; and the **Financials/Settlement and Extra Driver Payment cards were cut entirely** per owner request (not just restyled) — their modals (`PostTripSettlementModal`, the cash-payout `ConfirmModal`), state, and mutations were removed from the page. `router.tsx`'s `/trips/:id` route and `UserChip.tsx` were restored (both needed by the rebuilt page); `tripService.ts` regained the `replaceDriver` call and the `Trip`/`TripStop` fields (`created_by`, `updated_by`, `updatedAt`, `vehicle.capacity_kg`, `vehicle.icces_device_id`, `stop.delay_logged_by`) that the deletion commit had trimmed along with the old page, since the rebuilt page still uses them for the audit trail, GPS-tracker badge, and delay "logged by" chip; the `TripLiveMapCard` call site was updated to that commit's simplified prop interface (just `tripId`/`refId`/pickup+dropoff lat-lng, no more `status`/`vehiclePlate`/`hasTracker`/`lastLat`/`lastLng`/`pickupName`/`dropoffName`). `CustomerDetailsPage.tsx` and `DashboardPage.tsx` had their own independent conflicting redesigns from the same period (both had already moved their trip tables onto the `DataTable` component) — those were kept as-is, just with their `Cargo Type` columns dropped. Pickup/dropoff address display needed no change: `TripStop.location_name` is captured via the Create Trip form's Nominatim address search and was already preferred over raw lat/lng, with coordinates only a fallback for stops never given a name (e.g. bulk-imported trips). Earlier the same day: **Trips KPI cards redesigned as clean stat tiles**: `KpiCard.tsx` rewritten — the sharp `rounded-none` "instrument panel" card (corner ticks, hairline accent rule, mono fonts, and a different gadget crammed into each card: semi-circle arc gauge, animated fake-74% pulse bar, donut ring, mini stage boxes) was replaced with one quiet tile that follows `UI_GUIDELINES.md`: `rounded-2xl`, `border-black/[0.06]` + `shadow-sm`, 10px uppercase muted label, 28px bold sans value, tinted rounded-xl icon chip, optional trend pill, and a single restrained footer visual per data shape (slim segmented distribution bar + dot legend, thin single-hue completion meter, live-pulse text row, inline stage counts, or a sparkline — the always-on fake `defaultChartData` sparkline was removed; sparkline now renders only when real `chartData` is passed). All existing props kept so the ~15 other pages using `KpiCard` compile unchanged; the `SemiCircleArcGauge` component was deleted (the `semiCircleGauge` prop now renders as the segmented bar). On the Trips page itself: meaningless decorative trend chips ("↑ All Operations", "→ Scheduled") removed, and TOTAL TRIPS' hardcoded fake sparkline data replaced with the real Completed/In-Transit/Queue status breakdown. `tsc --noEmit` clean; not verified in-browser this session. Earlier that day: **Drivers list actions reworked**: replaced the `Actions` dropdown trigger on the Drivers list table with inline action icon buttons (View, Quick Status, Docs, Edit, Delete) matching the style and layout pattern used in the Trips list table. Earlier that day: **Trips page: Export reworked as a dropdown menu**: replaced the modal-based export flow — which showed generic "All Drivers"/"All Vehicles" `Select`s instead of letting the owner actually pick one, and read as visually inconsistent with the rest of the app per owner feedback — with a `DropdownMenu` matching the pattern already used on Drivers/Invoices/Rate Cards list pages: a CSV/PDF format toggle at the top (emerald `FileSpreadsheet` / rose `FileText` icons), a quick "All Trips" item, "By Status" items (Completed/Delivered, In Transit, Not Completed), and "A Specific Driver"/"A Specific Vehicle" submenus that list actual driver names and plate numbers rather than a scroll-to-find select. Only date-range picking stayed in a dialog (now much smaller — just status + from/to date + the same format toggle), since a date range doesn't fit a menu item. `handleExport` was generalized into a parameterized `runExport(format, opts)` so both the dropdown items and the date-range dialog share one code path. Earlier the same day: **Trips page: CSV import added**: new "Import" button opens a dialog that bulk-creates `Draft`/`Dispatched` trips from an uploaded CSV — customer matched by name, driver/vehicle optionally matched by name/plate (each row independent; a bad row is reported with a reason, not fatal to the batch). Backend: `POST /trips/bulk-import` (`bulkImportTrips` in `tripController.ts`, `bulkImportTripsBody` zod schema, capped at 500 rows/request) reuses the same claim-driver/claim-vehicle transaction logic as `createTrip` but skips the stops requirement — imported trips have no route stops until added later through the normal trip edit UI, since a CSV can't reasonably carry map coordinates. Frontend: `parseCSVFile` added to `exportUtils.ts` (quote-aware CSV parser, header normalization), a downloadable template button, and a results view (imported/failed counts + per-row error list). Also **Export CSV/PDF buttons recolored** per owner request — green (emerald) for CSV, red (rose) for PDF, applied consistently to the Export dialog buttons and the row-selection bulk-action buttons (`DataTable`'s `BulkAction` gained an optional `className` passthrough to support it). Typechecks clean on both workspaces; not verified in-browser (dashboard requires auth, preview pane wasn't rendering this session). Earlier the same day: **Trips page: interactive KPIs + unified export + "New Trip" label**: the "New Trip Draft" button on `/trips` is now just "New Trip" — trip creation was never a separate draft-then-publish flow, `Draft` is simply the `TripStatus` a trip starts in when it's created without both a driver and vehicle assigned (scheduling via `planned_start`/`planned_end`/stop `planned_arrival` was already independent of that status and untouched here). The four KPI cards (`KpiCard.tsx` gained `isActive`/`onStageClick`/`onHealthClick`) are now clickable to filter the trip list by status/route-health/pipeline-stage, with an active ring style; KPI counts were also fixed to compute from the full unfiltered trip set (`allTripsRes`, a new unpaginated query) instead of just the current page. The separate Export CSV/PDF buttons were replaced with one Export dialog (status group, driver, vehicle, date range), and `GET /trips` (`tripController.ts`) plus `tripService.ts` gained a `vehicle_id` filter to support it. Not verified in-browser (dashboard requires auth; another session had the dev server on this port). Earlier the same day: **site-wide `--font-mono` set to JetBrains Mono**: `frontend/web-dashboard` added `@fontsource-variable/jetbrains-mono` (self-hosted, matching the existing `@fontsource-variable/geist`/`inter` pattern) and declared `--font-mono` in `index.css`'s `@theme inline` block, which previously had no override — Tailwind's `font-mono` utility (used across 41 files for plate numbers, ref IDs, timestamps, currency) was falling back to the default OS-monospace stack. `--font-sans` (Geist-based) and the separate "Geist Mono Numbers" digit-only `@font-face` hack were left untouched — out of scope for this change. Verified: `font-mono` utility now resolves to `'JetBrains Mono Variable'` via computed styles, the woff2 loads with a 200 from `node_modules` locally (self-hosted, no CDN), and both `tsc --noEmit` and a full `vite build` pass.
